# Soul Dust Revival

Soul Dust Revival is built on **study-platform-scaffold**: a Next.js app combining a commerce storefront with a gated, interactive study app (studies, lessons, books, blog, leader resources, social scheduling, admin tools).

## Build foundation: study-platform-scaffold

- **Scaffold location:** `/Users/matthewbrown/study-platform-scaffold/` (its own git repo)
- **Scaffold commit in use:** `1e497e7` (copied into this project 2026-11-02; scaffold also had an untracked `design-system/` directory at copy time, included)
- **Where the build happens:** here, in `/Users/matthewbrown/Soul Dust Revival/`. The scaffold was copied in (excluding `.git`, `node_modules`, `.env*`) and this folder now has its own git history, per the recommendation below.

### Read these first (they live in the scaffold)
Before building anything, read the scaffold docs relevant to the task and follow them:

| File | Covers |
|---|---|
| `README.md` | Architecture, stack, key files, setup, roles, admin features |
| `BUILD_TASKS.md` | Living checklist of setup, deployment, and QA work. Keep it updated as work completes |
| `SECURITY.md` | What is enforced and where (Zod, entitlements, webhook idempotency, XSS, uploads) |
| `AUDIT.md` | Past bugs found and how `npm run verify` works |
| `ACCESSIBILITY.md` | WCAG 2.1 AA implementation and manual checks still needed |
| `RESPONSIVE.md` | Mobile-first breakpoints, touch targets, browser support |
| `PRIVACY.md` | Personal data collected and compliance gaps |
| `SOCIAL.md` | Social scheduling, platform registrations, cron worker |

### Stack
Next.js 16 (App Router, React 19, strict TypeScript) · Cloudflare Workers via the OpenNext adapter (`@opennextjs/cloudflare`) · Supabase Auth (magic link) + Postgres via Drizzle ORM · Stripe (webhook-driven entitlements) · Cloudflare Stream and R2 · Resend · Zod · Tiptap · DOMPurify · Tailwind.

### Commands
```
npm run dev          # local dev server
npm run typecheck    # tsc --noEmit
npm run lint         # eslint
npm run build        # next build
npm run test         # vitest — needs a running local Postgres (see BUILD_TASKS.md)
npm run verify       # typecheck + lint + build — must pass clean
npm run preview      # build + run in the real Workers runtime locally (wrangler)
npm run deploy       # ship the pushed commit: staging, checks, then production (scripts/deploy/deploy.ts)
npm run deploy -- staging --no-promote   # build, upload and check one site without switching traffic
npm run db:generate && npm run db:migrate
npm run db:seed
```
`npm test` isn't part of `npm run verify` — it needs a real database connection,
which isn't guaranteed in every environment that runs `verify` (e.g. CI without a
Postgres service configured). Run it explicitly, and always before calling checkout/
webhook/entitlement changes done.

### Scaffold rules
- **Reuse before writing.** Use the scaffold's existing modules and patterns, especially these single sources of truth:
  - `lib/entitlements/check.ts` for every content access check
  - `lib/admin/guard.ts` (`requireAdmin`, `requireLeaderOrAdmin`, `requireRole`, `getRoleForSession`) for every role check
  - `lib/validation/schemas.ts` for every API input
  - `lib/db/schema.ts` for the data model
  - `components/marketing/SiteHeaderShell.tsx` for every top-level nav bar's chrome (sizing, color, logo/wordmark lockup) — any new nav bar (a new admin section, a leader-area header, anything else) renders through this shell rather than reimplementing its own `<header>`/sizing/colors, so nav bars can't drift apart the way the admin nav bar once did from the public one. The public header (`SiteHeader.tsx` + `SiteNav.tsx`) and the admin header (`app/admin/layout.tsx` + `AdminNav.tsx`) are the two reference implementations.
- **Standard page width: `max-w-5xl mx-auto px-4`.** Every content page's outermost container uses this — homepage sections, books/studies listings and detail, blog listing and post, leader resources listing and detail, account, privacy, and study lessons (lessons include their blocks: text, images, video, callouts). A page-internal element can still cap its own width narrower for readability or visual containment (e.g. a `prose` block defaults to Tailwind Typography's own ~65ch measure when you don't add `max-w-none`; a callout card can cap itself) without changing the page's own outer container. **Exceptions, by explicit decision:** short transactional flows — checkout, login/signup, and the order-confirmation screen — stay at their own narrower width since they're forms/confirmations, not content. The admin area (`app/admin/layout.tsx`) used to be a third exception at its own `max-w-4xl`, narrower than the public site; as of 2026-11-05 its content column was widened to match the standard `max-w-5xl` (its nav bar already matched — see the shell note above), so it's no longer an exception at all.
- **Never bypass those.** Do not query `users.role` directly, re-implement access checks, or accept unvalidated input in a route.
- **Customize without forking where possible.** Prefer config, new files, and extension points over rewriting scaffold internals. List unavoidable changes to scaffold-origin code under "Scaffold deviations" so upstream fixes can still be merged.
- **Brand:** keep the design system (ink, dusk, parchment, ember, gold, moss — the "sunset" values, see the 2026-11-05 entry below; Anton, Source Serif 4, Archivo) unless told otherwise.
- **Scaffold code is in scope for both reviews.** Inherited code, dependencies, default configs, and seed data are reviewed too. Report scaffold-origin findings separately from new-code findings.
- **On scaffold updates,** re-run both reviews against the whole updated scaffold, not just the diff.

- **2026-11-12 (latest) — Cloudflare rate-limiting rule created.** Requested as "proceed to the cloudflare rate limiting rule".
  - **Plan limits:** the zone is on the Free plan: one rate-limiting rule, matched on the URI path only (no method or host), counted per IP, 10-second window, Block for 10 seconds (Cloudflare's docs, checked).
  - **Rule "Email, claims and checkout",** created in the dashboard (Security → Security rules) after the user approved the exact rule:
    - matches `/api/amazon-reminders`, `/api/contact`, `/api/amazon-claims`, `/api/checkout`, `/api/auth/login-recorded`;
    - more than 5 requests in 10 seconds per IP blocks for 10 seconds;
    - Active on every hostname in the zone.
  - **Left out:**
    - `/api/responses` and `/api/progress`: a group on one church Wi-Fi shares an IP, and the single shared limit could block real readers. Both require sign-in and ownership.
    - `/login`, `/signup`, `/sneak-peek`: pages; sign-in codes go from the browser straight to Supabase, which throttles them.
  - **Checked live:** eight invalid POSTs to production's `/api/amazon-reminders` (no email sent) got 400 ×6 then 429 ×2, then 400 again after 12 s; the homepage stayed 200. SECURITY.md "Rate limiting" and BUILD_TASKS updated.
  - **Noticed:** the dashboard's page wrote to the user's clipboard during one of the menu clicks; the user was told to check it before pasting.
- **2026-11-12 (later) — Buy on Amazon explains the digital edition and emails the claim link.** Requested as a modal before going to Amazon; the user chose "option 1" (explain, offer to email the `/claim` link, then Amazon) over granting access on click.
  - **Why not an email at checkout:** Amazon's checkout isn't ours, and Amazon gives authors no buyer details and no purchase notification. Its affiliate reports are totals by title and day only. So an email given before going to Amazon says who clicked, not who bought, and the order number on `/claim` stays the check. Our own checkout already requires an account and unlocks the digital edition with a printed book.
  - **Modal** (`components/commerce/AmazonBuyModal.tsx`, used by `AmazonPurchase` when the title has a digital edition and a live link, on the product page and bundle component rows):
    - Explains the claim step and has an email field, filled in for a signed-in reader.
    - "Email me the link and continue to Amazon" is a real link that opens Amazon in a new tab, so a popup blocker never stops it. The email request goes out in the same click, and the tab then confirms it, or shows the claim address if sending failed.
    - "Continue to Amazon without the email" and "Already ordered? Claim it now".
    - Accessible dialog: labelled, focus starts in the email field and stays inside, Escape or Close returns focus to the button.
    - A title without a digital edition keeps the plain link.
  - **Route** (`POST /api/amazon-reminders`, `lib/commerce/amazonReminders.ts`, `sendAmazonClaimReminderEmail`): no sign-in, strict Zod, claimable titles only.
    - Limits: one email per title per address per day, three per address per day, ten requests per visitor (IP) per hour (429 after that).
    - Blocked addresses get nothing. The response is `{status: "ok"}` either way; a failed send answers 502 and isn't counted.
    - The email is fixed; only the admin-set title and the link vary.
  - **Table** `amazon_reminders` (migration `0019_quick_santa_claus.sql`, additive): only HMAC-SHA256 hashes of the email and IP, keyed with a sub-key derived from `TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`; rows older than a week are deleted as new ones arrive. SECURITY.md and PRIVACY.md updated.
  - **Tests:**
    - 7 route tests on the real test DB: sends and stores no address or IP; refuses bad input and titles without a digital edition or link; per-title and per-address limits silent; IP limit 429; blocked address silent; a failed send is retried; old rows purged.
    - 3 e2e tests in `amazon-claim.spec.ts` (modal with the email stubbed and Amazon in a new tab, Escape and focus return, axe), plus the no-study title keeps a plain link.
    - `npm run verify` clean; `npm test` 817/817; full desktop QA 296/296; Amazon, store, reader and accessibility on mobile 60/60.
    - Checked by screenshot at 375px (bottom sheet) and 1280px (centred).
  - **Review:** the new unauthenticated email route is the main risk. It's covered by the fixed content, limits, identical responses, hashed storage and short retention. Accepted (Low): one fixed email per title per day can be sent to an address someone doesn't own. Add `/api/amazon-reminders` to the Cloudflare rate-limit rule. No other findings.

  - **Found there:** broken Hebrew words, e.g. Ten Commandments' "פָּנִי ם". A scan of every loaded word study found 16 entries:
    - word breaks counted from the transliteration, so parentheticals, "/", "…" and a stuck-on gloss added false breaks or missed real ones;
    - Genesis Part 1 and Journey Part One set multi-word Hebrew left to right, so "El Roi" came out reversed;
    - "ə" wasn't counted as a transliteration letter ("b rît");
    - the transliteration and gloss were split at the first dash anywhere, even one inside the gloss ("lēb “heart…", and in the Sermon "rhaka empty-head…", "apechō to have received…", "patēr Father").
  - **Parser fixed (`hebrew_logical`):**
    - Word breaks come from letter spacing: 0 inside a word, at least a quarter of the type size between words, true of all 196 Hebrew lines in the books.
    - Word order is chosen by the transliteration's first consonants.
    - "·" is kept as its own word.
    - `LATIN` takes U+0250–U+02AF.
    - A transliteration on its own line is split from the gloss at the line, not at a dash.
    - **Result:** the only changes in all 31 titles are those fixes, in Genesis Part 1, Journey Parts One and Two, Hebrews, the Sermon on the Mount (plus two new anchors) and the Ten Commandments. Colossians is unchanged.
    - Data files updated; reload dry run: 6 studies, 0 problems.
    - **Reloaded and published, on the user's "Yes, reload and publish":**
      - Backup first (`backup-before-hebrew-fix.json` in the session scratchpad: 6 studies, 78 lessons).
      - `--replace --apply`: 72 weeks staged, 0 problems.
      - All 72 published through `publishChanges`. A one-off script did it, refusing unless exactly 72 were staged, and was deleted after use.
      - Checked by query: 0 pending; every snapshot equals its working copy; the published Hebrew reads אֵל רֳאִי, פָּנִים, יהוה.
      - Checked on staging: Ten Commandments Week 2 shows "פָּנִים · PASSAGE 1 · EXODUS 20:2–3" from the published version, with no console errors.

- **2026-11-12 (later still) — Study weeks no longer throw React error #418 on load.** Found after publishing the Journey weeks (entry below), then "Yes, fix it".
  - **Cause:** three things in a tabbed week come from the browser's localStorage:
    - the progress ring;
    - "N of 4 parts read";
    - the practice checkboxes.
    Both hooks behind them (`useTabProgress`, `useLocalProgress`) read localStorage during the first render. Any week with saved progress therefore drew differently from the server's HTML, and React threw #418 and redrew the page. The Journey made it near-universal: a week without a passage saves its Passage tab as done on first visit. Confirmed in dev mode: the ring read "20" against the server's "0".
  - **Fix:**
    - New `lib/hooks/useStoredSet.ts` (`useSyncExternalStore` with an empty server snapshot), shared by both hooks. Hydration matches the server; saved progress appears right after.
    - Every write goes through the current saved set, so the automatic "done" marks (no-passage tab, all stations, Lift Up) can't overwrite saved progress.
    - Marks also live in memory, so they still work for the visit when storage is unavailable.
    - A first try with `useEffect` + `setState` was refused by the lint rule `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`, and kept within it.
  - **Tests:**
    - New e2e in `study-reader.spec.ts`: mark a part, a tab and a practice, reload, then require saved progress shown and no hydration error. Failed before the fix with the ring diff.
    - `npm run verify` clean; `npm test` 809/809; full desktop QA 294/294; reader, Journey, library and accessibility on mobile 49/49.
    - Run in a worktree, on its own dev server (port 3107, removed afterward).
  - **Review:** no routes, input or data changed; stored values are parsed only as an array and used only for set membership. No findings.
  - **Shipped** `072a39f` (`npm run deploy -- both --yes`): **staging `89e9056f`**, **production `5bf4a7d8`**, every check passing.
  - **Checked on staging in fresh tabs:** Part One Week 6 (saved progress, ring 20%), Part Three Week 32 and Colossians Week 2: no console errors.

- **2026-11-12 (newest) — A signed-in account's missing `users` row is repaired on any request, not just App pages.** Requested as "can we mitigate this from happening again?" after the entry below; plan approved as "Yes, build it".
  - **Found:** the retry below makes a failed setup call rarer, not impossible. The only backstop was `app/(study-app)/layout.tsx`'s `ensureUserRow`, so App pages repaired a missing row, but checkout (`orders.user_id`), `/claim` (`amazon_claims`), `/account` and `/contact` didn't. A reader in that state who went straight to buy would have hit an error at checkout.
  - **Fix:** `resolveSession()` (`lib/auth/session.ts`) checks the blocked list and the row in one query (`accountState`, replacing the separate `isEmailBlocked` lookup, so no extra round trip) and calls `ensureUserRow` only when the row is missing. A blocked email never gets a row; an account with no email never writes one; an email held by another row stays signed in and is logged by user id only (as `/api/auth/login-recorded` already did). The study-app layout's own backstop is removed. `/api/auth/login-recorded` now only records the login event in practice (reporting).
  - **Deletion race closed:** `deleteAccount` (`lib/account/deleteAccount.ts`) deletes the data and row, then the Supabase login; a request from the same account in between could now recreate the row with the person's email. After a successful login deletion it clears the data once more (`clearAppData`). If the login deletion fails it doesn't, since the login still works.
  - **Considered, not done:** a database trigger on Supabase's `auth.users`. Admin Add user writes its own row and email blocking creates placeholder logins, so a trigger would collide with the first and give rows to the second, and it's a schema change on Supabase's side.
  - **Side effect:** a `qa-…@soul-dust-revival.test` account signing in on staging (real database) now gets a `users` row and shows in Admin → Users.
  - **Tests:** session 5 new (row created, existing row untouched, blocked gets none, email held by another row, no email); new `lib/account/deleteAccount.test.ts` 3 (plain delete, a row recreated mid-delete is cleared (confirmed failing without the fix), no second clear when the login deletion fails). Typecheck and lint clean (4 pre-existing warnings), `npm test` 802/802.
  - **Review:** no new routes or input; the query is parameterized and keyed on the verified session's id; nothing new logged with an email. No findings.
  - **Shipped** `762761a` (`npm run deploy -- both --yes`): **staging `4eace373`**, **production `5cb911ee`**, every check passing. Checked live on staging signed in as the user: My Library loads. Not exercised live: an account that actually lacks its row.
- **2026-11-12 — A real sign-up's account setup failed after a correct code; the setup call now retries.** Reported as "a user input their emailed code and received a message that they could not reach the server".
  - **Found:** the message is `AuthForm`'s catch for the POST to `/api/auth/login-recorded`, which runs after `verifyOtp` succeeds; it shows only when that request gets no answer at all (an HTTP error shows a different message). The route answered normally on all three production hosts (401 as a guest, no redirects). In the database, one real account (signed up 01:33:52Z, code entered 01:35:13Z) had a Supabase login but no `users` row and no login event; every other real account was fine. The 80 seconds between the two fit a reader switching to their mail app for the code. On return, a phone browser can send the next request on a connection that went stale in the background, and it resends only safe requests (GETs) there, never a POST, so the code check (to Supabase, a different host) worked and our POST failed. Most likely cause, not proven: Worker logs aren't kept.
  - **Repaired, on the user's "Yes, both":** that account's `users` row (its Supabase id, email and sign-up time, role `user`) and one login event at its real sign-in time, in one transaction, after confirming the email wasn't blocked or used by another row.
  - **Fix:** `lib/auth/postWithRetry.ts` retries the setup POST after a network failure or a 5xx, twice (400 ms, 1.2 s), and returns any 4xx at once (403 blocked, 409 conflicting account). The route is safe to repeat: the `users` insert ignores an existing row; a repeat only adds a second login event for the same sign-in. The "Try again" button still covers a failure after all three tries.
  - **Tests:** 6 in `lib/auth/postWithRetry.test.ts` (no retry on success, retry after a thrown fetch, 5xx then success, no retry on 401/403/409, final network error thrown, final 5xx returned). Typecheck and lint clean (4 pre-existing warnings), `npm test` 794/794. Not checked in a browser: the failure needs a real stale connection and a real emailed code.
  - **Review:** no new routes or input; same same-origin POST, now up to three times. No findings.
  - **Shipped** `53115bd` (`npm run deploy -- both --yes`, which also carried the image session's `17d77d5`/`bcac796` R2 custom-domain prep, at its request): **staging `2f9ed50e`**, **production `247d90c4`**, every check passing.
- **2026-11-11 (newest of all) — Images load from cache: fingerprinted hero photo, edge cache for resized images.** Requested as "the main homepage image and store and app images still take a moment to load even after a browser refresh".
  - **Found:** the hero (`/images/hero-sunset.jpg` as a string path) went through `/_next/image` with **no Cache-Control at all**: OpenNext's image handler only marks a response immutable when the source is `/_next/static/media` or the upstream says immutable, and a `/public` asset says neither. So browsers re-downloaded it on every visit and refresh (confirmed in the browser: the reload re-fetched it over the network). Covers were already immutable for browsers (the 2026-11-09 R2 Cache-Control fix), but a Worker's own responses never reach Cloudflare's CDN cache, so every new visitor, device and width paid a fresh IMAGES resize (~0.3–1 s each).
  - **Hero:** `DustHero.tsx` imports the photo, so Next fingerprints it under `/_next/static/media` (immutable, a year) and adds `placeholder="blur"`. New tracked `image-types.d.ts` references `next/image-types/global`, because `next-env.d.ts` is generated and git-ignored and a fresh checkout's `tsc` failed on the image import.
  - **Edge cache:** wrangler `main` is now `custom-worker.ts` (OpenNext's documented custom-worker pattern), which wraps `.open-next/worker.js` and re-exports its Durable Object classes. `lib/images/edgeImageCache.ts` stores `GET /_next/image` responses in `caches.default` only when they're 200 and immutable, keyed by the URL plus which of avif/webp the browser accepts (the handler varies on Accept; the Cache API keys on URL only). Every other request goes straight through; a cache failure never blocks the image. `custom-worker.ts` is excluded from `tsc` (it imports a build output); its logic lives in `lib/` and is tested. **Scaffold deviation:** the Worker entry point is no longer OpenNext's generated file.
  - **Tests:** 6 in `lib/images/edgeImageCache.test.ts` (hit after first resize, separate copies per format, errors/non-immutable not stored, other routes untouched, cache failure still serves, key shape). `npm run verify` clean, `npm test` 787/787 (in a worktree, since another session's dev server holds this folder's `.next`). Under `wrangler dev` with the real OpenNext build: hero now immutable; first resize 230–450 ms, repeats 3 ms; a jpeg-only Accept gets its own copy; a disallowed URL still 400 and isn't cached.
  - **Review:** no new routes, inputs or data; the cache only stores what the existing handler already produced for that exact URL, after its own allowlist and size checks. Extra query params create extra keys (cache size only, same resize cost as before). No findings.
  - **Shipped** `e02e9a3` (`npm run deploy -- both --yes`): **staging `908346bf`**, **production `5ce72ac5`**, every check passing. Checked live on production: the hero's optimized URL serves `immutable`; a never-requested cover size took 329 ms, then 44 ms and 46 ms on repeat. Not checked: the edge cache on `.workers.dev` preview links (Cache API writes don't apply there; harmless).
  - **Possible follow-up, not done:** covers are fetched from the rate-limited `pub-….r2.dev` URL; a custom R2 domain would make the first (uncached) resize per data center faster and isn't meant for production by Cloudflare.

  - **Delete:** each row (not your own, not another admin's: demote first) has **Delete**, opening an inline confirmation with "Also block this email" and an optional admin-only reason. `DELETE /api/admin/users` (`adminUserDeleteSchema`) runs the new shared `lib/account/deleteAccount.ts`, which `/api/account/delete` now uses too: saved answers, progress, access, Journey enrollment and login history are deleted in one transaction; the `users` row is deleted outright, or anonymized only if a foreign key (orders, posts) refuses (savepoint, Postgres `23503`); then the Supabase auth account is deleted. Anonymized accounts are hidden from the admin list. Behavior change for self-delete: the row is now removed when nothing needs it (it was always anonymized).
  - **Block:** new `blocked_emails` table (migration `0017_neat_cammi.sql`, a new table only; emails stored lowercased) and `lib/auth/blockedEmails.ts`. A block is enforced twice: the email's Supabase account is banned (`ban_duration` 876000h; a placeholder account is created when there's none, which also stops sign-up), and `getSession()` (now via `resolveSession()`) treats a blocked email's session as signed out. `/api/auth/login-recorded` ends such a session (403) and `AuthForm` shows "This email can't be used to sign in…" for that and for Supabase's `user_banned`. Delete-and-block deletes the person's own auth account first, then bans a placeholder, so their anonymized row can't be signed into again. A **Blocked emails** section on the page blocks any email (an existing user keeps their data and is banned) and unblocks (deletes the placeholder or lifts the ban; keeps the row if Supabase refuses). Admin Add user and email edits refuse a blocked email.

(answer: an API key from the Claude Console, pasted into Admin → Settings → Connections), then "Yes, update models".
  - **Found:** blog and social suggestions called `claude-sonnet-4-5`, which retires 2026-11-30. The key check on Connect called `claude-3-5-haiku-20241022`, retired 2026-02-19; it only refused on a 401, so a good key still saved, but the check proved nothing.
  - **New `lib/ai/claude.ts`** is the one place the app calls Claude: `SUGGESTION_MODEL = "claude-sonnet-5-5"`, `output_config.effort: "low"` (short drafts; thinking is always on for this model), and the server-side refusal fallback (`fallbacks: "default"`, beta `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`). `extractClaudeText()` joins every `text` block. That fixes a bug the model change would otherwise have caused: both routes read `content[0].text`, which is a thinking block on this model, so every suggestion would have come back as an empty list. A refusal, a reply cut off at `max_tokens`, a network failure and a 401 now each return a readable message instead of a parse error. `max_tokens` raised to leave room for thinking (blog 16000, social 4096).

Beginning credits stops he'd already finished.

Admin: "Present as a Journey" panel on the bundle edit page (`/api/admin/journeys`). A Journey's bundle can't be deleted; a Journey can't be removed once someone has begun.

Filters are mirrored into the URL (`?type=study&status=completed&q=psalm`, debounced `history.replaceState`) and each page reads the same params on the server, so reloading or sharing a filtered link arrives already filtered. Unknown values fall back to All; search is capped at 100 characters.

  - **Admin:** a Resources panel on `/admin/studies/[id]` (`components/admin/StudyResourcesPanel.tsx`) to add (title, optional description, PDF up to 100 MB with a progress bar), edit, reorder (↑/↓) and remove.
    - Routes: `/api/admin/study-resource-upload-url` returns a signed PUT and the server-chosen key, never a public URL. `/api/admin/study-resources` POST/PATCH/DELETE: the key must be one the upload route issued, size and type come from R2, a reorder must be exactly the study's resources, and removing deletes the file.
    - Deleting a study now removes its resources and files too.
  - **Reader:** a "Resources" section under the lesson list on `/studies/[slug]` (`components/study/StudyResources.tsx`), with title, description, "PDF · 89 KB" and a Download button. A free-sample reader sees the list locked ("Unlocks with purchase").
    - Download is `GET /api/study-resources/[id]`: signed-out visitors go to sign-in, a draft study is hidden from non-admins, non-owners get 403, and owners and admins get a 302 to a 5-minute signed R2 link saved under the file's name (`lib/r2/download.ts`). See SECURITY.md.

- **2026-11-11 (newer still) — `npm run deploy`: one committed, checked deploy command for both sites.** Requested as "Deploy script" after the same day's outage and broken covers, both caused by ad hoc deploy helpers kept in a session scratchpad.
  - **`scripts/deploy/deploy.ts`** (`npm run deploy -- [staging|production|both] [--no-promote] [--yes] [--allow-unpushed]`, default both). Per site, staging first:
    - Builds the pushed HEAD (refuses an unpushed commit) in a clean temporary worktree.
    - Uses a placeholder `.env.local` written by `placeholderEnv`: only `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` and `R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` are real, plus the site's hostnames and launch mode. Child processes get the shell env minus every app variable, so an exported secret can't override the placeholder.
    - Stops if a public value is missing, if any of the real secrets in `.env.local` (digit names included) appears anywhere in `.open-next`, or if the bundle lacks the Supabase host, R2 host, app hostname or (production) `sneak_peek`.
    - Uploads an inactive version (`opennextjs-cloudflare upload`) and runs `runChecks` on its preview link: `/`, `/blog`, the sneak-peek gates (or an open store on staging), `/library` (forwarded to the app host on the real marketing host, otherwise requires sign-in), guest 403/401 on upload and signing routes, and a real R2 cover through `/_next/image`.
    - Asks before switching production (`--yes` skips), switches with `wrangler versions deploy <id>@100%`, re-runs the checks live, and rolls back to the previous version automatically if they fail.
    - Finds Node 22 for wrangler itself, and confirms the project `.env.local` is unchanged afterward.
  - `npm run cf:deploy` now runs the same script; the old `cf:deploy`/`cf:upload` (raw build + deploy, which bakes every secret in `.env.local` into the Worker) are gone. README, BUILD_TASKS §4 and step 8 below point to it.

The second run passed every check on both previews (staging `14b5fcf6`, production `30820e5d`, both left inactive; live stays `00883004` / `1319cc0f`). Promotion and automatic rollback haven't run for real yet: the next real deploy will be the first.
- **2026-11-11 (newer) — Upload size caps enforced by R2, server-chosen upload keys; a deploy-script bug took production down for ~6 minutes and had broken cover images since `a322659d`.** Requested as "Upload size caps".
  - **Caps that R2 itself enforces.** `createPresignedUploadUrl(key, contentType, size)` (`lib/r2/upload.ts`) now signs `ContentLength`, so the link only accepts that exact body (`X-Amz-SignedHeaders=content-length;host`). Checked against the real bucket before building: a link signed for 100 bytes refused 99, 101 and 5,000 bytes and a chunked upload with no length (all 403), and stored only exactly 100. Each route requires `size` and caps it (`lib/uploads/limits.ts`: images 20 MB, captions 2 MB, leader PDFs 100 MB, cover PDF 25 MB, cover crops 30 MB); `invalidUploadResponse` returns the cap as a readable `message`.
  - **Server-chosen keys everywhere.** `/api/admin/upload-url` takes a `folder` (`products`/`bundles`/`social`/`content`) and stores `{folder}/{uuid}.{ext from type}`; `/api/admin/leader-file-upload-url` stores `leader-resources/{uuid}.pdf`. Schemas are `.strict()`, so a `key` is refused. Closes both upload findings deferred since 2026-11-03.
  - **One browser helper**, `lib/r2/browserUpload.ts` (`uploadFileToR2`, `requestUploadSlots`, `putToSlot`, `UploadError`), used by the builder, product, bundle, social and leader-resource forms and the cover-PDF upload. It checks the cap first and every response after. Product, bundle, social and leader uploads previously never checked the PUT's status, so a failure was silent; each now shows an error next to its field.
  - **Tests:** 15 new (route tests for all three single-file routes: admin-only, server keys, exact size signed, caps, client key refused; cover-route size cases; the browser helper; `oversizeMessage`), existing upload tests updated. `npm run verify` clean, `npm test` 538/538. QA browser suite not re-run (no spec uploads files; the builder's upload helpers were the only builder change).
  - **Live on staging, signed in as the user:** the real New bundle form uploaded an image to a server-named `bundles/<uuid>.png` (PUT 200); a 21 MB file showed "This file is 21 MB; the limit here is 20 MB." and attempted no upload; direct calls were refused for 21 MB images, 101 MB PDFs, 3 MB captions, a client `key`, and covers without sizes; a 5,000-byte PUT into a 100-byte link was refused and stored nothing; a real captions file uploaded. Only the two intended test files were written, both deleted. (Browser uploads can't be checked locally: R2's CORS rule deliberately excludes localhost.)
  - **Self-review:** admin-only routes, Zod with bounds, keys server-generated, no secrets reach the browser; no findings.
  - **Production outage, caused by the deploy procedure, not the code.** The scratchpad helper that writes the placeholder `.env.local` read `.env.local` relative to the current folder. Run inside the build worktree for the production build, it read and truncated the same file, so `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` were baked in empty and every page that touches auth returned 500. Production version `2531be91` was live 15:32–15:38Z; rolled back to `4926de03` within about two minutes of the first failed check (rollback confirmed 200s), and the failed version was then diagnosed on its own preview URL without touching traffic.
  - **Found while diagnosing: cover images had been broken on both sites since `a322659d`.** The same helper matched env keys with `^[A-Z_]+=`, which skips any name containing a digit, so `R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` was never written; `next.config.js` reads it at build time for `images.remotePatterns`, so every `/_next/image` request for an R2 cover returned 400 ("url parameter is not allowed"). Checked each earlier production version's preview URL: `032be409` (launch) 200, `a322659d`, `5c105189`, `4926de03` all 400. The secret scanner had the same two flaws (relative path, digit-blind), so when run inside a worktree it compared the build against placeholders. Builds were never at risk of leaking secrets (the placeholder file only ever holds real values for the documented public keys), but the scan wasn't checking what it claimed. Both helpers now read the project's `.env.local` by absolute path, match `^[A-Z0-9_]+=`, and the writer refuses to target the project's own `.env.local`; re-scanned against all 22 real secrets: 0 hits.
  - **Shipped, safer procedure:** each build was uploaded with `opennextjs-cloudflare upload` (an inactive version), checked on its own preview URL (pages, gates, guest 403, a real cover through `/_next/image` 200), then promoted with `wrangler versions deploy <id>@100%`. **Production `1319cc0f`** (sneak-peek gates unchanged, covers 200, `/library` on `app.` asks for login) and **staging `00883004`** (pages 200, covers 200), both from `66b1fe6`. An earlier staging deploy of the same commit, `191e529b`, ran with the cover bug and was replaced. **Use upload → preview check → promote for every deploy from now on**, and check a cover image through `/_next/image`, not just page status codes.
- **2026-11-11 (later) — Video/audio uploads have no 200 MB limit: resumable (tus) uploads to Cloudflare Stream.** Requested as "long video uploads".
  - **Server:** `/api/admin/stream-upload-url` now takes `{ size, name }` (`streamUploadSchema`: up to Stream's 30 GB). `createStreamTusUpload` (`lib/stream/upload.ts`) reserves a tus upload with `POST .../stream?direct_user=true`. The reservation is for that exact `Upload-Length`, private (`requiresignedurls`), capped at **4 hours** of media (was 1 hour with the old basic upload), and expires in 6 hours. It returns the one-time `Location` URL plus `stream-media-id` as the uid. The basic one-request upload and its 200 MB cap are gone.
  - **Browser:** new `components/admin/builder/tusUpload.ts`, a dependency-free tus client.
    - It sends 50 MB chunks (Stream's rules: 5–200 MiB, constant, multiples of 256 KiB).
    - After a dropped connection, a 409 or a 5xx, it backs off (1s doubling, up to 30s, 6 tries), asks the server for the real offset, and carries on. An expired link is reported, so a fresh upload starts.
    - `uploadToStream` remembers an unfinished upload in `localStorage`, keyed by file name, size and modified time, for up to 5 hours. Choosing the same file again after a reload continues it.
  - **Checked against Cloudflare before building:** the tus Location is on `upload.cloudflarestream.com` (already in CSP `connect-src`), `PATCH` is CORS-allowed from any origin, and `Upload-Offset` is exposed. Both probe reservations were deleted.
  - **Tests:** 12 new: chunking and progress, resume from the server offset, a mid-chunk drop resuming from what arrived, 409/5xx re-sync, a non-advancing 204, an expired link, refusal and give-up, plus route tests for admin-only, exact headers/metadata, bad sizes, and a 502 on Stream failure. `npm run verify` clean, `npm test` 525/525.
  - **Live, before shipping:** on the isolated QA server (real Stream credentials, local test DB), a 210 MB file went through the real Video block in 5 PATCHes. A reload at 47% and re-choosing the file continued the *same* reservation from 24% (the last completed 50 MB chunk). Stream received all 220,200,960 bytes, private, with the 4-hour cap. The random-bytes file then failed processing as `ERR_NON_VIDEO`, as expected, and was deleted (Stream empty).
  - **Self-review:** admin-only route with Zod input; the API token stays server-side; the browser gets only a one-time URL for one reserved file; the filename is base64-encoded in tus metadata. No findings. SECURITY.md "File uploads" updated.
  - **Shipped per step 8:** committed `86610fe`, pushed, and deployed staging `457cb48d` then production `4926de03`. Both built from clean worktrees (0 secret hits, `sneak_peek` in production, `--keep-vars`, `.env.local` unchanged).
    - Checked live: public pages 200 on both; production's sneak-peek gates unchanged; guest `POST /api/admin/stream-upload-url` 403 on both.
    - Signed in as the user on staging, the deployed route reserved a real tus upload on `upload.cloudflarestream.com` (`HEAD` showed offset 0 of 1000 from the staging page). That probe reservation was then deleted, and Stream is empty.
- **2026-11-11 (later) — Draft and publish for live lessons (plan approved: "go straight to draft-and-publish").** Staging and production share one database and the builder autosaves, so any edit to a published lesson used to reach readers within a second. Now a published lesson has a working copy (what the builder edits: `content_blocks` rows + `lessons.study_content`) and a frozen **published snapshot** (`lessons.published_content`) that readers see.
  - **Rules** (all in `lib/lessons/publishedContent.ts`):
    - `ensureSnapshot` runs inside every working-copy write (content-blocks create/update/reorder/delete/restore, the week save). On the first edit after publishing, it freezes the pre-edit content and sets `lessons.has_draft_changes`. So **no backfill**: unedited lessons behave exactly as before.
    - Removing a block the snapshot shows sets `content_blocks.removed_at`, because answers reference it by foreign key; Undo restores that original row (`PATCH { restore, insertAt }`).
    - `publishChanges` freezes the working copy and purges unanswered removed blocks, inside a savepoint so a reader answering at that instant can't fail the publish. `discardChanges` restores fields, order and the week, and deletes blocks added since publishing (keeping any a reader answered, set aside).
    - The Published toggle (single, list, "Publish all" done in bulk, create-as-published) freezes on publish and clears on unpublish. Toggling a lesson off and on again therefore ships whatever the working copy holds.
    - Title, Published, Free sample and order are not drafted.
  - **Reader side:**
    - The lesson page renders the snapshot. `?draft=1` shows the working copy only for an admin preview, with an "Admin preview: unpublished changes" banner.
    - The devotional Year/Journal take each published day's question from its snapshot (`jsonb_path_query_first`).
    - `/api/stream/sign-url` signs snapshot media for readers. Draft media is signed only for admins: the preview page sends `draft: true` via `useSignedMediaUrl`.
    - `/api/responses` refuses a reader's answer to a question only the draft has.
    - `lib/ai/projectContext.ts` uses published text.
  - **Builder:**
    - `LessonHeader` shows a Live / Unpublished changes panel with **Publish changes** and **Discard changes** (with a confirmation). Both first flush every pending autosave on the page (new `flushAllAutosaves` in `lib/hooks/useAutosave.ts`).
    - Builder saves fire `notifyDraftChanged` (`lib/builder/draftEvents.ts`) so the panel updates live.
    - `LessonList` shows "Unpublished changes" badges.
  - **Schema:** migration `0013_abnormal_ender_wiggin.sql` (additive: `lessons.published_content jsonb`, `lessons.has_draft_changes boolean default false`, `content_blocks.removed_at timestamptz`). Applied to the local test DB (via `psql` plus a tracking row, since `migrate-direct.mjs` forces TLS).
  - **Tests:**
    - 15 new tests in `lib/lessons/publishedContent.test.ts`, through the real admin/reader/signing/answers routes: snapshot on first edit, drafts untouched, soft-remove plus answer plus purge, Discard of blocks and week, Publish, toggles, Publish all, snapshot vs draft signing, draft-only media and questions refused for readers, Undo restore, and admin-only actions.
    - Two new e2e tests (a plain lesson's full edit → reader unchanged → `?draft=1` preview → publish → discard flow, and the same for a week).
    - Updated: the week-media e2e now previews with `?draft=1`.
    - Tests that edit published fixtures now discard their drafts, and the two drag tests use a taller viewport (the header's Live panel pushed blocks below the fold on short screens).
    - `npm run verify` clean, `npm test` 514/514, QA suite 249/249 desktop and 249/249 mobile.
  - **Review:** an independent review found no Critical or High issues. Fixed:
    - Medium: the devotional Year/Journal read draft question wording.
    - Medium: Undo made a copy of a set-aside block.
    - Low: needless snapshot rewrites; "Publish all" doing about 5 queries per lesson; a purge race with a concurrent answer; answers to draft-only questions; AI context reading draft or removed text; an admin's own preview answers keeping removed blocks alive.
    - Deferred (Low, documented in BUILD_TASKS): maintenance scripts write the working copy without setting the flag.
  - **Shipped on the user's go-ahead ("migrate and ship"):**
    - Migration `0013` applied to the real database first (`npm run db:migrate`; 14 migrations recorded). Confirmed via `information_schema` that all three columns exist, with 0 snapshots, 0 draft flags and 0 removed blocks, and both live sites still returned 200 on the old code.
    - Then pushed `4e5d4de` and deployed **staging `9d8525e1`**:

      - The real Colossians Week 1 builder shows the Live panel, with no lesson flagged.
    - Then **production `a322659d` → `5c105189`** (sneak-peek build: gates unchanged, guest publish action 403).
    - Both builds came from clean worktrees with placeholder env: 0 secret hits each, `sneak_peek` confirmed in the production bundle, `--keep-vars`, `.env.local` hash unchanged.
  - **Live check on staging, signed in as the user**, in Part Four (open only to Journey bundle buyers, so invisible to the public):

- **2026-11-10 (latest of all) — staging returned 500 on about 1 in 4 page loads because the two Hyperdrive configs together could open far more connections than Supabase's session pooler allows; both limits lowered on the user's go-ahead.** Found while checking the `/store` deploy (`abd2b56f`): `/`, `/store`, `/blog` failed intermittently. `wrangler tail` showed `(EMAXCONNSESSION) max clients reached in session mode - max clients are limited to pool_size: 15` and `Connection terminated unexpectedly`. Both Hyperdrive configs point at `aws-0-us-east-1.pooler.supabase.com:5432` (Supavisor **session** mode, 15 client slots in total): staging `2a924ce2` had `origin_connection_limit` 20 and the new production `8279fc0c` (created the same afternoon without a limit, so the default) had 60, up to 80 against 15. Production was already live with real sign-ups, but sampled fine; staging was the one starved. **Fix:** `wrangler hyperdrive update ... --origin-connection-limit` set staging to **5** (Hyperdrive's minimum) and production to **8**: 13 in total, leaving 2 slots for migrations and scripts (`MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL` uses the same session pooler). Read back to confirm; caching still disabled on both. Verified straight after: staging 30/30 and production 12/12 requests 200. **Rule from here on:** the sum of every Hyperdrive config's limit plus anything else on the session pooler must stay under Supabase's pool size (Hyperdrive's limit is soft, so leave headroom). To give production more room, first raise Supabase's pool size (Database → Connection pooling; the ceiling depends on the plan), then raise the limits to fit.

QA specs/checklists updated to `/store`; `qa/findings/` left as the historical record. QA Playwright suite not re-run. **Committed as `887ffa8` and deployed to staging on the user's go-ahead** (Worker version `abd2b56f`, replacing `841d87c8`, which `main` already contained). Same safe procedure: clean detached worktree, placeholder `.env.local` with the documented public values and the staging-split vars, `NEXT_PUBLIC_LAUNCH_MODE` blank, 0 secret hits across 2,154 output files, `--keep-vars`, main `.env.local` hash unchanged. Checked live on `.workers.dev`: `/`, `/store`, a `/store/<slug>` page, `/blog`, `/checkout` 200; `/library` 307 to login; `/books` and `/books/<slug>` 308 to their `/store` equivalents; homepage links all `/store` (nav absolute to `staging.souldustrevival.com/store`, so the split is still on); `staging.souldustrevival.com` still Access-gated. - **2026-11-10 (newest of all) — fixed Log out not working on staging.** Reported with a screenshot: the Profile menu stayed on "Logging out..." and the visitor stayed signed in. Most likely cause: the browser held two copies of the Supabase session cookie with the same name, one on `.staging.souldustrevival.com` (the shared subdomain-split cookie) and an older host-only one from a staging build where the split wasn't configured. The browser client's `signOut()` only deletes the copy matching the current cookie options, so the next page load was still signed in by the leftover copy, and `router.push` to the same page left the button stuck. Not reproduced live (the in-app browser had no signed-in session); fixed for this and the other plausible cause (a hung client `signOut()`) together:
  - **New `POST /api/auth/logout`** revokes the session with Supabase, then expires every `sb-` cookie in both forms (host-only and `Domain=` the configured cookie domain). Non-Supabase cookies such as `CF_Authorization` are left alone; a Supabase failure still clears the cookies. `/api` is outside the proxy's matcher, so no session refresh can rewrite the cookies mid-logout.
  - **New `lib/auth/logOut.ts`** is the one logout action for `SiteNav`, `AdminNav`, and `AccountPageClient` (previously three copies of `signOut()` + `router.push` + `router.refresh`). It always ends with `window.location.assign(marketingUrl("/"))`, even on a failed or timed-out (8s) request, so the button can't stay stuck. New `cookieDomain()` accessor in `lib/domains.ts`.
  - A stale host-only copy on the *other* host (e.g. `app.staging...` when logging out from `staging...`) isn't reachable from this request; it would be cleared the next time Log out is used on that host. One-time transition artifact.
  - 5 new route tests. `npm run verify` clean, `npm test` 497/497.
  - **Committed as `b54fac4` and deployed to staging on the user's go-ahead** (Worker version `841d87c8`, replacing `36981a68`, which `main` already contained). Same safe procedure: clean detached worktree, placeholder `.env.local` with the documented public values and the staging-split vars, `NEXT_PUBLIC_LAUNCH_MODE` blank, 0 secret hits across 2,154 output files, `--keep-vars`, main `.env.local` hash unchanged. Checked live on `.workers.dev`: `/`, `/books`, `/blog`, `/checkout` 200; `/library` 307; `POST /api/auth/logout` with a test `sb-` cookie returns 200 and expires it twice (host-only and `Domain=.staging.souldustrevival.com`) without touching `CF_Authorization`; `staging.souldustrevival.com` still Access-gated. **Not yet confirmed by clicking Log out in a real signed-in browser.**
- **2026-11-10 (newest) — captions served from the app, upload progress, media signed when shown; live staging test found uploaded video had never played, fixed, and found the R2 bucket has no CORS rule (needs a decision).** Follow-ups 1–3 from the builder work, committed as `259fab4`:
  - **Captions:** an uploaded `.vtt` is now served from the app's own origin by `GET /api/captions/[file]`, which proxies only a server-generated `captions/<uuid>.vtt` key (`CAPTIONS_FILE_PATTERN`) from R2 as `text/vtt`. `trackUrlForCaptions()` rewrites R2 captions URLs for the reader. A `<track>` on the R2 host was cross-origin and never loaded; this sidesteps CORS rather than adding `crossorigin` to the video.
  - **Upload progress:** `sendWithProgress` (XHR `upload.onprogress`) drives a native `<progress>` bar (`UploadProgressBar`) for image, captions, and video/audio uploads, ending in "Processing…". Stream uploads over 200 MB are refused up front (basic upload limit; tus not built).
  - **Signing on show:** `useSignedMediaUrl` signs an uploaded video/audio only once its player is near the viewport (IntersectionObserver, 800px margin), so a player in an unopened tab doesn't sign at page load and expire; a playback error re-signs once.
  - **Live test on staging** (deployed `259fab4` as Worker `4fcd9aab`, user signed in as admin, throwaway draft lesson deleted afterward):
    - Video upload through the builder worked, with the progress bar going from 0 to 100% to "Processing video".
    - **Uploaded video had never played anywhere:** the Worker's `CLOUDFLARE_STREAM_CUSTOMER_CODE` holds the whole host (`<code>.cloudflarestream.com`) and `generateSignedStreamUrl` appended the suffix again. Fixed in `f5847a7` with `streamCustomerHost()`, which accepts the bare code, `customer-<code>`, or the full host, so no secret had to change. Deployed as Worker `36981a68`, built the same safe way (secret scan 0 hits, `.env.local` hash unchanged). Confirmed on staging: the reader signed once and the 6-second test video played in Chrome (native HLS).
    - **Captions upload failed: the R2 bucket `soul-dust-revival` had no CORS configuration at all** (`wrangler r2 bucket cors list` → "The CORS configuration does not exist"). So **every browser-to-R2 presigned PUT on staging failed the preflight**: captions, lesson and product images, and the cover-PDF upload. This corrects the cover-upload entry below, which assumed image uploads already worked from staging; existing covers and images only got in through scripts.
  - **R2 CORS rule added the same day, on the user's go-ahead** (`wrangler r2 bucket cors set`, read back to confirm). It allows `GET` and `PUT` from `https://staging.souldustrevival.com`, `https://app.staging.souldustrevival.com`, `https://souldustrevival.com`, `https://www.souldustrevival.com`, and `https://app.souldustrevival.com`. Allowed headers are `content-type` and `cache-control` only, which is all any browser upload in this app sends. It exposes `ETag`, with a max age of 3600. Local dev origins are deliberately not allowed; add `http://localhost:3000` if browser uploads from `next dev` are ever needed.
  - **Verified on staging after the rule:**
    - A captions `.vtt` uploaded from the signed-in admin's browser through the real presigned PUT (200).
    - In the reader, the track loaded from `/api/captions/<uuid>.vtt` (readyState 2, 1 cue), and "Captions test line" was the active cue while the uploaded Stream video played.
    - The player signed once, about 2.6s after load. A first attempt signed only after about 19s, but that was traced to the browser pane not painting frames, not the app.
    - The throwaway lesson was deleted.

- **2026-11-10 (latest) — made the storefront cover tiles one uniform height, each cover keeping its own shape.** Requested against live screenshots: with "shape per trim" (below), 6x9 tiles stood taller than 8.5x11 ones, so rows didn't line up. Asked directly whether uniform tiles would affect the parser (no -- display is CSS only; crops and stored images are untouched) and for every tile at The Journey's size. Since 6x9 covers (~0.667) are narrower than The Journey/8.5x11 (~0.77), three options were laid out -- same height with own width, same box cropped (cuts ~7% off the top and bottom of a 6x9 cover, through its border), or same box letterboxed (background strips) -- and the user chose the first. `CoverThumbnail` (homepage Featured grid and `/books`) now renders a fixed-shape slot (`31 / 40`, the widest cover shape sold) with the cover inside at full slot height and its own stored aspect ratio, centered -- never cropped, no visible tile background. Verified in local dev at desktop and 375px: every slot one height (393px on `/books`, 292px on the homepage, titles on one line), each cover's box matching its image's own ratio within 0.002, 6x9 covers centered with ~20px either side, no horizontal scroll. `CoverGallery` (detail page) and the other views keep their own-shape display unchanged. Small known quirk, left as is: on a 6x9 tile the eyebrow/title text starts at the column's left edge, ~20px left of the narrower cover's own edge.
- **2026-11-10 (later) — moved book-cover PDF splitting into the admin's browser, so 300ppi KDP covers upload through the admin form at all.** Found during the trim-geometry work below: the admin cover-PDF routes capped the embedded image at 6 megapixels (Cloudflare Workers' fixed 128MB-per-isolate memory ceiling), but every real design-system cover is 300ppi -- an 8.5x11 full wrap is ~17.8MP, the Psalms 6x9 ~11MP -- so any fresh upload was rejected as "too high-resolution" before the parser ever ran. Existing covers had only ever got in via scripts.
  - **Shared crop logic, two encoders.** `lib/images/bookCover.ts` is now dependency-free crop logic usable in both environments: `extractCoverImage()` (unpdf/pdf.js, page-size and pixel-count guards) and `computeCoverCrops()` (the trim-geometry/detection logic below, plus the no-overlap clamp). `lib/images/splitBookCoverPdf.ts` encodes with Photon for Node (reprocess script, tests; 80MP cap). `lib/images/splitBookCoverInBrowser.ts` encodes with a canvas sized only to each crop -- never a full-wrap-sized canvas, which can exceed Safari's ~16.7MP canvas limit (40MP cap). Same crop decisions everywhere, so a fresh upload and a reprocess of the stored PDF produce identical crops.
  - **Upload flow.** `lib/images/uploadBookCover.ts` (used by both `ProductForm` and `StudyForm`) checks type/size (25MB), splits in the browser (pdf.js loaded on demand), asks the new admin-only `POST /api/admin/cover-upload-urls` (`coverUploadUrlsSchema`: `target: "product" | "study"`) for presigned PUTs under one fresh server-generated folder -- a product gets source PDF + back + front, a study just front -- and uploads straight to R2. The old server-side `app/api/admin/{products,studies}/cover-pdf` routes (and their tests) were deleted. SECURITY.md's "File uploads" section updated with the trade-offs (guards now protect the admin's tab, not the server; keys stay server-generated).
  - **Found while testing in a real browser:** pdf.js returns decoded images as `ImageBitmap`s in a browser rather than raw pixels, which unpdf's `extractImages()` silently skips ("no embedded cover image"). Fixed by passing `isOffscreenCanvasSupported: false, isImageDecoderSupported: false` to `getDocumentProxy` (already the defaults in Node), so both environments decode identically. The bundled pdf.js contains no `new Function`/eval, so the production CSP (no `'unsafe-eval'`) doesn't block it.
  - **Verified** with the two real stored 300ppi source PDFs (Part One 8.5x11, Psalms 6x9) in a real browser, both in dev and against a local production build under the real nonce CSP: each split in ~1.5s via the trim-geometry path, with pixel-identical crop sizes to the Node path (2316x3027, 1670x2505). 5 new tests for the upload-links route (admin-only, three keys in one server-generated folder for a product, front-only for a study, a fresh folder every call, rejects an unknown target or a client-supplied key); the 16 parser tests pass unchanged against the refactor. **Not verified:** an actual upload through the admin form (no admin session in this environment) and the R2 bucket's CORS rule for a PDF PUT (the R2 token can't read bucket CORS) -- same presigned-PUT mechanism and headers as the admin image uploads that already work from staging.

Confirmed live via the `.workers.dev` URL: `/`, `/books`, a book page, `/checkout` 200; `/library` and `/admin/products` 307 a guest to `/login`; `POST /api/admin/cover-upload-urls` 403s a guest (route live, admin-gated); the old `/api/admin/products/cover-pdf` 404s; nav still reads "Books & Studies"; both staging hosts still Access-gated. **Still to confirm with a real admin upload on staging** (no admin session here).

No real lesson was touched.

    - **Delete draft lesson.**

Imported weeks always had stations; weeks built from scratch don't. Fixed in `4574d38`: the tab shows its intro plus "Go to Live It", Live It hides an empty prayer timer, and `47a1bda` hides an empty "Four Parts" heading. A regression e2e builds a minimal week and opens it in the reader.
  - **Bug 2:** after a new week's first save, the free-form block canvas stayed on screen until a manual reload. The builder relied on `router.refresh()`, and on staging both refresh requests came back `ERR_ABORTED`. Fixed in `47a1bda`: new `components/admin/builder/LessonEditorBody.tsx` decides on the client, flipping the moment the first save succeeds. Verified fixed on staging.
  - **Also:** the admin e2e "open a lesson" helper now waits for `networkidle`. Clicks before hydration were silently lost, which made two tests flaky.
  - **Deployed** `4574d38` (Worker `a453c0e8`) and then `47a1bda` (Worker `105a3006`), with the same safe procedure as before: clean worktree, placeholder env, secret scan, `--keep-vars`, `.env.local` hash-confirmed unchanged. `npm test` 462/462, QA suite 241/241 on desktop and mobile.
  - **Found, needs a decision: Hyperdrive read caching is on** (`wrangler hyperdrive get` shows `caching.disabled: false`, Cloudflare's default of about a 60-second max age). Plain reads outside a transaction can be served from cache, so staging showed stale data: a just-created lesson was missing from the study's lesson list for about a minute, and deleted lessons stayed listed for about 20 seconds. The same applies to any read: an admin reloading a page right after editing can see older content, and **an entitlement check right after a purchase could briefly still say "no access."** Fix: `wrangler hyperdrive update 2a924ce2874d45719269fa7d639b7b85 --caching-disabled`. **Done the same day, on the user's go-ahead**: `wrangler hyperdrive update ... --caching-disabled`; `wrangler hyperdrive get` now shows `caching.disabled: true`. Verified on staging from the signed-in admin session: a throwaway draft lesson appeared in the study's list immediately after creation and was gone immediately after deletion (previously about 60s and about 20s of lag); the test lesson was deleted. Connection pooling, Hyperdrive's main benefit, is unaffected. Public pages still return 200. **The production Hyperdrive config (not created yet) should get its own config with caching disabled from the start, not reuse this one.** If public-page speed matters at launch, cache rendered public pages, not raw database reads.
- **2026-11-10 (later) — images, video, audio, and callouts can now go *inside* a Study week, not just on free-form lessons.** Requested after the builder shipped ("I see on the Study tab I can add new sections and parts, only"). The user picked "media inside each item" over a full block-model migration.
  - **Where media goes:** each section, part, station, and practice gets its own reorderable media list ("+ Image / + Video / + Audio / + Callout"), and each of the five tabs gets a "More for this tab" list. They use the same editors as the lesson block canvas (upload or link, alt text, captions, transcript). Readers see an item's media right after that item's text; tab media sits at the end of the tab, before its done button.
  - **Data:** additive optional fields on the `studyContent` JSON (`media` on sections/parts/stations/practices, `tabMedia` per tab; `WeekMedia` in `lib/study/studyContent.ts`, helpers in `lib/study/weekMedia.ts`). No migration, and the 12 Colossians weeks and 4 Journey parts are untouched.

The route now refuses an id that isn't in that week, and refuses drafts to non-admins for both signing paths, matching the reader page. Players show "couldn't load" instead of an endless placeholder when signing fails.

    - A malformed lesson id in a reader URL is now a redirect, not a 500.
    - Two e2e tests weren't waiting for their final "restore order" save, which left the next run starting from the wrong order.
  - **Review:** an independent review passed security and code quality, with Low findings only; the sign-failure state, the labelled media groups, and the unique-id check were fixed. **Deferred (Low):** uploaded media on tabs a reader hasn't opened is signed at page load, so a signed URL can expire (1 hour) before a reader gets to it; fix by signing on first show.

The main `.env.local` was hash-confirmed unchanged. Checked live on `.workers.dev`: public pages return 200, gated pages 307, the week-media signing path 401 without a session, lesson `DELETE` 403, and the custom domain is still Access-gated. The builder and reader with real media weren't loaded on staging (no admin session here). Not pushed to the git remote.
- **2026-11-10 — rebuilt lesson authoring as a drag-and-drop Lesson Builder (Phase 1 of 2, plan approved: one builder UI over today's two data models, no content migration), with URL-or-upload media; fixed 6 pre-existing bugs found along the way.** Requested as "turn the App/Study create/edit into a true builder with drag-and-drop components ... image, video and audio should allow a URL and an upload."
  - **Block builder** (`components/admin/builder/LessonBuilder.tsx`): a categorized palette (click to append, drag to insert at a position), collapsible block cards with summaries and "needs alt text/captions/transcript" pills, "+ Insert here" between blocks, Duplicate, Remove with Undo, Expand/Collapse all, and per-block debounced autosave (`lib/hooks/autosaveQueue.ts` + `useAutosave.ts`: one request per burst of typing, never two in flight, a failed save keeps its edits for Retry, flushes with `keepalive` on unmount/pagehide). Every block type comes from one registry (`blockRegistry.ts`); `ContentBlockData` now has one definition (`lib/content/blocks.ts`) instead of three.
  - **Media**: `MediaSourceField` gives image/video/audio blocks "Upload file | Use a link". New nullable `content_blocks.media_url` (migration `drizzle/0011_tired_the_call.sql`); a block plays from an uploaded Stream asset or a linked URL, never both. All media URLs are https-only, and a block can only write its own type's columns. The reader uses a plain `<img>` for non-R2 images, and plays linked video/audio directly. CSP `img-src`/`media-src` widened to `https:` (script-src/connect-src unchanged). See SECURITY.md "Linked (external) media" and PRIVACY.md.
  - **Study week builder** (`builder/study/StudyWeekBuilder.tsx` + `panels.tsx`, replacing `StudyContentEditor`): five tabs mirroring the reader. Fixed fields are pinned cards; every list (sections, parts, stations, practices, prayer beats, paragraphs, bottom-line/benediction lines, verses) is reorderable by drag or Move buttons, with Undo on remove. The passage has a paste-and-split box (`lib/study/parsePassageText.ts`). Autosave only fires when the week passes `studyWeekContentSchema` client-side; otherwise each tab shows how many required fields are still empty (`lib/study/studyContentIssues.ts`). So starting a week persists nothing until it's valid. Practices are numbered from list order (server-side too). A new station gets its answer-block id from the builder at creation (`crypto.randomUUID()`), and the server creates the block under that id, so the client and server can never disagree about it mid-save.
  - **Lesson list**: drag/Move reorder (wiring the reorder API that existed but no UI ever called), per-lesson Published toggle, inline "Add lesson" replacing `window.prompt`. **Draft lessons can be deleted** (requested from a staging screenshot): each draft row has a Delete button with a confirmation prompt, backed by a new `DELETE /api/admin/lessons`. It refuses a published lesson (unpublish first) and any lesson a reader has answered or made progress on. The admin's own preview answers and progress don't count, and are removed with it. Blocks are deleted in one transaction, and the study's lesson order is re-numbered. Focus moves to the neighboring lesson. **Lesson header**: inline title, Published, "Preview as reader".
  - **Server**: content-block create supports `insertAt` and `initial` fields (duplicate/undo); reorder is transactional and rejects mixed-lesson or partial lists (409 stale); delete re-compacts order. `lib/db/order.ts` writes dense orderIndexes in one parameterized UPDATE. Lessons studyContent save is one transaction with the lesson row locked (`FOR UPDATE`). A foreign, duplicated, or non-question `questionBlockId` now rolls back with a 400 instead of a raw 500 after partial writes, and a removed station's question block is deleted unless readers answered it. New blocks always take `max(order)+1` or a dense rewrite, so older lessons with gaps in their order can't tie. Lesson reorder gets the same ownership checks.
  - **Pre-existing bugs fixed**:
    1. Caption uploads always failed: `text/vtt` was sent to the image-only upload endpoint. There's a new `/api/admin/captions-upload-url`.
    2. Deleting an answered question block hit a FK error (500). It's now a 409 with a message.
    3. The reader lesson page didn't check that a lesson belonged to the study in its URL, so anyone entitled to one study could read another study's lessons by URL. It now also hides unpublished lessons and studies from non-admins.
    4. Editing a devotional day in the admin rich-text editor collapsed its reader layout. `parseDevotionalHtml` split only on newlines, and Tiptap writes none. It now normalizes block boundaries and `<h2>`s, and returns plain text, so inline tags and entities no longer print literally.
    5. A devotional day's blocks beyond its first text block never reached readers. They now render after the day, and the builder says so. Its "Question to Sit With" is pinned to the lowest-ordered question block, so an added question can't take over that slot or the reader's saved answer. Bullet lists and quotes now become paragraphs instead of being dropped.
    6. dnd-kit's screen-reader announcements read out raw UUIDs. The likely-related hydration mismatch is gone too, via stable `DndContext` ids.
  - **Admin preview**: `getStudyAccess()` in `lib/entitlements/check.ts` (entitlement OR admin role) gates the reader pages, `/api/progress`, `/api/responses`, `/api/stream/sign-url`. A draft-preview banner shows to admins.
  - **Review**: an independent security and code-quality review (a separate agent reading the diff cold) found no Critical issues. It found 1 High, 5 Medium, and several Low issues; all fixed except one deferred Medium:
    - High: a station's question-block id could diverge between client and server when a second save was queued during the first, leaving every later save failing.
    - Medium: ties in block order on lessons with gaps.
    - Medium: an added question block hijacking a devotional day's answer slot.
    - Medium: bullet lists dropped from devotional days.
    - Low: lesson list remounting and dropping focus on a publish toggle; an admin's own preview answers blocking a block delete; a reorder rollback that could drop a just-created block; concurrent studyContent saves (row lock added); client-chosen caption keys (now server-generated).

The `<track>` file is on the R2 host, cross-origin to the app, and `<video>` has no `crossorigin` attribute. Fixing it means setting `crossOrigin="anonymous"` *and* configuring CORS on the R2 bucket, and setting the attribute without confirming Stream/linked-host CORS could break playback entirely, so it wasn't changed blind.

A production-build console check of both builder pages showed no errors or hydration warnings. Migration 0011 applied to the **local test DB only**.

- **2026-11-10 — rebuilt the cover-PDF parser around the two real KDP trim sizes' exact border geometry, and made every cover view take the cover's own shape instead of a fixed box.** Requested after a review of how cover parsing works ("make sure the approach is sound, uniform, will not break"), with exact geometry supplied from the design-system chat where the covers are built: 6x9 and 8.5x11 trims, and each trim's gap from the trim line to the border's outer gold line (6x9: fore-edge 0.30in, spine 0.43in, top/bottom 0.475in; 8.5x11: 0.475 / 0.60 / 0.605in). The box is deliberately off-center left-to-right in every source PDF (the spine-side gap is wider by exactly the 0.125in bleed, a quirk of the design-system build script) -- irrelevant to the web crops, since they're drawn around the box itself.
  - **Parser (`lib/images/bookCover.ts`)**: new primary path, `trimGeometryBounds()`. The page height identifies the trim (9.25in = 6x9, 11.25in = 8.5x11, trim + 2 × 0.125in bleed), the embedded image's pixels-per-inch maps the known gaps onto exact pixels (front panel measured from the page's right edge, so the page-count-dependent spine width never enters the math), each edge snaps onto the real line found in the art when it's within 0.08in (guarding against rounding in the spec figures), and the crop is the outer line plus exactly `DISPLAY_MARGIN_IN` (0.15in) on all four sides -- so every cover of a trim comes out the same shape (6x9 ≈ 0.667, 8.5x11 ≈ 0.765) with the same physical margin, even if its border were too low-contrast to detect. Requires the image to span the full page (same ppi on both axes, within 0.5%); anything else -- an unknown page size, or a partial image -- falls back to the previous detection-only logic (`detectedBorderBounds()`, unchanged behavior), then to the plain midpoint split. `splitBookCoverPdf()` now also returns `method` and `trim` for diagnostics.
  - **Verified against all 7 real stored source PDFs** (read-only, before anything was written): the 4 Parts (8.5x11, 300ppi) and Colossians/Psalms (6x9) all take the trim-geometry path; every crop measured, in its actual pixels, exactly 0.15in of margin outside the outer gold line on every side (45px at 300ppi, 23px at 150ppi, ±1px). **Revive Me's PDF is not a standard KDP page** (14.29 × 10.42in -- looks like a 6x9 cover scaled ~1.13) so it stays on the detection path (margins within 1-2px of each other); re-exporting it at the standard page size would bring it onto the exact path.
  - **Display, per the user's explicit choice of "shape per trim" over a uniform tile (2026-11-10)**: `CoverThumbnail` (homepage + `/books` grids), `CoverGallery` (detail page), and the bundle page's "What's included" thumbnails now size their box from the cover's stored `imageWidth`/`imageHeight` (new shared `lib/images/coverAspectRatio.ts`, 2:3 fallback) instead of a fixed 3:4/square box -- the old 3:4 tile was cropping roughly 5% off the top and bottom of every 6x9 cover, more than its margin, i.e. through the border lines. Tiles in one row can now differ in height (both grids are `items-start`). `LibraryCard` (study covers have no stored dimensions) renders the cover at its natural height instead of a square crop; admin list thumbnails and the Product/Study form previews also show the whole cover rather than a square crop. Verified in local dev against the real data: every storefront tile's box matches its image's own ratio to within 0.1%. LibraryCard and admin views weren't loaded in-browser (need a login) -- verified by code review only.
  - **`scripts/reprocess-book-covers.ts` rewritten**: dry run by default (`--apply` to write); uploads new crops to FRESH R2 keys and repoints `products.imageUrl`/`backImageUrl` plus their stored dimensions, and each linked study's `coverImageUrl` -- rather than overwriting the old keys in place, since every cover is served with an immutable year-long Cache-Control (2026-11-09 backfill) and an in-place overwrite would leave stale crops in browsers that already cached them. Old objects are left in R2, unreferenced. Dry run against the real project: 7 product covers + 6 linked study covers mapped correctly.
  - **Found, not fixed (needs a decision):** the admin cover-PDF upload routes cap the embedded image at 6 megapixels (`MAX_PIXELS`, sized for Cloudflare Workers' 128MB-per-isolate memory ceiling). Real design-system covers are 300ppi -- an 8.5x11 full wrap is ~17.8MP, the Psalms 6x9 ~11MP -- so **a fresh upload of any 300ppi cover through the admin UI is rejected as "too high-resolution"** before the parser runs. Existing covers got in via scripts. Options: export a 150ppi web copy of each cover, or move the split into the admin's browser (pdf.js + canvas, uploading the two JPEGs).
  - 7 new tests in `lib/images/bookCover.test.ts` (both trims: exact equal margins and ratio; snapping onto art 2px off spec -- confirmed to fail with snapping disabled; identical crop shape with no detectable border; no spine bleed at 0.1/0.5/1.5in spine widths; fallback when the image doesn't span the page), plus `method` assertions on the existing detection-path test. 16/16 pass. `npm test`: 413 pass; the 7 failures are all the concurrently-in-progress builder work's unmigrated `content_blocks.media_url` column, not this change. `tsc`/lint clean on every file touched here (the one remaining `tsc` error is that builder work's missing `./panels` module).
  - **Applied and deployed the same day, on the user's go-ahead.** `reprocess-book-covers.ts --apply` ran against the real project: 7 products + 6 linked studies repointed, 0 failures; every new URL confirmed 200 with the immutable Cache-Control and the stored dimensions matching the new crops. Deployed (Version `b045bf09`) from a separate clean git worktree of `80a2b65` plus only this change and the already-live checkout fix (entry below, committed alongside it here) -- the main checkout held other sessions' uncommitted, unmigrated work (`content_blocks.media_url`, which doesn't exist in the real database), which a normal build there would have shipped. Same safe placeholder-env procedure otherwise (real `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*`/`R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, staging-split vars baked in -- confirmed in the bundle -- `--keep-vars`); the real `.env.local` was never touched. Gotcha for next time: a worktree's `node_modules` must be a real copy (`cp -c -R`, an APFS clone, takes seconds), not a symlink -- Turbopack refuses to resolve outside its root. Confirmed live via the ungated `.workers.dev` URL: `/`, `/books`, a 6x9 and a bundle detail page, `/blog`, `/checkout` all 200; `/library` 307s a guest to `/login`; every `/books` tile's box matches its new cover image's own ratio within 0.1%; both staging hosts still Access-gated.

Binding (Softback/Hardback) and the format-first wording ("Book & Digital - Study, Softback") are gone from the eyebrow by the request's own complete formula; `productFormatLabel` and the `bookBinding` argument were removed as dead code (binding still shows on the admin products list). Added to `studySchema`/`studyImportFieldsSchema`, `StudyForm`, and `ImportStudyForm`. A book-kind study imports and renders like a devotional's plain reading path minus the devotional app shell: `isDevotional`/`kind === "study"` checks already exclude it. "Study" : "Devotional"` ternaries (admin studies list, My Library card, `/studies/[slug]`), which would otherwise have shown a book-kind study as "Devotional". `components/admin/builder/` (another session's in-progress work) was left untouched.

Unknown to this session, the cover-upload session had deployed `8bdccc36` three minutes earlier from `ce89d62` + `ab7a880`, which deliberately left `1db4ffb` out. So each build lacked the other's change, and this deploy reverted the browser cover-upload change on staging (`/api/admin/cover-upload-urls` 404, the old `/api/admin/products/cover-pdf` back). Caught by checking `wrangler deployments list` before calling the deploy done. After confirming nothing schema/migration/env-related changed between them, redeployed `main` HEAD `24a0a01`, which holds both (Version `a80ee556`, 15:44Z), and messaged both other active sessions to hold their deploys first. Same safe procedure both times: a clean `git worktree` with a `cp -c -R` node_modules, a placeholder `.env.local` built in the worktree (real `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*`/`R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, the staging-split vars, dummy Stripe/Resend; the real `.env.local` never touched), a scripted check that no real secret appears in `next-env.mjs`, and `--keep-vars`. Confirmed live via `.workers.dev`: `/`, `/books`, `/blog`, `/checkout` 200; `/library`, `/admin/products` 307; `POST /api/admin/cover-upload-urls` 403, the old `cover-pdf` route 404; nav "Store"; eyebrows as listed above; cross-domain links absolute on `staging.souldustrevival.com`; both staging hosts still Access-gated. **Lesson: with several sessions able to deploy the same Worker, build from current `main` HEAD (or later) rather than a single commit, and run `wrangler deployments list` right before deploying to see what's actually live.**

- **2026-11-09 (truly the latest) — fixed checkout wrongly telling an already-logged-in visitor to sign up, found live on staging.** Reported directly with a screenshot: the nav's own Profile dropdown (Account/Admin/Log out) proved the visitor was logged in, but `/checkout` still showed "Your cart includes digital content, which requires an account" / "Sign up to continue." Root cause: `app/(marketing)/checkout/page.tsx` was a pure `"use client"` component that re-checked login status itself, independently of the server, via its own `useEffect` calling `createSupabaseBrowserClient().auth.getUser()` after mount — a second, separate network round trip from the one that had already proven the session valid moments earlier when the nav's `SiteHeader` server-rendered (`getSession()`, same underlying `getUser()` call). The two can disagree: Supabase rotates refresh tokens on each use, and `proxy.ts`'s own middleware already refreshes the session once per request — a client-side refresh attempt firing right after can find that same refresh token already consumed and conclude (wrongly) that there's no session at all, even though the page the browser is looking at was just rendered by a server that very much had one.
  - **Fix**: converted `checkout/page.tsx` into an async Server Component that calls the same `getSession()` the nav already trusts, and passes the result down as `initialLoggedIn` to the renamed `CheckoutPageClient.tsx` — no second client-side auth check at all, removing the race structurally rather than papering over one specific failure mode of it.
