This matches the pattern `/account` already uses for the identical reason (2026-11-07's fix #5/#6 entry, further down this file). `checkout/layout.tsx`'s stale comment (claimed `page.tsx` was still the client file carrying no route config) was corrected to match.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (406, unchanged — this is a structural move of an existing check from client to server, not new testable logic) both clean; the build confirms `/checkout` still renders dynamically (`ƒ`), so the nonce-based CSP is unaffected.
  - **Not independently reproduced live** — `staging.souldustrevival.com` is fully Cloudflare-Access-gated (confirmed via a plain `curl`, which just gets bounced to the Access login), so this session has no way to drive a real authenticated browser session there; the fix is verified by code reading the actual mechanism (the two independent `getUser()` calls, now collapsed to one) rather than by reproducing the exact race.
  - **Deployed** the same session, on the user's go-ahead, via the documented safe build procedure (placeholder `.env.local` with the staging-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars kept at their real standing values per BUILD_TASKS.md, `--keep-vars`); the real `.env.local` was restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live via the Worker's own `.workers.dev` URL (`/`, `/books`, `/blog`, `/checkout` all 200) and confirmed the real `staging.souldustrevival.com` domain is still correctly Access-gated and unaffected. **Confirmed fixed by the user in their own authenticated browser** — checkout with a digital item in the cart no longer prompts an already-logged-in visitor to sign up.
- **2026-11-09 (latest of all) — rewrote the storefront eyebrow label (homepage featured grid, `/books` listing, a product/bundle's own detail page) around one shared, consistent rule instead of three hand-duplicated, pre-`kind`-field ternaries.** Reported directly against live screenshots: "the eyebrow names are off and need updating," with the rule spelled out — Book / Digital / Book & Digital by format, Study / Devotional by kind — then, mid-build, clarified for bundles specifically ("if it is a Bundle display Bundle - Study & Digital for example").
  - **Root cause**: the three pages' eyebrow ternaries predate `products.kind` (added 2026-11-09, "even newer" entry below) and still hardcoded the word "Study" for a digital-granting format regardless of the product's actual kind — a plain `kind: "book"` product with `format: "both"` showed "Book & Study" ("Revive Me" in the reported screenshot), and `productKindBindingSuffix()`'s own kind suffix then piled a second, often-duplicate kind word on top (a real Study product showed "Book & Study - Study"). The homepage's copy of this ternary also had no bundle branch at all, so a bundle like "The Journey" fell through to the same mislabeled generic wording.
  - **Fix**: replaced `productFormatLabel`'s callers and the old `productKindBindingSuffix` with one new `productEyebrowLabel({ type, kind, format, bookBinding })` (`lib/commerce/productEyebrow.ts`) used identically by all three pages. Non-bundle: `"{Book|Digital|Book & Digital}"`, with `" - {Study|Devotional}, {Softback|Hardback}"` appended only for whatever of kind/binding is actually set (never "Book" as a kind, matching the existing design rule). Bundle: `"Bundle - {kind} & {format label}"` (kind omitted the same way; the format label never is), e.g. `"Bundle - Study & Digital"` per the clarified example, or plain `"Bundle - Book"` for a bundle with no kind set — this also incidentally fixes the homepage's missing bundle branch.
  - 14 new tests in a first-ever `lib/commerce/productEyebrow.test.ts`. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (406, 14 new) both clean. Verified live against local dev (pointed at the real connected database): homepage, `/books`, and `The Journey`'s own bundle detail page all now read correctly — `"Book & Digital - Hardback"` (Revive Me), `"Bundle - Book & Digital"` (The Journey bundle), `"Book & Digital - Study, Softback"` (Colossians), `"Book & Digital - Devotional, Softback"` (the Psalms devotional), and `"Book - Study, Softback"` for each physical-only Journey part.
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure (placeholder `.env.local` with the staging-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars kept at their real standing values per BUILD_TASKS.md, `--keep-vars`); the real `.env.local` was restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live via the Browser pane against the real `staging.souldustrevival.com` homepage, `/books`, and `The Journey`'s bundle detail page (all showing the corrected eyebrow), and confirmed the marketing/app subdomain split wasn't regressed (`/library` still redirects to `app.staging.souldustrevival.com`).

- **2026-11-09 (after all of the above) — rebuilt the book-cover PDF parser's margin logic to equalize the margin on all four sides of each panel's own border box, not just avoid spine bleed; found and fixed a second real bug this surfaced along the way.** Reported live, with screenshots: "the pdf cover parser is still failing to create the cover images correctly" (the homepage's "Revive Me" tile showed the back cover's rotated spine title text bleeding down the left edge of the front crop), followed by an explicit follow-up requirement: "I want to be absolutely sure that moving forward all cover pdfs will be processed against an equalized margin outside the double-line border top, bottom, left and right so that the double-line border rectangle is centralized in the display windows."
  - **First bug, found by diagnosing the reported screenshot directly against the real stored source PDF**: the 2026-11-09 ("even later still") fix's spine-side cushion was correct in principle but this one real cover ("Revive Me") has an outer trim margin (154px, 7.2% of width) nearly double the others already reprocessed — mirroring/extending past the border by that cushion still worked out fine on every other real cover (all had small, single-digit-percent margins) but, combined with this one's comparatively wide margin, the crop boundary landed well inside the spine gap, right where the spine's own rotated title text sits (confirmed via a pixel-brightness profile of the real source image). Root cause wasn't a one-off edge case to patch around — it was the whole mirroring approach never having a real guarantee against reaching into the spine at all.
  - **Real fix, per the explicit requirement**: rebuilt `lib/images/bookCover.ts` around a genuine 4-sided equalization. New `findHorizontalBorderClusters()` (the dual of the existing `findBorderClusters`, now renamed `findVerticalBorderClusters`, with the shared clustering core factored into `findBrightClusters()`) detects each half's own top/bottom border stroke, scoped to sampling columns strictly inside that half's own already-detected left/right border interior (so a column can never land in the spine or the other half's content). New `equalizeHalfBorder()` computes the SMALLEST of all four natural margins (trim-side, top, bottom, and a spine-side budget) and applies that one value uniformly to every side — cropping away any side's excess margin rather than preserving whatever asymmetric margin the source PDF happened to have, so the border box always reads as genuinely centered. Falls back to the prior fix's behavior (spine-side cushion only, full height) when a half's horizontal border can't be reliably detected, and to the original plain midpoint split when its vertical border can't be found at all — both already-tested safety nets, unchanged.
  - **Second bug, caught before shipping, not after**: the first version of this rebuild used "half the real spine gap" as the spine-side margin BUDGET rather than just a safety CEILING — since `Math.min()` picks the smallest of the four margins, and the real spine gap is often much wider than a small cushion needs to be, this let the equalized margin balloon back out to deep inside the spine on the exact same real cover that motivated the fix, reintroducing the identical text-bleed bug through a different path (confirmed via debug instrumentation showing the back/front crop boundaries landing 1px apart, almost exactly at the spine text's own center). Fixed by keeping the spine-side budget capped at the existing small, fixed cushion (`INNER_BORDER_CUSHION_FRACTION`, ~1% of width) as the default, with half the real gap used only as a last-resort ceiling for the rare case where the spine itself is narrower than that cushion — never as the value being equalized toward.
  - 9 tests in `lib/images/bookCover.test.ts` (1 existing test's expectations updated for the new cushion-based math — all other existing tests were unaffected, confirming the fallback paths preserve prior behavior exactly; 1 new regression test for the 4-sided equalization itself, using a synthetic cover with a genuine closed border rectangle and deliberately unequal natural margins on every side, asserting all four measured margins come out equal and match the smallest natural one, not an average or the largest). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (390, 1 new) both clean.
  - **Reprocessed all 7 real covers twice** (once after the first version of this fix, again after the spine-budget correction) via `scripts/reprocess-book-covers.ts` against the real connected Supabase project and R2 bucket — verified visually after each pass by fetching the real source PDFs and running the parser directly (not just trusting the live site), and the final pass was confirmed live on `staging.souldustrevival.com/books` and the homepage: every cover's border box now sits with visibly equal margins on all four sides, no spine bleed on "Revive Me," no regressions on the other 6.
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure (placeholder `.env.local` with the staging-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars kept at their real standing values per BUILD_TASKS.md, `--keep-vars`); the real `.env.local` was restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live via the Browser pane against the real `staging.souldustrevival.com/books` and homepage.

- **2026-11-09 (one more still after all of the above) — switched the uniform-tile fix's letterboxing (`object-contain`) to crop-to-fill (`object-cover`), after the user asked why covers were now "sitting on a background."** The previous entry's fixed-ratio box kept every tile uniform and every cover fully uncropped, but since real covers don't all share that box's exact ratio, the box's own background color showed through as visible padding around whichever covers didn't match it — read as an unwanted background, not a deliberate design choice. Asked the user directly which of the three mutually-exclusive properties (uniform size / zero cropping / no visible background) to give up, since a fixed fallback ratio can't hold all three at once unless every cover shares one exact shape; chosen: crop-to-fill, no background. `CoverThumbnail` now uses `object-cover` again — safe this time, unlike the box's original 2026-11-09 incarnation, since each cover's own border is already centered with an equal margin on every side (the very first fix in this whole chain), so a small crop here only ever trims a bit of that margin, never content or the spine.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (406, unchanged) both clean. **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure; `.env.local` restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live (via the ungated `.workers.dev` URL, since this session's Cloudflare Access session had expired) on both the homepage and `/books`: tiles are uniform, edge-to-edge, no background visible.

- **2026-11-09 (one more after all of the above) — restored a uniform tile size across the cover grids, after the previous entry's "render each cover at its own natural size" fix made every tile a different height.** Reported directly, with a live screenshot: "why are the display tiles different sizes. They should all be uniform in tile size." Rendering every cover at its own true aspect ratio (no shared box at all) did fix the cropping bug, but traded it for an uneven grid — a real, equally-unwanted regression, not something to leave as "working as intended."
  - **Fix: back to one shared fixed-size box per tile, but `object-contain` instead of `object-cover`.** `CoverThumbnail` now wraps every cover in a `3/4`-ratio box (`position: relative`, `aspect-ratio` via inline style) with `next/image`'s `fill` + `object-contain` — the image is letterboxed to fit inside the shared box rather than either cropped (the original bug) or freely sized (this entry's regression), so every tile in a row lines up to the same size while the full cover still always shows, uncropped. `imageWidth`/`imageHeight` are no longer read by this component at all (`fill`+`object-contain` doesn't need them to avoid distortion -- the browser's own decoded image drives the contain-fit, not any stored attribute), so both call sites (`app/(marketing)/page.tsx`, `app/(marketing)/books/page.tsx`) stopped passing them. The stored dimensions/migration/capture code from the previous entry is left in place regardless -- harmless, already deployed, and still potentially useful elsewhere later -- just no longer load-bearing for this specific display.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (406, unchanged -- pure markup/CSS, no new test-worthy logic) both clean.
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure; `.env.local` restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live on both `staging.souldustrevival.com/books` and the homepage: every row of tiles is now uniform in size again, with each cover still shown in full inside its own tile.

- **2026-11-09 (truly after all of the above) — stopped force-cropping product cover tiles into a fixed-ratio box, which had been re-introducing asymmetric cropping right on top of the border-centering fix just above.** Reported with two reference screenshots ("north star" images) after the prior fix was already live: "things are still off" — the homepage's Featured content grid and the `/books` listing grid both still rendered every cover through a fixed `aspect-[3/4]` + `object-cover` box, which crops *some* amount off any image whose own ratio doesn't match 0.75 — confirmed every real cover's actual ratio varies (0.665–0.674 for three covers, 0.766–0.767 for four others), so the box was always cropping someone's cover regardless of how well-centered the border was inside the source file itself.
  - **Real fix: store each cover's own true pixel dimensions, render uncropped at that ratio.** New `products.imageWidth`/`imageHeight`/`backImageWidth`/`backImageHeight` columns (migration `drizzle/0010_aromatic_lyja.sql`, additive/nullable). `splitBookCoverPdf()` now also returns each crop's real width/height (already known from the Photon crop objects, no extra decode needed) — the product cover-PDF route reports them in its response. The plain (non-PDF-split) image upload path, used by both `ProductForm`'s generic product-image field and `BundleForm`'s master-cover field, has no server-side decode step at all (the file goes straight to R2 via a presigned PUT), so new `lib/images/readImageDimensions.ts` reads the real dimensions **client-side** (a throwaway object URL + the browser's own image decoder) right before upload, with a safe `.catch(() => null)` fallback if the read ever fails.
  - **New shared `components/commerce/CoverThumbnail.tsx`** replaces two independent, identically-broken copies of the cropped-box markup (the homepage's Featured content grid and the `/books` listing grid) — renders the cover via `next/image` with its real `width`/`height` props (not `fill`), `className="w-full h-auto"` so it scales responsively while preserving its exact true ratio, wrapped in `rounded-xl overflow-hidden shadow-lg` to match the user's reference images exactly (full image, soft rounded corners, drop shadow, no letterboxing). Falls back to a plain `aspect-[2/3]` placeholder box only when a product has no image at all; a product with an image but no *stored dimensions yet* (nothing currently, see backfill below) falls back to a reasonable default ratio (2:3) rather than breaking the layout.
  - **Backfilled all 8 real active products** via a temporary script (deleted after use): re-ran `splitBookCoverPdf()` against each of the 7 book-cover-PDF products' already-stored source PDF to read the exact crop dimensions (no re-upload needed), and decoded "The Journey" bundle's flat PNG cover directly for its own width/height. Dry-run checked first; confirmed via direct query afterward that all 8 real products now carry correct, non-null dimensions.
  - 2 new tests (`route.test.ts`'s image-dimension persistence cases) plus updated assertions in the cover-PDF route's own test (now asserting the response includes real, positive width/height for both crops). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (392, 2 new) both clean.
  - **Migration applied to the real connected Supabase project** (approved on request after the sandbox's "Production Deploy" classifier blocked the first attempt, same recurring pattern as every other migration this project has needed) and to the local test database (applied directly via `psql`, with a matching `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` tracking row inserted the same way prior local-only migrations have been, so local test runs and the real project stay in sync).
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure (placeholder `.env.local` with the staging-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars kept at their real standing values, `--keep-vars`); the real `.env.local` was restored and hash-confirmed identical immediately after. Confirmed live on both `staging.souldustrevival.com/books` and the homepage: every cover now renders uncropped at its own true shape, with rounded corners and a drop shadow, matching the user's two reference screenshots exactly — including the visibly different heights across one row, since each manuscript's own trim ratio differs slightly and nothing forces them into a uniform box anymore.

- **2026-11-09 (even later) — added a per-product "Kind" (Study/Devotional/Book) and a physical book's Binding (Softback/Hardback), shown in the storefront eyebrow.** Requested directly: Studies already have a Kind selector at creation, but Products don't, and there was no way to tell a Study from a Devotional (or either from a plain book with no interactions at all) from the storefront, or to say whether a printed book is soft- or hardback.
  - **`products.kind`** (`"study" | "devotional" | "book"`, NOT NULL, default `"book"`) is deliberately its own admin-set field on the product, not derived/joined from a linked study's own `kind` — a product can exist with no linked study at all (the new `"book"` value means exactly that: no interactions, nothing to read in the app), and a product shouldn't silently lose its displayed kind if its study link ever changes. **`products.bookBinding`** (`"softback" | "hardback"`, nullable) only applies to a physical copy — `productSchema` rejects it on a purely digital product via the same `.refine()` pattern as `digitalPriceCents`.
  - **`ProductForm.tsx`** gained a Kind radio group (mirroring `StudyForm`'s own copy/style, with the new "Book (no interactions)" option added) and a Binding select, shown only inside the existing "Book Purchase Options" fieldset (so it's never asked for a purely digital product).
  - **Eyebrow**: new shared `lib/commerce/productEyebrow.ts` (`productKindBindingSuffix()`) appends `" - Study"` / `" - Devotional"` / `", Softback"` / `", Hardback"` to the *existing* format-based eyebrow text on both the `/books` listing and a product's own detail page, separated with `-` per the request. Deliberately omits `"Book"` itself when there's no binding set either — a plain book with nothing new specified yet keeps today's exact eyebrow text completely unchanged, rather than showing a redundant "- Book" next to wording that already says "Book". Also added a Study/Devotional badge (reusing the Study list's own accessible-contrast tokens exactly) and a Binding mention to the admin products list, matching the Study list's existing Kind-badge precedent.
  - 10 new tests (`lib/validation/schemas.test.ts`'s new kind/bookBinding cases; `app/api/admin/products/route.test.ts`'s new describe block covering create/update persistence and the digital+bookBinding rejection). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (388, 10 new) both clean.
  - **Migration `drizzle/0009_superb_master_chief.sql`** (two new nullable/defaulted columns, nothing destructive) applied to both the local test database and, on the user's explicit retry request, the real connected Supabase project (refused once by this session's own sandbox auto-classifier as a "Production Deploy" action before the retry succeeded).
  - **Backfilled the real data, not just the bare `DEFAULT 'book'`**: ran `_backfill-product-kind.mjs` (dry-run checked first) against the real database -- of the 6 real non-bundle products, 5 with a linked Study now read `kind: "study"` and the Psalms product now reads `kind: "devotional"`, matching each one's actual linked study exactly. `bookBinding` has no equivalent backfill possible -- nothing recorded anywhere, previously, says whether an already-printed copy is soft- or hardback, so every existing product starts unset (confirmed: all 6 show `book_binding: null`) and needs the admin to set it by hand going forward. Temporary backfill script deleted after use.
  - **Deployed, then found and fixed a real gap the user caught live**: the homepage's own "Featured content" section had a *third*, separate copy of this same format-based eyebrow ternary (`app/(marketing)/page.tsx`) that the first deploy never touched -- only the `/books` listing and a product's own detail page had been updated. Grepped the whole app for every remaining copy of the eyebrow text before fixing it this time (confirmed exactly these 3 files, all now fixed) rather than assuming two was the full set. Redeployed and verified live: the homepage now correctly shows "BOOK & STUDY - STUDY" (Colossians) and "BOOK & STUDY - DEVOTIONAL" (Psalms), matching the listing/detail pages. The Journey bundle correctly shows no kind suffix, since bundles are excluded by design.

- **2026-11-09 (later still) — found and fixed a self-inflicted regression: the marketing/app subdomain split on staging had silently gone back to single-domain behavior.** Reported directly: "when I select the App nav-bar menu item it does not route to app.staging.souldustrevival.com but rather to staging.souldustrevival.com/library why is that?" Not a routing bug — `lib/domains.ts`'s `appUrl()` only returns an absolute cross-domain URL once `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_HOSTNAME` is baked in at build time; left unset, it returns the plain path unchanged, which stays on whatever domain served the page. The split was built, deployed, and verified working end-to-end on staging 2026-11-16 -- but every deploy made *this session* for unrelated fixes (the R2 cache-control fix, the study-reimport feature, the Saved-indicator fix) used this project's own documented "safe" placeholder-`.env.local` build procedure, which correctly keeps real secrets out but -- not having been told this one project has a standing exception -- also left the three subdomain-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars unset each time, silently reverting a previously-working feature with no error anywhere to flag it.
  - **Fix: redeployed with `NEXT_PUBLIC_MARKETING_HOSTNAME=staging.souldustrevival.com`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_HOSTNAME=app.staging.souldustrevival.com`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_COOKIE_DOMAIN=.staging.souldustrevival.com`** all correctly set at build time, same safe-deploy procedure otherwise. Confirmed the Cloudflare-side custom domains were still intact first (`staging.souldustrevival.com`/`app.staging.souldustrevival.com` both still resolve, both still Access-gated) before redeploying. Verified via the Browser pane (which already had a live Access session): `staging.souldustrevival.com/library` now correctly redirects to `app.staging.souldustrevival.com`'s own `/login`, and `app.staging.souldustrevival.com/` correctly redirects back to the marketing host -- both directions confirmed, plus `marketingUrl()`-generated absolute links on the homepage confirming the split is genuinely active again, not just the proxy redirect.
  - **To stop this from recurring**: updated BUILD_TASKS.md's main "4. Deployment" safe-order checklist (not just the separate, now-stale-sounding "testing on staging" section further down) to list these three vars alongside the other build-time-must-be-real exceptions, and reframed that section's own heading/intro from "testing... first" to "now standing config" -- staging's split isn't a one-off experiment anymore, and the deploy checklist needed to say so plainly rather than reading like it still was.
  - No code changed -- this was purely a build-time configuration fix, so nothing to commit for this entry's own fix (the BUILD_TASKS.md documentation update is the only file change, committed alongside this entry).

- **2026-11-09 (truly latest) — gave every admin Save button a real "Saved" confirmation.** Reported directly: "there is a Save button but not an actual Saved state to know a Product or a study actually got saved as part of the admin experience." Investigated and found the real cause: `ProductForm`/`StudyForm`/`BundleForm`/`BlogPostForm` all unconditionally navigated away on a successful edit (`StudyForm`/`BlogPostForm` to the exact same URL they were already on -- a no-op navigation with nothing for the admin to see; `ProductForm`/`BundleForm` to their own list page) -- the Save button's only visible state was a brief "Saving..." flash, then nothing. `LeaderResourceForm.tsx` already had a real "Saved" confirmation (its own autosave-on-blur pattern), the only one of the five that did.
  - **Fix**: extracted `LeaderResourceForm`'s own inline "show Saved for 2 seconds" logic into a shared `lib/hooks/useSavedIndicator.ts` (`{ saved, markSaved }`), and switched all five admin forms onto it. For the four explicit-Save-button forms, *editing an existing record* now stays on the same page (no navigation at all) and calls `markSaved()` + `router.refresh()`, showing a `role="status" aria-live="polite"` "Saved" message next to the Save button -- *creating* a new record is unchanged (still navigates to the new record's own page/the list, which is itself a real, already-visible "it worked" signal a brand-new record doesn't otherwise have).
  - **Scope note**: the user named Product and Study specifically; Bundle and Blog share the exact identical gap (same copy-pasted navigate-away-on-save pattern) and were fixed in the same pass rather than left for a future session to rediscover as new, since the shared hook made it a one-line change per form once written.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (378, unchanged -- this is UI/interaction logic over already-tested save endpoints, no new persisted behavior, and this codebase has no component-testing precedent for a React hook like `useSavedIndicator` to extend) both clean. **Not verified end-to-end in a real admin session** -- confirmed via code review and a clean typecheck/build/local dev-server console check (no admin session available in this environment; the established temporary-`requireAdmin()`-bypass workaround has been separately refused by this session's sandbox on past attempts, so it wasn't attempted again here).
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure, on the user's explicit retry request (the first attempt this same session had been refused outright by the sandbox as a "Production Deploy" action; the retry went through cleanly). Confirmed live: `/`, `/books` 200, `/admin/products` 307s for a guest as expected.

- **2026-11-09 (latest) — built the missing ability to re-upload a study's materials document after it's already been created.** Reported directly: "I am noticing that I am not able to re-upload a materials pdf after a study is initially created and saved." Investigated and confirmed this was a genuinely missing feature, not a bug — `/admin/studies/[id]` only ever showed the originally-imported file as a read-only link; the only existing "re-parse from a document" path was `app/api/admin/studies/import/route.ts`, which always creates a brand-new study, and the only "re-process an existing study" path was `scripts/reprocess-study-lessons.ts`, a one-off CLI script that re-parses the *same already-stored* source, not a new upload.
  - **New `POST /api/admin/studies/reimport`** (`studyReimportFieldsSchema`: just `studyId` — title/slug/productId/kind are unchanged, since those stay under the study's own normal metadata edit) takes a fresh .docx/.pdf, parses it the same way the create-path importer does, and replaces the study's lessons wholesale: deletes every existing lesson's `responses` → `lesson_progress` → `content_blocks` → the lessons themselves (same dependency order the study DELETE route already uses, inside one transaction), then inserts the freshly-parsed lessons as drafts and sets the study itself back to `published: false` — mirroring the create-path importer's own "review before publishing" rule, since a reimport is exactly as unreviewed as a fresh import. **Refuses with 409** under the exact same condition the study DELETE route already refuses a delete — the linked product having real order or entitlement history — since replacing the lessons out from under an already-sold study would silently orphan a paying customer's saved progress/answers. A wholesale replace (not a title-matched merge, unlike the reprocess script) was the deliberate choice: a genuinely revised manuscript can freely add, remove, rename, or reorder sections, so there's nothing reliable to match the old lessons against the way "re-parse the identical source with a fixed parser" has.
  - **New `components/admin/ReimportStudyForm.tsx`**, added to `/admin/studies/[id]` above the existing metadata form: a file picker that asks for a native `confirm()` (same pattern as `PublishAllLessonsButton`) naming exactly how many existing lessons will be replaced before anything happens, then shows the same indeterminate-progress treatment `ImportStudyForm` already uses for a long parse.
  - **Refactor, found while writing this**: the create-path importer's own ~30-line "parse by file type and study kind" branch and its "turn parsed blocks into content_block insert values" logic would otherwise have been copy-pasted verbatim into the new route. Extracted both into a new shared `lib/import/parseStudyDocument.ts` (`parseStudyDocument()`, `toContentBlockInserts()`) and switched the existing import route over to it too, so the two routes' parsing/sanitization rules can't drift apart from each other.
  - 6 new tests in a first-ever `app/api/admin/studies/reimport/route.test.ts` (admin-only; replaces lessons/content blocks and resets publish state; the old lesson's content block/progress/response rows are gone, not orphaned; the two 409 order/entitlement guards; an unsupported file type; a nonexistent study). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (378, 6 new) both clean.
  - **Not yet verified in the real admin UI** — no real admin session is available in this environment (the established "temporary/reverted `requireAdmin()` bypass" pattern this project has used before is a security-weakening action this session's sandbox has separately refused on past attempts, so it wasn't attempted again here); verified instead via the route's own test coverage against the real local test database, plus a clean typecheck/build.
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure, on the user's explicit retry request (the first attempt this same session had been refused outright by the sandbox as a "Production Deploy" action; the retry went through cleanly). Confirmed live: `/`, `/books` 200, `/admin/studies` 307s for a guest as expected, and an unauthenticated `POST /api/admin/studies/reimport` correctly returns 403 rather than 404 -- the route exists and its admin gate is live, not just passing in tests.

- **2026-11-09 (newer still) — fixed a real gap in the Foreword/Preface narrowing feature (2026-11-09 entry below), and reprocessed the two real documents that exposed it.** Reported directly: "the Colossians Study and the Psalms Devotional the Intro still hold more than just the preface and foreword where they are applicable."
  - **Two separate things, not one.** (1) Both studies' stored "Introduction" lesson content predates `narrowToForewordAndPreface()` entirely (both imported 2026-11-06/07, the narrowing feature shipped 2026-11-09) — the feature only ever touches the *import* path, so it never retroactively changed what these two already had stored (same class of gap as the same-day R2 Cache-Control fix above). (2) Re-running the *current* parser directly against each study's own stored source PDF (`studies.source_file_url`) showed the Psalms devotional's Foreword→Preface narrowing is already correct once reprocessed, but Colossians' real document has a third front-matter section the narrowing function didn't know to stop at: Foreword → Preface → "BEFORE WE BEGIN" (a lead-in heading for "The Six Faces of the Dust," a longer reflective essay) → Week 1, with no heading between Preface and that essay — so the essay silently rode along as if it were still part of the Preface, same failure shape as the body-text leak the 2026-11-09 entry already fixed once, just a step later in the document.
  - **Fix:** added `"BEFORE WE BEGIN"` to the closed `FRONT_MATTER_END_HEADING` set (same pattern as `CONTENTS`/`INTRODUCTION`/etc. already there) — a line matching it now ends the kept Foreword/Preface section exactly like any other recognized front-matter label would. 2 new regression tests (one per import path) reproduce the exact real failure shape (Foreword → Preface → "Before We Begin"/"Six Faces of the Dust" → a real heading) and assert the essay is excluded. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (372, 2 new) both clean.
  - **Reprocessed both real documents' Introduction lessons** with a temporary, dry-run-first script: re-fetched each study's own stored source PDF, re-ran it through the now-fixed current parser, and replaced the single existing "Introduction" text content-block's body with the newly-narrowed HTML (confirmed both before running: exactly one text block existed, and the re-parsed result also produced exactly one, so this was a straightforward body replacement, not a block insert/delete reconciliation). Colossians: 11,403 → 5,233 chars (now ends cleanly at the close of the Preface). Psalms: 11,154 → 3,773 chars (the Universal Edition's own front matter has no "Before We Begin" essay at all, so this length drop is purely the original title-page/copyright narrowing already fixed 2026-11-09, applied for the first time). Verified directly against the database: neither block contains "Six Faces"/"Before We Begin" (Colossians) or copyright/edition boilerplate (Psalms) anymore, and both still correctly open with "FOREWORD" and close at their Preface's own natural ending. Script deleted after use.
  - **Found, not yet fixed:** "The Journey" Parts One–Four (built 2026-11-08, also predating the narrowing feature) show the same symptom — still-oversized Introduction content (5,119–11,580 chars) — but none of the four has a stored `source_file_url` (built via a direct one-off driver script against the admin's own library calls rather than the real import route, which is what normally stores it, per that entry's own "the driver script itself was deleted after use" note), so this same reprocess-from-source approach can't be used without the original manuscript PDFs again. Not requested by name this time; flagged here rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover as new.
  - **Deployed** the code fix via the documented safe build procedure; confirmed live (`/`, `/books` 200, a gated study page 307s as expected for a guest). Not yet committed.

- **2026-11-09 (even newer) — fixed the real cause of "images take a moment to load on refresh": every R2-uploaded image had no Cache-Control metadata at all, so next/image's Cloudflare Images optimizer had no "immutable" signal to pass through to the browser.** Investigated as a diagnostic request first ("is there anything to do about that?"), not a build — found via `search_cloudflare_documentation` and reading `@opennextjs/cloudflare`'s own `images.js` source directly that `/_next/image` is **already** auto-wired to `env.IMAGES` whenever the binding is configured (no custom loader, no `open-next.config.ts` override needed) — `wrangler.jsonc` already has `"images": {"binding": "IMAGES"}`. Deployed a temporary, fully-reverted diagnostic route calling `env.IMAGES.info()`/`.transform()` against a real R2 cover to confirm the binding itself works on this account with no billing error (confirmed: real metadata read back, a real transform produced real bytes) — so the optimizer was never the problem.
  - **Real root cause, found by reading `images.js`'s own logic**: the Cache-Control it sends back for an optimized image is `public, max-age=315360000, immutable` only when the *upstream* R2 response's own `Cache-Control` header contains `"immutable"` — otherwise it sends none at all. Every R2 upload path in this codebase (`lib/r2/upload.ts`'s presigned-PUT helper, and the four server-side direct uploads in `products/cover-pdf`, `studies/cover-pdf`, `studies/import`, and the `reprocess-book-covers.ts` maintenance script) wrote objects with no `CacheControl` metadata at all — confirmed by reading every `PutObjectCommand` call site. With no upstream signal, every single page load/refresh re-fetched the original from R2 and re-ran the binding transform from scratch, with the browser given no instruction to cache the result.
  - **Fix**: new `IMMUTABLE_CACHE_CONTROL = "public, max-age=31536000, immutable"` constant, in its own zero-dependency `lib/r2/cacheControl.ts` (not `lib/r2/client.ts`, which constructs an `S3Client` against server-only credentials -- putting the constant there would pull the R2 client/AWS SDK into the browser bundle the moment a client component imports it) and re-exported from `lib/r2/client.ts` for server-only callers' convenience. Applied to: (1) the four server-side direct `PutObjectCommand` calls, trivially safe since the server controls both ends; (2) `lib/r2/upload.ts`'s presigned-PUT signer, which required updating all 6 client call sites that `fetch(uploadUrl, {method: "PUT", ...})` (`ProductForm`, `BundleForm`, `LeaderResourceForm`, `BlockEditor`'s two upload functions, the social composer) to also send a matching `Cache-Control` header on the PUT itself -- `CacheControl` becomes part of the SigV4-signed request the same way `ContentType` already was, so a mismatched or missing header on the client's actual PUT would fail with a signature error, not silently succeed uncached.
  - **Deliberately excluded**: `scripts/reprocess-book-covers.ts`, the one place that overwrites an *existing* R2 key in place (to fix already-wrong bytes at an already-published URL) rather than minting a fresh UUID -- a long immutable `max-age` would make it harder for a browser that already cached the old bytes to ever see a future correction at that same URL. Left uncached there on purpose, with a comment explaining why, rather than applied blindly for consistency.
  - Every R2 object this app writes is already stored under a server-generated random UUID key and never overwritten in place elsewhere (per SECURITY.md), which is exactly what makes `immutable` semantically correct everywhere else it's now applied.
  - New `lib/r2/upload.test.ts` (first test for this module) asserts the signed command carries the Cache-Control. Updated `app/api/admin/products/cover-pdf/route.test.ts`, `studies/cover-pdf/route.test.ts`, and `studies/import/route.test.ts` (new assertions, plus their existing `@/lib/r2/client` mocks needed the new export added) to assert the uploaded objects carry it too. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (370, 4 new) both clean.
  - **Deployed** via the documented safe build procedure; confirmed live (`/`, `/books`, `/blog` 200, `/library` guest 307).
  - **Backfilled the 37 objects already in the real R2 bucket, with the user's explicit go-ahead to retry.** The code fix only touches the write path, so it couldn't retroactively change what's already stored (confirmed: a real existing book cover fetched directly had no `Cache-Control` header right after the deploy above). Wrote a one-off script (`CopyObjectCommand` with `MetadataDirective: "REPLACE"`, re-specifying each object's existing `ContentType` from a `HeadObjectCommand` first so only `CacheControl` actually changes -- metadata-only, no bytes touched, fully reversible) to set the same immutable header on all 37 already-stored objects (30 book-cover back/front/source files, 1 bundle cover, 3 study covers, 3 study source docs). First attempt was refused outright by this session's own sandbox auto-classifier as "Cloud Storage Mass Delete" (a bulk loop over many R2 keys reads as that pattern even though every call in it is a same-key copy, never a delete) -- held rather than worked around, per the tool's own instruction; the user said to try again and the retry succeeded cleanly (ran from inside the project directory this time, since the script's first location in the session's scratchpad directory couldn't resolve `@aws-sdk/client-s3` -- Node ESM resolves packages relative to the script's own path, not `cwd`). 37/37 updated, 0 failed. Verified live: the same real book cover now serves `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` with its original `Content-Type: image/jpeg` intact. Temporary script deleted immediately after, confirmed via `git status` that only the intended code changes remain.

- **2026-11-09 (newest of all) — permanently removed the "Foundations: A 6-Week Study"/"Foundations: The Companion Book" placeholder products, the "Foundations" study, and their associated order/entitlement/lesson data from the real connected Supabase project, per explicit request ("these were only ever placeholders").** Both products were already `active: false` and the study already `published: false` (someone had already deactivated them), but real test history was still attached: 1 paid $29 order + 1 entitlement under `matt.brown@souldustrevival.com`'s own account (from testing the study purchase flow), and 2 abandoned guest `test-checkout-verify@example.com` orders (from testing the checkout-hang fix, both still `pending`) — all 3 documented elsewhere in this file's own history. The existing admin DELETE routes refuse to remove a product with order/entitlement history attached (by design, see SECURITY_REVIEW.md), so this was done as a direct, transaction-wrapped SQL deletion rather than through the admin UI, in the correct dependency order: saved `responses` → `order_items` → the 3 `orders` → `entitlements` → `content_blocks` → the lesson → the study → both `products` → the "Why We Built Foundations" blog post (confirmed with the user first: keep or remove the 3 now-empty orders, and whether the blog post should go too — both "remove"). Confirmed zero rows remain for any of these by name/slug, and confirmed live on staging afterward: neither product appears on `/books` (they were already inactive, so this is unchanged there) and the blog post is gone from `/blog`'s listing. **`scripts/seed.ts` (the local-dev onboarding seed script, which creates this same placeholder content for a fresh setup per README.md's documented step 4) was deliberately left unchanged** — that's a legitimate, separate mechanism for giving a new local setup some sample content to explore, not itself "the placeholder" being complained about; the problem was this content having leaked into the real, live catalog via a real seed run, not the seed script's own existence. No code changed, no migration needed (pure data deletion), no tests affected.

- **2026-11-09 (even newer) — narrowed imported Study/Devotional Introduction lessons down to just their Foreword/Preface content, and added a "both" (physical + digital, two independent Stripe purchases) format option for bundles.** Two unrelated requests in one build: "for the app studies/devotionals/books in the introduction we can strip out everything except for the forward and preface when a study or devotional is uploaded" and "for bundles there needs to be the option for both physical and digital. Bundles physical will route through Stripe so the bundle itself does not need an amazon feature."
  - **Introduction narrowing.** Both PDF-import paths that build a leading "Introduction" lesson from a document's own front matter (`splitByHeadingKeywords`, the Devotional/generic path, and `splitStudyPlainTextByHeadings`, the Study path) now pass that front matter through a new `narrowToForewordAndPreface()` before keeping it — a title page, a Table-of-Contents duplicate, a dedication, acknowledgments, etc. are all discarded; only a genuine Foreword and/or Preface section survives. No Foreword/Preface at all means no Introduction lesson is created, rather than an empty one. The generic path only applies this when at least one other real section follows the Introduction — a document with no real headings anywhere still keeps its one and only section intact (that's the whole document misdetected as "Introduction", not actual front matter to narrow).
  - **A required security/code-quality review (8 parallel angles, 1-vote verify) caught two real defects in the first version of this logic before it shipped, both fixed, not just flagged:** (1) a dot-leader Table-of-Contents entry for "Foreword"/"Preface" itself (e.g. "Foreword .......... 3") matched the same keep-heading check as a real section start, since the check only looked at the line's own prefix; (2) the function reused `looksLikeHeadingLine` — a heuristic built for a much lower-stakes job, deciding whether to bold a line within body text — as the *only* signal for when to stop keeping a section, so any ordinary short, capitalized, punctuation-free line genuinely inside a real Foreword (a pull-quote-style sentence) would silently truncate the rest of the section. Fixed by splitting the heuristic into two closed, purpose-built checks matching this file's own established convention elsewhere (`QUESTION_INLINE`/`GOING_DEEPER_PREFIX`/`VERSE_REF_HEADING` are closed markers too, not a generic reuse): `FRONT_MATTER_KEEP_HEADING` now also rejects a line with a trailing dot-leader/page-number shape, and a section only ever *ends* on a closed set of other recognized front-matter labels (`FRONT_MATTER_END_HEADING` — Acknowledgments, Dedication, Copyright, Contents, Introduction), never on any heading-shaped line in general. New regression test reproduces both exact failure shapes in one fixture (a real Foreword surviving past its own internal pull-quote line, with the TOC's dot-leader references for it correctly excluded).
  - **Bundle "both" format.** `bundleSchema` gained `format: "both"` (previously `"physical" | "digital"` only) and a `digitalPriceCents` field, with the identical "required iff format is both" refinement `productSchema` already uses for regular products — now a single shared `hasValidDigitalPriceCentsForFormat()` check both schemas call, rather than two independent copies. `BundleForm.tsx` gained a third format radio and a second price input, mirroring `ProductForm.tsx`'s own established "both" pattern. `app/api/admin/bundles/route.ts` passes `digitalPriceCents` through and resets `digitalStripePriceId`/`digitalStripeProductId` on a digital-price change, reusing a new shared `stripePriceInvalidationFields()` helper (`lib/stripe/products.ts`) instead of duplicating that reset logic a second time (it previously existed only in the products route). A bundle still never has its own `amazonUrl`/`sellOnStripe` fields at all regardless of format — both of a "both" bundle's purchase routes are Stripe-only by construction, exactly as asked. The entire checkout → webhook → entitlement pipeline (`lib/stripe/products.ts`'s `isDigitalVariant`/`isPhysicalVariant`/`getOrCreateStripePrice`, `lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.ts`'s digital-cascade and physical-founder-email branches, `app/api/checkout/route.ts`'s variant validation) needed zero changes — confirmed by reading each directly, not assumed: all of it was already keyed off `order_items.variant` and `isDigitalVariant`/`isPhysicalVariant`, not `product.type`, so a bundle with `format: "both"` is handled identically to a regular "both" product the moment the schema/route/UI allow writing that value.
  - **The same review caught 3 more real bugs in the bundle-format build, all fixed:** (1) selecting "Both" for a brand-new bundle never defaulted `digitalPriceCents` away from `null` — the digital price field displays "0.00" from its own initial text state, looking already filled in, so an admin who never touched it would hit a confusing generic "Couldn't save" with no visible cause; fixed the same way `ProductForm.tsx`'s own format selector already does. (2) `/admin/bundles` and the public `/books` listing both had a binary format label (`"physical" ? X : Y`) with no case for `"both"`, silently mislabeling a "both" bundle as digital-only (admin list) or physical-only (public listing) — the same "a new column not fully wired everywhere it's read" pattern this file has flagged before; both pages now show both prices/labels. (3) `BundleForm`'s "this bundle saves $X" calculation compared the bundle's own *combined* (physical + digital) price against `partsTotalCents`, which only ever sums one price per component — two mismatched baskets producing a misleading figure; fixed by only showing that comparison for `"physical"`/`"digital"` bundles, where it's a true one-price-to-one-price comparison, and skipping it for `"both"` rather than inventing a new comparison that might still be wrong.
  - **Deferred, flagged, not fixed:** four structurally identical "label + sanitized-decimal input" price-field blocks now exist across `ProductForm.tsx` (2, pre-existing) and `BundleForm.tsx` (2, new) with no shared component; and `app/(marketing)/books/[slug]/page.tsx` has four separate `product.type === "bundle" ? X : Y` inline ternaries for copy instead of one computed source. Both are real, named duplication/structure findings from the review, but reach into already-shipped code (`ProductForm.tsx` predates this build) — left as follow-up refactors rather than folded into this build's scope.
  - 15 new/updated tests (`schemas.test.ts`'s new `bundleSchema` "both" describe block; `app/api/admin/bundles/route.test.ts`'s new create/PATCH-reset cases; `webhook-handlers.test.ts`'s new "both"-bundle cascade/founder-email cases; `splitIntoLessons.test.ts`'s narrowing/regression cases, with 4 pre-existing tests updated to match the new, correct behavior). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (369, up from 359) both clean.
  - **Not yet committed or deployed.** `BundleForm.tsx`'s own UI behavior (the "both" radio's digital-price default, the new price field) wasn't exercised live in-browser this pass — this session's sandbox refused a temporary `requireAdmin()` bypass attempt as a security-weakening action and the attempt was abandoned immediately, reverted, and confirmed clean via `git diff`, rather than worked around. Verified instead by the schema/route/webhook test coverage above and by code review. The public `/books` listing (unaffected format badges) was confirmed rendering correctly against local dev.

- **2026-11-09 (newest) — rebuilt the book-cover PDF parser around the real root cause: each cover's own genuine double-line border was being cropped away by a blind, fixed-percentage spine-margin guess, not reconstructed; a first attempt this same session got the diagnosis wrong and was fully replaced once the user supplied the actual source PDF.** Reported directly by the user with a homepage screenshot: three featured covers ("The Journey," Colossians, the Psalms devotional) showed visibly inconsistent side margins next to each other.
  - **First attempt, wrong diagnosis (fully superseded below, not left in the code).** Measuring a single row at each half-crop's own vertical mid-height, `equalizeBorderMargins()` seemed to show no border at all on the spine-facing side of either half — concluded (wrongly) that these covers draw one continuous border frame around the entire unfolded back+spine+front wrap, open at the spine, and built logic to synthesize a matching border stroke on the missing side by copying pixels from the opposite half's genuine edge. This looked right in isolation (even survived a full regeneration of all 6 covers) but the user caught it immediately: "are you creating your own border? Why can you not discern the existing image borders?" — then supplied the actual original uploaded source PDF directly.
  - **Real root cause, found by rendering that exact source file whole and measuring it properly.** Every cover's PDF genuinely has its own complete, independent double-line border on both the back and front panel — confirmed by the user directly ("every cover I upload has all four borders, double-lined, in the PDF"). The actual bug: `SPINE_MARGIN_FRACTION` (6%, tuned up from 4% on 2026-11-08 for a different cover) was simply too wide for this cover — it cropped the back half off at pixel x=841 and started the front half at x=1071, which excludes the real borders sitting at x≈842-854 (back's own right edge) and x≈1058-1070 (front's own left edge) before any border-aware code ever ran. A single mid-height detection row made this worse: for the back cover specifically, that row cuts straight through the "EACH WEEK INCLUDES" bullet list, producing dozens of spurious brightness transitions with nothing to do with the real border.
  - **Fix: stop guessing the spine margin at all.** New `findBorderClusters()` samples 30 rows spread down the full page height (clear of top/bottom corner flourishes) and keeps only the columns bright in ≥80% of them — a real border stroke runs the entire height so it lights up consistently at the same x in every sampled row, while a line of body text only crosses whichever one or two rows happen to land on it, so it's filtered out by construction rather than needing a separate plausibility guard. `findHalfBorders()` splits the resulting clusters by which side of the page's own midpoint they fall on and returns each half's own left/right border span. `splitBookCoverPdf()` then crops each half directly at its own real border — the crop's far (trim) side gets a margin outside the border equal to the border's own natural distance from that page edge, mirrored onto the spine-facing side, so every cover ends up with a complete, evenly margined box on all four sides using only pixels that were genuinely always there. Falls back to the old plain `SPINE_MARGIN_FRACTION` split only if a half's border can't be reliably found at all (a design with no border, or one whose palette doesn't contrast enough to detect).
  - All of the first attempt's machinery (`equalizeBorderMargins`, `synthesizeMissingBorderEdges`, `captureBorderBand`/`pasteBorderBand`, `TARGET_BORDER_MARGIN_FRACTION`, `MIN_BORDER_SPAN_FRACTION`) was removed, not kept alongside the fix.
  - Rewrote `lib/images/bookCover.test.ts`'s border-specific cases to match: a fallback test (a bright spot only a few rows tall is correctly ignored, crop falls back to the plain spine split) and a comprehensive real-shape test (a synthetic cover with two independently-margined genuine borders, a dark spine fill between them, and a one-row-only body-text-shaped bright smear) asserting the exact expected crop width on each side, that both of each half's own border strokes survive, and that neither crop contains any trace of the spine. 7 tests in this file, all passing; `npm run verify` and `npm test` (359) both clean.
  - **Reprocessed all 6 real covers** (Colossians, the Psalms devotional, all 4 "Journey" parts) via `scripts/reprocess-book-covers.ts` against the real connected Supabase project and R2 bucket — re-uploaded in place, no DB rows changed. Verified live on the real deployed staging site: the homepage's featured grid and the full `/books` listing both show every cover with its own complete, correctly positioned double-line border and zero clipped text on either the front or back — including Part Three's front cover, whose title ("Confession & Repentance") had been clipped by the first attempt's synthetic border and now renders in full.

- **2026-11-08 (newest) — built "The Journey": 4 new Part products/studies (physical, Amazon-only) imported from their own manuscript PDFs, plus a digital bundle that grants access to all 4; found and fixed 5 real bugs along the way, 2 of them in shared production code, not just this import.** Source materials were 4 full study-manuscript PDFs ("Part One — Out of the Dust," "Part Two — Formed and Sent," "Part Three — Confession & Repentance," "Part Four — Life," titles confirmed from each PDF's own front matter) plus a full-wrap cover PDF per part and a flat PNG bundle cover, all supplied directly by the user, with per-part Amazon URLs and pricing ($25/part, $80 bundle) given explicitly after an initial price-only guess was asked and answered mid-session.
  - **Parser bugs found and fixed in `lib/import/splitIntoLessons.ts`** (`splitStudyPlainTextByHeadings`, the Study-document PDF-import heuristic Colossians already used) — this new document family exposed real gaps the Colossians-only test suite never exercised:
    1. **Case-insensitive `WEEK_HEADING` matched ordinary recap prose.** Part One's own "Movement capstone" weeks recap earlier weeks ("Week 4 let the mask fall. Week 5 named the distance..."), and a line-wrapped sentence starting with "Week 4" at the front of an extracted line matched the old `/i`-flagged heading regex just as readily as a real title-page marker, producing dozens of spurious lessons titled with a fragment of running prose. Fixed by making the regex case-sensitive: every document in this family renders its *real* title-page marker in full caps ("WEEK 1"), while recap prose and (as it turned out) some table-of-contents listings use ordinary title case ("Week 1") — a distinction the old `/i` flag was erasing on purpose but shouldn't have.
    2. **Three different real Contents-page layouts, none matching the old TOC-exclusion logic.** Part One's TOC lines read "Week N · Title ###" (title case, now excluded by the case fix above); Part Two's read as bare "WEEK N" with no distinguishing mark at all (identical in every way to a real body heading); Part Three's and Part Four's used yet other shapes. The old `findStudyTocRange()` assumed a single Contents-page shape (borrowed from Colossians) and either bailed out after the very first TOC entry (swallowing nothing, letting every later TOC line double as a spurious "heading") or, worse, kept *extending* its exclusion range through every real body heading it met afterward (since Part Four's real headings carry a `"· LIFE"`/`"· WISDOM"` suffix exactly like a dotted TOC entry) — collapsing an entire 48-week document into one giant fallback lesson. Replaced the whole TOC-detection approach: rather than trying to recognize a Contents page at all, take the **last** occurrence of each week number's heading and discard any earlier occurrence as front-matter noise — a Contents listing can only ever come *before* the body it indexes, never after, so this is correct regardless of what decoration (a dot, a page number, nothing) any given document's TOC happens to use. Existing Colossians-shaped synthetic test updated to assert the new (equally correct, slightly different) behavior: the Contents text is no longer specially excluded, just harmlessly folded into the Introduction lesson as plain prose.
    3. **Inline "QUESTION \<text\>" layout** — Parts Three and Four render a station's prompt on the *same line* as the label ("QUESTION Where in your life right now are you confessing...") instead of Parts One/Two's own "QUESTION" on its own line with blank space below to write in. The old exact-line-match check only recognized the latter, so every one of Part Three/Four's ~500 questions would have been silently folded into plain reading text instead of becoming a saved-answer `question` block. Fixed with a word-bounded `QUESTION_INLINE` regex (`/^QUESTION\b[:\s]*/`) used both for detection and as the new section-boundary check — the word-boundary specifically guards against a real false-positive risk in this document family ("Questionable content never earns a place...").
    4. **Numbered-book verse references** (`1 JOHN 1:9`, `1 PETER...`) weren't recognized as section boundaries at all (`VERSE_REF_HEADING` required the book name to start with a letter) — low-incidence but a real gap, since Part Three cites several numbered books; fixed with an optional leading `[123]\s+` prefix.
    - 3 new regression tests added (inline-question extraction, the "Questionable" false-positive guard, numbered-book-reference boundary detection) alongside the updated existing test. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (358 passing) both clean.
  - **Import results, reviewed lesson by lesson, not assumed clean**: all 4 parts split into exactly 13 lessons each (an Introduction + 12 weeks), matching each PDF's own Table of Contents count. Part One/Two: 5 questions + 5 Hebrew/Greek callouts per week, matching their own stated "five passages... five word studies" structure. Part Three/Four: 8-10 questions + 5 callouts per week — a genuinely different, denser per-week structure (confirmed against each PDF's own preface text describing "eight or nine passages... each with a question"), not a parser inconsistency. Two small, hand-fixed cosmetic defects found on review, both from a title or word genuinely wrapping across two source lines in a way the generic heuristic can't be expected to merge back: Part Three's Week 25/36 titles were truncated to "Confession & Repentance:" (missing "An Overview"/"Keep Walking It," the title's second line) — corrected directly in the `lessons` table; and 5 Hebrew/Greek callout titles across all 4 parts extracted as a single stray diacritic character instead of the real word (an RTL-text-extraction quirk, same general class of issue CLAUDE.md has flagged before for Hebrew) — corrected by pulling the real word back out of each callout's own body text, which still had it intact.
  - **A second, more severe cover-image bug found live by the user, in shared production code, not just this import.** After the parts were live, the user flagged the front/back cover crops as "not centralized" — turned out to be two distinct things. First, this session's own one-off local rasterization script (used only because the real cover PDFs' ~300 DPI embedded images exceed `lib/images/bookCover.ts`'s production 6-megapixel memory cap — see below) had picked too wide a spine-clearance margin, visibly cutting off the front cover's own left-side decorative border entirely. Fixed locally and re-verified by eye. But the user's report, investigated further, pointed at a second, real, **pre-existing bug in the shared production `lib/images/bookCover.ts`** itself: a plain midpoint-based spine cut leaves each crop's own border frame sitting measurably closer to the spine-adjacent edge than the outer-trim edge (confirmed by pixel-scanning the border's distance from each edge: ~30px vs ~72px on these new covers) — a real, print-convention artifact (the spine-fold margin is narrower than the outer-trim margin in these cover designs) that reads as visibly off-center once cropped into a standalone web image with no spine left to explain the asymmetry. Confirmed this wasn't new: the **already-live** Colossians cover (processed by the real, unmodified production pipeline, not this session's script) showed the exact same ~4.3% asymmetry when measured the same way. Ported the fix into `lib/images/bookCover.ts` itself (`equalizeBorderMargins()`, using Photon's own `get_raw_pixels()`/`crop()` — no new dependency) so every future cover-PDF upload through the real admin UI gets a centered result automatically, not just this one import. 1 new regression test (`bookCover.test.ts`) builds a synthetic cover with a deliberately asymmetric border and asserts the output crop is centered to within a few pixels. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (358, 1 new) both clean. The 4 new parts' and the bundle's own covers were all regenerated through the corrected logic, re-uploaded, and the old, asymmetric-crop R2 objects deleted; the bundle's own flat PNG cover (no spine to split) was unaffected and already measured symmetric.
  - **Third, smaller bug found during live admin-UI verification**: the Study edit page's "Linked product" dropdown only ever queried `digital`/`both`-format products (the only formats that can grant an entitlement on their own purchase) — which silently misrendered for these new studies, each linked to a `physical`-only product (access instead comes only from buying the bundle). The dropdown's `<select>` showed the *first* digital product in the list as if selected, even though the underlying saved value was correct and unaffected unless an admin actually touched the control — still a real, confusing trap for whoever next opens this form. Fixed in `app/admin/studies/[id]/page.tsx` by always including the study's own already-linked product in the option list regardless of its format, with an updated helper note in `components/admin/StudyForm.tsx` explaining why.
  - **Fourth bug, flagged live by the user**: `/admin/bundles` (built 2026-11-08, "built bundled Products and bundled Studies" entry below) has never actually had a nav link — `AdminNav.tsx`'s `ADMIN_LINKS` never included it, so the only way to reach it was typing the URL directly (how this session itself had been getting there all along, without noticing the gap). Added a "Bundles" entry between Studies and Leader Resources. No test referenced the nav's link list directly, so nothing else needed updating.
  - **Design, confirmed with the user before building, not assumed**: `kind: "study"` for all 4 parts (each has the same `QUESTION`/`GOING DEEPER` interactive structure Colossians uses, not a plain devotional). Each part sells only as a physical book via Amazon (`type: "book"`, `format: "physical"`, `sellOnStripe: false`, the given Amazon URL) — there is no way to buy digital access to a single part on its own; the $80 "The Journey" bundle (`format: "digital"`, Stripe-only) is the only purchase that grants an entitlement, cascading to all 4 parts' underlying studies at once via the existing bundle webhook mechanism (2026-11-08 "built bundled Products and bundled Studies" entry below) — this works regardless of each component's own `physical` format, since the bundle cascade grants entitlements by component product id directly, not through each component's own purchase mechanics.
  - **Built via direct library calls mirroring the real admin routes' own logic** (same pattern as `scripts/backfill-colossians-study-content.ts`), not the HTTP API — reused `parsePdfToText`/`splitStudyPlainTextByHeadings`/`sanitizeHtml` exactly as `app/api/admin/studies/import/route.ts` does, and the same insert shapes as `app/api/admin/products/route.ts`/`app/api/admin/bundles/route.ts`, rather than reimplementing the logic. The one-off driver script itself was deleted after use, per this project's established convention for this kind of import.
  - **Verified live** against the real connected Supabase project: all 5 new catalog entries (4 parts + bundle) render correctly on `/books` and their own detail pages (covers, prices, Amazon buttons, the bundle's "What's included" list); the admin Studies/Bundles lists and a sampled lesson's content blocks (including the corrected callout titles) all confirmed via the standard temporary/reverted `getSession()` admin bypass (confirmed clean revert via `git diff`).
  - **Security/code-quality review**: the parser changes are pure text-processing with no new input/auth surface; the `bookCover.ts` change adds a pixel-measurement step over an already-decoded, already-size-capped buffer with no new external input path; the Study edit page fix only widens a `requireAdmin()`-gated page's own read query. No new dependencies in production code. No Critical/High findings.
  - **Not deployed** — this was data/content work plus two small, reviewed production code fixes (the parser hardening and the cover-margin fix), per the task's own scope; `lib/import/splitIntoLessons.ts`, `lib/images/bookCover.ts`, and the two admin-page fixes are committed-but-unshipped until the user asks for a deploy.

- **2026-11-08 (even later) — fixed the real reason login was still failing after the earlier same-day fix: GoTrue's other failure path, which the earlier fix didn't cover.** Reported live again as "magic-link login is still not working," after extensive investigation (shared-cookie mechanics, Cloudflare Access, Supabase redirect_to validation behavior) had ruled out everything except the actual callback route itself. The user pasted their real magic-link verify URL directly; tracing it (reading only the non-sensitive `error`/`error_code`/`error_description` fields back, never the token) showed it landing on `/auth/callback` with `error_code=otp_expired` and **no `code` param at all** — a different GoTrue failure shape than the one the earlier same-day fix handles. That fix only checks `if (code) { ...then branches on exchange failure... }`; when GoTrue's own `/verify` step fails before ever issuing a code (an already-used/expired link, the same prefetch-by-email-scanner scenario as before), there's no `code` to check, so the whole block was skipped and execution fell straight through to the unconditional `return NextResponse.redirect(`${origin}${next}`)` — silently redirecting to `/library` as if login had succeeded. That then got caught by the marketing/app subdomain split (since `/library` is an app path), landed on `app.staging.souldustrevival.com/library` with no session, and bounced to that domain's own `/login?next=/library` — exactly the screen reported. Fixed with a second guard, ahead of the `if (code)` block: `if (!code && searchParams.has("error"))` redirects to `/login` with the same `error=link_expired` messaging the earlier fix already established, rather than adding a new error state. 1 new regression test in `app/auth/callback/route.test.ts` (a GoTrue-level `error`/`error_code`/`error_description` response with no `code`, asserting the redirect to `/login` and that `exchangeCodeForSession` is never called). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (354 passing, 1 new) both clean. **Deployed the same session** (built with the subdomain-split/Supabase vars matching the already-live staging config per the safe-deploy procedure, `.env.local` restored byte-identical afterward) and confirmed live via `curl` against the real deployed Worker: the exact `error_code=otp_expired`/no-`code` shape now correctly 307s to `/login?error=link_expired` instead of silently redirecting to `/library`. **Superseded almost immediately, see the entry right below** -- fixing the symptom here didn't fix the actual cause, which turned out to need a different approach entirely.

- **2026-11-08 (even later still) — the real fix for the underlying cause: stopped sending a clickable magic-link URL at all, switched login/signup to a numeric OTP code.** The user retried with a brand-new login email right after the deploy above and got the "link expired" message again -- correctly shown this time instead of a silent failure, but still showing on *every single fresh link*, not an occasional fluke. Confirmed `souldustrevival.com`'s mail is hosted on Google Workspace (`dig MX`/`TXT` SPF both point at Google) -- a well-documented class of problem where Workspace's own link-prefetching/security scanning consumes a single-use link before the real human click, and a Supabase magic-link is consumed by whichever request reaches `/auth/v1/verify` first, scanner or not. Attempted to confirm this definitively by querying GoTrue's own `auth.audit_log_entries` table directly (would show two different requests/IPs hitting the same token) -- **blocked by this session's own sandbox** (generic "judged dangerous," no specific reason given) before getting an answer either way, so this remains the best-available explanation rather than a confirmed one, but it's consistent with every other data point: identical failure on every attempt, real Workspace mail hosting, and the project's own earlier entries already suspecting exactly this (2026-11-16 below, under "Credential Materialization" guardrails of its own).
  - **Fix, with the user's explicit go-ahead on this exact approach over two alternatives offered:** `components/auth/AuthForm.tsx` no longer passes `emailRedirectTo` to `signInWithOtp()` and no longer shows a "check your email" dead-end screen. Submitting the email form now transitions to a second step -- "Enter your code" / "We sent a 6-digit code to {email}" -- with a numeric input (`inputMode="numeric"`, `autoComplete="one-time-code"`, max 6 digits) that calls `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: "email" })` directly client-side. A code has no URL for anything to prefetch, so nothing but the person who actually has the email can consume it. On success, navigates via `window.location.href = next` (a real browser navigation, not `router.push`) so it still goes through `proxy.ts`'s own marketing/app subdomain redirect exactly the way `/auth/callback`'s server-side redirect already did -- `next` can be an app-domain path (e.g. `/library`) while the login page itself is served from the marketing domain. A "Use a different email" link returns to the email step. Kept the existing "no_account"/`link_expired` error handling and focus-management pattern (now focusing the code step's heading instead of a confirmation message), so none of that accessibility/UX work needed redoing.
  - `app/auth/callback/route.ts` and `components/auth/SessionHashRecovery.tsx` are both left completely unchanged -- dead code paths for the normal flow now, but harmless to keep as a fallback for any already-sent magic-link email from before this shipped, or if this project ever needs the link-based flow again for some other reason.
  - Updated `qa/e2e/specs/auth-access.spec.ts` (button name regex `Send login link` → `Send code`) and `qa/checklists/02-auth-and-account.md` (rewrote the manual walkthrough for the new code-entry steps, added a note on why it changed). `qa/e2e/fixtures/auth.ts`'s server-side test-login mechanism (`verifyOtp` with `type: "magiclink"` + `token_hash` from the Admin API, not the 6-digit `token` a person types) is unaffected -- different `verifyOtp` call shape, doesn't go through this UI at all.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (354, unchanged -- this is UI/interaction logic over an already-exercised Supabase Auth call, no new testable pure logic) both clean. **Verified in a browser**: the email-step → `no_account` error path (unchanged logic, confirmed still correct with the renamed handler functions) rendered correctly for a nonexistent login email. **Could not verify the actual code-step transition or real email delivery in this session** -- submitting the *signup* form (the only path that would have exercised the new success branch, since login correctly refuses a nonexistent account) was blocked by the sandbox as writing to a shared resource (this project's dev database is the same real, connected Supabase project staging uses, per BUILD_TASKS.md's own design) -- creating a real test account wasn't something to do unsupervised. Verified by code review and a clean typecheck/build instead.
  - **Deployed the same session**, same safe-build procedure (subdomain-split vars matching live staging config, `.env.local` restored byte-identical after, confirmed via `cf:deploy`).
  - **Action needed from the user, not something this session can do:** Supabase's Magic Link email template (Authentication → Emails → Magic Link in the dashboard, branded 2026-11-16) currently shows only a "Sign in" link button -- it needs the `{{ .Token }}` template variable added so the email actually displays the 6-digit code this new flow expects. Until that template is updated, the email won't show a code to type in, even though the login form itself is already waiting for one. **Done the same session** -- walked the user through replacing the `<a href="{{ .ConfirmationURL }}">Sign in</a>` button with a styled `{{ .Token }}` block (not just adding the code alongside the link -- the two verify against the same underlying OTP, so a scanner still prefetching the link would invalidate the code too, reintroducing the exact bug this build fixes), user confirmed saving it in the dashboard.

- **2026-11-08 (latest, before the one right below) — fixed a real bug in the new OTP flow's error handling, caught live the first time the user actually tried their own real account.** Right after the Magic Link template was updated, logging in with the real `matt.brown@souldustrevival.com` account showed "We couldn't find an account for that email" -- for an account that's logged in successfully many times before. `AuthForm.tsx`'s email-step handler treated *any* `signInWithOtp` error as "no account exists" whenever `shouldCreateUser` was false, inherited unchanged from the original magic-link version of this code. Checked the real error directly (calling `/auth/v1/otp` for both a genuinely nonexistent email and an immediate second call for the real account): a nonexistent email returns `error_code: "otp_disabled"` ("Signups not allowed for otp"), but a real account hit immediately after a prior request returns `error_code: "over_email_send_rate_limit"`, a 429 ("For security purposes, you can only request this after N seconds") -- this session's own heavy testing of the login flow in one sitting had tripped Supabase's rate limit on that exact email, and the UI was showing the misleading "no account" message instead of the real, temporary rate-limit reason. Fixed by checking `authError.code === "otp_disabled"` specifically before showing "no_account"; any other error (rate limit included) now shows Supabase's own real message. Verified in-browser: a genuinely nonexistent email still correctly shows "We couldn't find an account" (unchanged); confirmed via direct API calls that the rate-limit case is now distinguishable. No new test file (this codebase has no component-testing precedent for `AuthForm`, consistent with other components) -- verified by code review plus the live browser check and the direct API error-shape comparison above. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (354, unchanged) both clean. **Deployed the same session**, same safe-build procedure, confirmed live via curl.

- **2026-11-08 (truly latest) — fixed the code input hardcoding 6 digits when the real project sends 8, caught live the moment the user actually received and tried to enter a real code.** The real sign-in email (just sent, after the Magic Link template edit above) showed an 8-digit code (`94812795`), but `AuthForm.tsx`'s code field had `maxLength={6}` and a `code.length !== 6` submit guard carried over unexamined from when this was first built -- every real code was silently truncated to 6 digits before `verifyOtp` ever saw it, which would have failed every single login attempt.
