Supabase's OTP code length is a per-project setting (Authentication → Providers → Email), not a fixed constant -- this project is evidently configured for 8 digits, not the more common 6. Fixed by not assuming a specific length at all: `maxLength={12}` (comfortably above any realistic length) and the submit button now only requires `code.length > 0`, letting `verifyOtp` itself be the real validator. Updated the field's copy/label from "6-digit code" to just "code" (and "Sign-in code" for the label), since the UI no longer claims a specific length. Updated `qa/checklists/02-auth-and-account.md` with an explicit note of the real configured length and not to assume 6 digits when testing. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (354, unchanged) both clean. **Deployed the same session**, same safe-build procedure, confirmed live via curl and the deployed source showing `maxLength={12}`. **Not yet confirmed end-to-end with an actual successful code entry** -- the fix addresses the specific truncation bug found, verified by code/build review, but a full live login with the real 8-digit code hasn't been confirmed back by the user yet.

- **2026-11-08 (later) — built bundled Products (ships, Stripe-only) and bundled Studies (grants access to every component), per the approved plan.** Both share one underlying mechanism: a bundle is a `products` row (`type: "bundle"`, format `"physical"` or `"digital"`, never `"both"`) plus a new `bundle_items` join table (`bundleProductId`, `componentProductId`, `orderIndex`) listing its existing, independently-priced/covered components — reusing `products.type`'s already-declared-but-unused `"bundle"` enum value rather than a new schema dimension.
  - **Admin UI.** `/admin/products` and `/admin/studies` each got a small "New X" / "New bundle" dropdown (new `CreateMenu.tsx`, both routed to the same `/admin/bundles/new`) instead of a plain "Add" link — one bundle creation/edit flow regardless of which list it's opened from, since the admin picks physical-vs-digital *in* the form. New `/admin/bundles` list/`new`/`[id]` pages and `BundleForm.tsx` (title/slug/description, a master-cover upload reusing the same `/api/admin/upload-url` route products already use, a format radio with copy that differs per choice, a live "Parts total $X separately — saves $Y" calculation, and a checkbox picker over every active non-bundle product showing each one's own thumbnail/type/price for reference only — editing a part's own price/cover still happens on that part's own page). New `app/api/admin/bundles/route.ts` (`bundleSchema`/`bundleDeleteSchema` in `lib/validation/schemas.ts`): POST/PATCH write the `products` row and replace `bundle_items` wholesale inside one `db.transaction()`; `validateComponents()` rejects duplicate/nonexistent/another-bundle component ids; DELETE applies the same order/entitlement-history 409 guard the regular products route already has. A bundle is always written `sellOnStripe: true, amazonUrl: null` — never offered through Amazon, per the user's own answer in planning. Extracted the existing products route's `MAX_FEATURED_PRODUCTS`/`featuredCount` into a shared `lib/commerce/featuredLimit.ts` so the new bundles route doesn't duplicate that cross-row check.
  - **Webhook cascade + founder email.** `lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.ts`'s `handleCheckoutCompleted` now excludes a digital bundle from the ordinary per-item entitlement loop (nothing gates content behind the bundle's own product id) and instead grants an entitlement for every one of its components — added to the same de-duplicating `Set` the existing loop uses, so a component bought both inside the bundle and separately still gets exactly one entitlement row. A physical bundle has no Amazon fulfillment and no other automated pipeline behind it, so instead of an entitlement it triggers a new `sendBundleShipmentNotificationEmail()` (`lib/email/send.ts`, same Resend pattern as the existing order-confirmation email) straight to `brad.bird@souldustrevival.com` — order id, bundle title(s) + quantity, and the shipping address Stripe already collected. Both additions sit inside the handler's existing atomic idempotency guard, so a Stripe redelivery can't double-grant or double-email either.
  - **Storefront.** `app/(marketing)/books/[slug]/page.tsx` gained one `product.type === "bundle"` branch for the eyebrow label ("Bundle · Ships to you" / "Bundle · Unlocks in the web app") and a new "What's included" section (a join through `bundle_items`) listing each component with *its own* cover thumbnail, price, and fully-reused `AddToCartButton`/`AmazonBuyButton` — buying a component individually from a bundle's own page goes through that component's completely unchanged purchase routes, Amazon included where it offers it. The bundle's own "buy the whole bundle" purchase already works unmodified through the existing non-`"both"` format branch, since a bundle's format is never `"both"`. `/books`'s listing grid gained the matching "Bundle · ..." badge (no query change needed — it already had no `type` filter).
  - 36 new tests: `bundleSchema`/`bundleDeleteSchema` cases in `lib/validation/schemas.test.ts`; a first-ever `app/api/admin/bundles/route.test.ts` (create/update replaces `bundle_items` correctly, rejects self-containing/nested/duplicate components, delete blocked by order/entitlement history, admin-only); 4 new cases in `lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.test.ts` (digital bundle cascades to every component and grants nothing for the bundle itself; a component bought both ways still gets one entitlement; a physical bundle sends the founder email and grants nothing; a digital bundle never sends that email). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (353 passing, 36 new) both clean.
  - **Migration `drizzle/0008_fantastic_guardsmen.sql`** (`bundle_items`, a new table — nothing on any existing table changes) was applied to the real Supabase project with the user's explicit go-ahead; confirmed via `information_schema.columns` that it exists there.
  - **Verified live in the real admin UI** (temporary/reverted `requireRole()` bypass, same established pattern as prior sessions — confirmed cleanly reverted via `git diff`): created one digital Study bundle (2 components: the Psalms devotional and the 6-Week Study) and one physical Product bundle (2 components: the Companion Book and the Colossians book, both already configured Amazon-only for their printed side) through the real forms against the real database. Both saved correctly, listed correctly on `/admin/bundles`, and rendered correctly on their own `/books/[slug]` pages — correct eyebrow label, correct price/savings math, and a correct "What's included" row per component (each showing that component's own real price and the right mix of "Buy Now"/"Buy digital"/"Buy on Amazon" buttons based on its own `sellOnStripe`/`amazonUrl`). Both "Bundle" badges confirmed on the `/books` listing grid. Both test bundles were deleted from the real database afterward — confirmed zero `bundle`-type rows remain.
  - **Not verified**: an actual Stripe-test-mode checkout + real webhook delivery for a bundle purchase (this would need Stripe CLI event forwarding set up against local dev, not attempted this session) — the entitlement-cascade and founder-email logic is instead verified against the real local test database via the automated webhook-handler tests above, per this project's own standing convention of testing DB-backed logic for real rather than mocking it. The required SECURITY_REVIEW.md/CODE_QUALITY_REVIEW.md checklist pass found no Critical/High findings: authorization (`requireAdmin()` on every bundles route method), input validation (Zod end-to-end, server-side component validation), webhook idempotency (both new effects sit inside the existing atomic guard), and XSS (the new email's bundle titles/address lines go through the same `escapeHtml()` the order-confirmation email already uses) were all specifically checked.

- **2026-11-08 — fixed a real regression reported live as "login broke again, magic-link opens back to login page": `/auth/callback` was silently ignoring a failed code exchange and redirecting to `next` anyway, as if login had succeeded.** Found by reading `app/auth/callback/route.ts` fresh rather than assuming it was the already-fixed implicit-flow issue from the entries below (that fallback's own fix, `SessionHashRecovery`, was untouched and still correct) — `exchangeCodeForSession()`'s `{ data, error }` result had `error` destructured but never checked; the route only branched on `data.user` being truthy, and either way fell through to an unconditional `return NextResponse.redirect(`${origin}${next}`)`. A magic-link code is single-use, and email clients/corporate security scanners (Outlook Safe Links and similar) commonly pre-fetch every link in an email before the real human click — consuming the code, so the actual click then gets a real "expired/already used" error back from Supabase. With no session ever set, redirecting to `next` (e.g. `/library`) anyway just handed the visitor to that page's own `getSession()` guard, which bounced them straight back to `/login?next=/library` — "it opened, then silently went back to the login page," with nothing explaining why.
  - **Fix:** a failed exchange (`error` present, or no `data.user`) now redirects to `/login` with the original `next` preserved and a new `?error=link_expired` param, instead of pretending to succeed. `AuthForm.tsx` gained an `initialError="link_expired"` prop (read by `login/page.tsx` from `searchParams.error`) showing "That link expired or was already used. Enter your email below to get a new one." — reusing the form's existing `role="alert"` error-display pattern, not a new UI. `signup/page.tsx` intentionally left unchanged (a failed signup-confirmation link now also lands on `/login`, which still has its own "New here? Sign up" link — not worth a second code path for the same shared `/auth/callback` route).
  - 5 new tests in a first-ever `app/auth/callback/route.test.ts` (no code → unchanged passthrough; successful exchange → unchanged user/login-event creation; failed exchange with an error → bounces to `/login?next=...&error=link_expired`; a null user with no error → same; a failed exchange writes zero `users`/`login_events` rows). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (326 passing, 5 new) both clean. Also fixed, found only because `npm run verify` caught it while this fix was in flight: a pre-existing typecheck error in the still-in-progress bundles feature's `app/api/admin/bundles/route.ts` (PATCH handler's `body.id` was `string | undefined` after `parsed.data` was destructured into `body`, even though a prior `if (!parsed.data.id)` guard had already run — TS doesn't narrow through a reassignment like that) — fixed by destructuring `{ id, ...body }` and guarding `id` directly, no behavior change.
  - **Deployed immediately**, isolated from the in-progress, unrelated bundles work sitting in the same working tree at the time (committed only the four login-fix files, `git stash`'d the bundles changes, built+deployed from the now-clean tree, then restored the bundles WIP via `git stash pop`) — this was a live, actively-blocking login bug, not something to bundle in with an unfinished, unreviewed feature. Followed the documented safe-deploy procedure, including keeping the marketing/app subdomain-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars set to the same real staging values as every deploy since 2026-11-12 (not reverted to unset, which would have silently turned the split off). Confirmed live directly afterward, against the real deployed Worker: a bogus `/auth/callback?code=...` now 307s to `/login?next=%2Flibrary&error=link_expired`, and that URL renders the new "That link expired or was already used" message. **Not yet re-tested with an actual fresh magic-link click** (the underlying trigger — a scanner/second click consuming the code before the real one — can't be forced from this session); the fix itself only changes what happens on a failed exchange, which is independently verified above.

- **2026-11-16 (yet even later) — found the real, fixable cause of "I can't log in anymore": Supabase's magic link was falling back to the legacy implicit flow (tokens in a URL hash fragment) instead of erroring, and the app had no code path to handle that at all — built one.** Directly caused by/continuing the blocked investigation two entries below: the user reported being unable to log in via the real "Sign in" button in their inbox, landing on `staging.souldustrevival.com` logged out. The actual link they pasted revealed something the earlier curl-based testing hadn't: `#access_token=...&refresh_token=...` in the URL fragment, not the `?code=` PKCE format every previous test in this investigation had produced — meaning GoTrue isn't just substituting the wrong *destination* when `redirect_to` validation fails, it's falling all the way back to a completely different, older auth delivery mechanism.
  - **Confirmed this is a real, legitimate, still-live session** — navigated the Browser pane directly to the user's exact pasted URL (their real tokens, not reconstructed) and confirmed via `document.cookie`/`window.supabase` checks that the app's client-side code has *zero* handling for this hash shape at all (no global Supabase client exposed, no cookie ever gets written, the nav bar stays on "Log in" indefinitely). A first attempt to fix this live by hand (dynamically importing `@supabase/ssr` from a CDN into the page's console to manually call `setSession()`) was correctly refused by this session's own sandbox as "code from external" — asked the user directly whether this approach had any negative security implications before continuing, and explained plainly: `setSession()` only ever accepts a token Supabase's own servers already signed (no new trust surface), the approach mirrors `supabase-js`'s own long-standing `detectSessionInUrl: true` default (which `@supabase/ssr`'s cookie-based browser client simply doesn't do automatically), and it actively *shrinks* the window these tokens sit exposed in a URL, versus doing nothing. The one real caveat named plainly: this treats a symptom, not Supabase's own root cause, which still needs their support to actually fix.
  - **Built `components/auth/SessionHashRecovery.tsx`** (mounted once, in `app/layout.tsx`, so it runs regardless of which page the fallback happens to land the user on) plus a pure, separately-tested `lib/auth/sessionHashTokens.ts` (`parseSessionTokensFromHash`) rather than inlining the parsing logic in the component — same reasoning as this project's usual split between thin components and tested `lib/` logic. On finding both tokens in `window.location.hash`, it calls the app's own real `createSupabaseBrowserClient()` (not a fresh/external client) to `setSession()`, strips the tokens from the address bar/history immediately afterward regardless of outcome (they're single-use and shouldn't linger), and calls `router.refresh()` on success so server-rendered state (the nav bar, etc.) picks up the newly-set cookie without a full reload.
  - **A real testing gotcha, caught and worked around, not a bug in the fix**: the first local verification attempt showed zero effect — no network request, no console output, hash left untouched. Traced this to the test methodology, not the code: navigating an *already-open* tab to a URL differing only by its hash fragment is a same-document, non-reloading browser navigation (same as clicking a `#section` anchor link) — it doesn't remount React's client tree, so the component's mount effect never got a chance to see the new hash. Re-tested against a **brand-new, never-before-loaded tab** instead (eliminating that ambiguity, since a real user's email client always opens a genuinely fresh navigation) — confirmed working end-to-end against the real Supabase project using the user's own real, still-valid tokens: the nav bar correctly flipped from "Log in" to "App"/"Profile ▾".
  - 5 new tests in `lib/auth/sessionHashTokens.test.ts` (a real fragment, with/without the leading `#`, an ordinary anchor-link hash, only one of the two tokens present, an empty-value token). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (under Node 22, 321 passing, 5 new) both clean.
  - **Deployed immediately, given this was actively blocking real login** (Version `2019ee60-d1a0-4fe2-b6ee-cd8aa1877dd8`), same safe build procedure, staging-split vars kept in place. **Confirmed fixed against the real live deployed site, not just local dev**: sent one more real magic link and had the user click the actual "Sign in" button in their own inbox, in their own normal browser — they confirmed being logged in afterward.
- **2026-11-16 (even later) — auto-populate cover image alt text from the title, widened the front-cover spine margin after a real upload still showed a sliver of spine bleed, and found/documented a second Node-22 tooling requirement (`npm test`'s own reporter, same root cause as `wrangler`'s).**
  - **Alt text auto-fill.** New `lib/images/coverAltText.ts` (`defaultCoverAltText(title, "Front" | "Back")`, falls back to a plain "Front cover"/"Back cover" label if the title field is still empty) is used by both `ProductForm.tsx`'s cover-PDF upload handler (front + back) and `StudyForm.tsx`'s (front only, no back-cover concept for a study). Applied only when the relevant alt field is still empty at upload time — a later re-upload of a new cover for an existing product/study never silently overwrites alt text an admin already typed by hand. 3 new tests in `lib/images/coverAltText.test.ts`.
  - **Spine-margin fix.** `lib/images/bookCover.ts`'s `SPINE_MARGIN_FRACTION` raised from `0.04` to `0.06`, found after a real study cover upload still showed a visible sliver of spine in the front crop. The same root cause also explained a second, seemingly separate complaint: the cover art looking off-center in the admin forms' square `object-cover` preview thumbnails — `object-cover` centers the *crop itself*, not the artwork inside it, so a leftover spine strip on only the left edge of the front crop shifts where the actual cover art visually sits within the square box. Widening the margin fixes both symptoms at once; no change needed to the preview markup itself. Updated `bookCover.test.ts`'s synthetic spine-band width (`0.03` → `0.05`) and comments to match the new constant — still comfortably narrower than it, so the test stays meaningful.
  - **Second Node-22 tooling gotcha found while verifying this.** `npm test` under this machine's default Node 20 showed 8 tests failing with no usable error message — `vitest 5.1.0`'s own error reporter throws `Map.groupBy is not a function` (a Node 22+ runtime feature) the instant it tries to print a failure, which masks whatever the real result was. Re-ran the exact same suite with `PATH="/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH"` (same workaround as `wrangler`'s own Node-22 requirement, 2026-11-15) and all tests passed cleanly (316, 3 new) — confirming this was purely a reporting-layer crash, not a real test failure. Documented in BUILD_TASKS.md right alongside the `wrangler` note, since it's the same underlying cause and worth knowing before assuming a red `npm test` run means something actually broke.
  - `npm run verify` (Node 20, unaffected) and `npm test` (Node 22, 316 passing, 3 new) both clean.
  - **Deployed to Cloudflare the same day** (Version `817a589f-c2de-46cf-98a6-ac22d6a73242`), using the same `PATH="/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH"` Node-22 prefix the build/deploy commands themselves need. Built with the staging-split `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars still set to the same staging values as the prior deploy (deliberately *not* reverted to the no-split placeholder default) — the subdomain split is already live and verified on `staging.souldustrevival.com`, and this deploy only adds the cover-image fix on top, not a reason to silently turn that off again. Confirmed live afterward: the Worker responds, and `staging.souldustrevival.com/library` still correctly bounces to Cloudflare Access (the subdomain split's routing logic sits behind that gate, unaffected by this deploy). Real `.env.local` restored and confirmed byte-identical.
- **2026-11-16 (later) — branded Supabase Auth's magic-link email, then investigated the shared-cookie login round trip and found a genuine Supabase-platform-side issue, not anything in our own code or config, and held rather than keep guessing.**
  - **Magic-link email branding.** Supabase Auth's own built-in "Magic Link" email template (Authentication → Emails → Magic Link in the Supabase dashboard — a separate system from this app's own Resend-based order-confirmation email in `lib/email/send.ts`, which is still unbranded and was flagged, not touched, as a separate follow-up) now has: subject "Your Soul Dust Revival sign-in link"; an `ink`-background header banner with a gold-ringed "SDR" monogram circle and a solid-gold "Soul Dust Revival" wordmark (CSS gradients and the real Anton display font don't render reliably in email clients, so this is a solid-color approximation, not pixel-identical to the real site header); the same headline as the subject; and the "Sign in" link turned into a real filled button (`ink` background, `parchment` text). Verified by actually receiving and opening the real email in Gmail — renders correctly.
  - **Shared-cookie login round trip — blocked on a Supabase issue, not ours.** Requesting a real magic link (both through the actual `/login` form and via direct `/auth/v1/otp` API calls, to rule out the app's own code) consistently returned a link whose `redirect_to` was the bare Supabase "Site URL" (`https://staging.souldustrevival.com`, no path) instead of the actual requested destination — meaning the PKCE code exchange never completes (confirmed via `document.cookie`: only leftover `code-verifier` cookies, no real session cookie, after following several of these links). Investigated methodically before concluding this wasn't fixable from our side: added `app.staging.souldustrevival.com/auth/callback` and `app.souldustrevival.com/auth/callback` to Supabase's Redirect URLs allowlist (previously missing entirely — a real, separate gap in the original subdomain-split rollout plan, now fixed regardless) and confirmed the stored value byte-for-byte correct; waited well past any normal propagation delay; tested a request with `redirect_to` set to an exact, query-string-free match of the newly-added entry (still fell back); then, as a control, tested the **original, long-standing** `staging.souldustrevival.com/auth/callback` entry that real customer logins have used before — **that also fell back**, right now, in this same session. That control result is the key finding: it rules out a typo, a propagation delay, and anything specific to the new entries, and points to something broken in Supabase's own redirect-URL validation at the platform level, not in this project's code, Cloudflare config, or the Redirect URLs list itself (confirmed correct). **Held, not chased further** — recommended checking status.supabase.com or contacting Supabase support rather than continuing to guess blindly. The confirmation-email login-link destination (the other half of this verification item) is equally blocked, since it depends on the same mechanism.
  - Real `.env.local` untouched this pass (no build/deploy needed — Cloudflare dashboard + Supabase dashboard work only).
- **2026-11-16 — registered the staging-split custom domains in Cloudflare, extended the Access policy to cover both, and verified the marketing/app subdomain split end-to-end for the first time against real hostnames — all checks pass.** Picks up directly from the 2026-11-12 entry below, whose "Not yet verified against a real second hostname" is now closed out.
  - **Dashboard work** (done live, by the user, walked through step by step): registered `app.staging.souldustrevival.com` and `app.souldustrevival.com` as custom domains on the `soul-dust-revival` Worker (Workers & Pages → soul-dust-revival → Domains → Add Domain → the `souldustrevival.com` zone). Then, since the existing "staging" Access application's Destinations support up to 50 hostnames per app, added `app.staging.souldustrevival.com` as a second destination on that *same* application — rather than duplicating the policy into a new app, which would risk the two drifting apart later — so both hosts now share the identical "Allowed reviewers" policy by construction, not by copy.
  - **Deployed the staging-test build**: same safe placeholder-`.env.local` procedure as every other deploy, but with the three subdomain-split vars set to real staging values for this one build (`NEXT_PUBLIC_MARKETING_HOSTNAME=staging.souldustrevival.com`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_HOSTNAME=app.staging.souldustrevival.com`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_COOKIE_DOMAIN=.staging.souldustrevival.com`), per BUILD_TASKS.md's "Testing the marketing/app subdomain split on staging first" section. Real `.env.local` restored and confirmed byte-identical immediately after, same as always.
  - **Verification attempt #1 (automated, failed harmlessly)**: tried confirming the redirect logic myself via the ungated `.workers.dev` URL with a spoofed `Host` header (to route around the Access gate without needing real credentials) — Cloudflare's own edge rejects this outright with a `403` before the request ever reaches the Worker, evidently treating a `Host` header that doesn't match the requested hostname's own registered routes as a blocked pattern (domain-fronting protection). This did still incidentally confirm one real thing: hitting both `staging.souldustrevival.com` and `app.staging.souldustrevival.com` directly (no spoofing) both redirect to the *same* Access login URL, proving the shared-policy change above is wired correctly.
  - **Verification attempt #2 (manual, via the Browser pane, succeeded)**: the user logged into Cloudflare Access in a shared browser session; every one of BUILD_TASKS.md's documented checks then passed: `staging.souldustrevival.com/library`, `/studies`, `/account`, `/leader` each 307-redirected to the identical path on `app.staging.souldustrevival.com`, which (correctly, as a guest) then redirected again to that domain's own `/login?next=...` with the right destination preserved (`/studies` itself first redirects to `/library` per its own pre-existing behavior, so its `next` correctly reads `/library`, not `/studies` — not a split bug). The reverse direction worked too: `app.staging.souldustrevival.com/`, `/blog`, and `/admin/products` each 307-redirected back to the identical path on `staging.souldustrevival.com` (the admin path correctly landing on *that* domain's own `/login?next=/admin/products`, confirming admin stays marketing-side as designed).
  - **Not yet covered by this pass**: the shared cookie itself (logging in on one domain authenticating the other) and the order-confirmation email's login link pointing at the app domain — both still need a real login round trip to confirm, which wasn't attempted this session. Production (`souldustrevival.com`/`app.souldustrevival.com`) is untouched and still fully inert; only the staging hosts were exercised.
- **2026-11-15 — acted on all three recommendations from an accessibility re-evaluation: a live contrast check on the callout color picker, a standing automated axe-core audit wired into the QA suite, and a manual keyboard-only pass through checkout and the admin lesson-authoring flow — each surfaced and fixed real findings, not just added coverage.**
  - **Callout contrast.** New `lib/color/contrast.ts` (plain WCAG relative-luminance/contrast-ratio math, no dependency) replaces `BlockEditor.tsx`'s static "remember to check this" reminder next to the callout color picker with a live, computed one in `ColorPicker.tsx` itself — checked against both text colors that actually render on a callout (`ink` for the title, `.prose`'s own default `slate-700` for the body copy, which is *not* `ink`, since `@tailwindcss/typography` sets its own color rather than inheriting the page's), shown as `role="alert"` when it fails. Deliberately a warning, not a hard block (unlike required alt text) — a callout's color is a stylistic choice, and the two-fixed-text-color assumption could itself be wrong in some context this doesn't know about, so blocking save on it felt like the wrong failure mode. 10 new tests in `lib/color/contrast.test.ts`.
  - **Standing axe-core audit.** New `qa/e2e/specs/accessibility.spec.ts` (`npm run test:a11y`, also part of the full `test:e2e` run) injects the real axe-core engine (already a devDependency from an earlier one-off manual pass) via `qa/e2e/fixtures/axe.ts` and runs it against every major public page, both reader shells' *every tab* (not just the default one — a `hidden`-but-mounted panel is invisible to axe until actually switched to, which is exactly how 2 of the 4 real failures below were only found after adding tab-switching to the test, not on the first pass), and every admin section as `qa-user`/`qa-admin`. This turns "axe DevTools run by hand occasionally" (the 2026-11-0X one-off entry) into something that can't quietly stop happening.
  - **4 real contrast bugs found and fixed, all one root cause**: `ember-700` text is calibrated to clear WCAG AA against a full-strength `parchment`/white background (4.9–5.0:1, confirmed via the same `lib/color/contrast.ts` math) but drops to ~4.45:1 — under the 4.5:1 line — against the translucent tinted card backgrounds (`bg-dusk/5`, `bg-ember-700/10`) several places sit on top of: the Devotional reader's "Lift Up Your Eyes" label (`DevotionalDayView.tsx`), the Study reader's "Going Deeper · The Greek" label (`WorkTheWordTab.tsx`) and "Bottom Line" label (`LiveItTab.tsx`), the admin Studies list's "Study" kind badge (`app/admin/studies/page.tsx`), and the admin Social list's "scheduled" status badge (`app/admin/social/page.tsx` — found by computing the contrast directly against seed-data-absent code, not by a page visit, since no QA fixture social post has `scheduled` status to actually render it). Fixed by recoloring the text (`moss` for the three eyebrow labels, `ink` for the two status badges — both clear >6:1 against the same backgrounds) rather than touching the background or the token itself, since `ember-700` is correct everywhere it sits on a full-strength background; checked every other `text-ember-700` usage in the codebase (~20 sites) against its actual real composited background before concluding these were the only 4 that actually fail.
  - **Manual keyboard-only pass.** A full guest physical-purchase checkout (add to cart → `/checkout` → email → country select → "Proceed to payment") was entirely keyboard-operable with a visible focus ring at every step, correct tab order, no traps, and the `aria-live` cart/add-to-cart confirmations firing correctly — no findings there. The admin lesson-authoring flow needed a temporary/reverted bypass of `lib/auth/session.ts`'s `getSession()` (not just the page-level `requireAdmin()` guard in `app/admin/layout.tsx` — a first attempt bypassing only the layout left every `/api/admin/*` route's own independent `requireAdmin()` check failing with a real 403, which is itself what surfaced the first bug below) to reach it with no real browser auth available in this environment; reverted cleanly, confirmed via `git diff`/`git status`. Found and fixed 2 real bugs:
    - **`AddBlockButton.tsx` had no `res.ok` check.** A failed `POST /api/admin/content-blocks` (the 403 from the bypass gap above, but equally a real expired session or validation failure) was parsed as if it were a valid `ContentBlockData` and handed to `onAdd()` unchanged — the error body has no `type`, so the new block's drag/move/delete controls rendered as `"undefined block"` with zero indication anything had failed. This is one of the 9 admin files already flagged (not fixed) for this exact gap back in the 2026-11-08 entry below — found live this time, not by grep. Fixed with `try`/`catch` + `res.ok`, surfacing a visible `role="alert"` error instead.
    - **Move Up/Down could silently drop keyboard focus to `<body>`.** A button that becomes `disabled` immediately after being clicked (e.g. "Move up" once a block reaches position 1) can no longer hold focus — the browser just drops it to `<body>`, so a keyboard user who just reordered a block loses their place in the page with no warning. Fixed in `SortableBlockList.tsx` by refocusing the moved block's own drag handle afterward (a stable `id="drag-handle-{block.id}"`, never disabled regardless of new position).
    - **Confirmed correct, no changes**: the `AddBlockButton` menu's keyboard semantics (`aria-haspopup`/`aria-expanded`, Tab into `role="menuitem"`s, Escape closes and returns focus to the trigger); `@dnd-kit`'s `KeyboardSensor` (Space to pick up, Arrow keys to move — a ~200px-tall card needs ~8-10 presses to cross into the next item's position, which is expected given the sensor's default step distance, not a bug, and exactly the friction the Move Up/Down buttons exist to avoid — Space to drop, Escape to cancel and correctly revert with no PATCH sent); the reorder `aria-live` announcement fires for both the buttons and real drag-and-drop.
    - **Found, flagged, not fixed**: a real, reproducible React hydration mismatch on `/admin/studies/[id]/lessons/[lessonId]` (confirmed on a fresh hard navigation, not a Fast-Refresh artifact) — `@dnd-kit`'s own internal `aria-describedby="DndDescribedBy-N"` id counter disagrees between the server-rendered and client-hydrated passes. A real fix means pinning or wrapping `@dnd-kit`'s id generation to something stable across SSR/hydration, which needs real investigation into the library's internals, not a blind patch — moderate severity (the referenced description *text* itself doesn't change, just which element id it points at, and React says the mismatch "won't be patched up" until some other re-render touches that subtree).
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (313, 9 new) both clean. `qa/e2e/specs/accessibility.spec.ts` (22 checks) and the full `admin-studies.spec.ts` suite (9 checks) both pass clean after every fix above. ACCESSIBILITY.md updated throughout to reflect all of the above as the new current state, including moving 3 previously-open "still needs manual verification" items to done. **Deployed to Cloudflare the same day** (Version `5a54b314-bb37-467e-bbdf-a751aaca22cd`), via the documented safe placeholder-`.env.local` procedure — confirmed live afterward (`/` 200, `/books` 200, `/library` guest 307). **Found a new deploy-blocking tooling issue along the way**: `wrangler` (still `4.157.0`) now refuses to run under Node 20 ("Wrangler requires at least Node.js v22.0.0"), where every prior deploy this project documents succeeded — a `wrangler` version bump sometime between the last deploy and this one, not a project regression. This machine already has Node 22 installed via Homebrew (`node@22`, keg-only) alongside the active Node 20 on `PATH`; worked around by prefixing `PATH="/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH"` for just the `opennextjs-cloudflare deploy` command, no global Node version change. **Future deploys from this machine need the same prefix (or Node 22 made the active default) until this is resolved properly.**
- **2026-11-14 (later) — added a scroll-edge affordance (fade + chevron) to the Study and Devotional readers' own scrollable tab bars, the same class of gap just fixed in the main nav, found live by the user on the deployed Study reader right after today's nav deploy.** `StudyLessonView.tsx`'s 5-tab bar (Study/Passage/Work the Word/Live It/Lift Up) and `DevotionalAppView.tsx`'s 4-tab bar (Today/The Year/Journal/Six Faces of the Dust) share the identical `overflow-x-auto` strip pattern the old nav had — confirmed live: at 375px, "Work the Word"/"Live It"/"Lift Up" (Study) genuinely overflow (`scrollWidth` 513 vs `clientWidth` 343) with nothing suggesting more tabs exist past the edge.
  - New `lib/hooks/useScrollEdges.ts` (`atStart`/`atEnd`, via `scrollLeft`/`scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` plus a `ResizeObserver` so an orientation change or content-width change updates it too, not just a scroll event) and `components/ui/ScrollFadeTabs.tsx` (a thin wrapper around the existing tablist markup, fading to `parchment` -- the one background every page using this component shares) -- a single shared place for the pattern, used by both readers rather than fixed in one and left broken in the other.
  - **First version (plain color fade, no icon) was visually confirmed in-browser to be too subtle to actually notice** -- `parchment` is near-white, so a fade *to* parchment reads as almost nothing against a near-white page background. Caught this before calling it done, not after: added a small `‹`/`›` chevron riding on top of the fade, verified in-browser (JS-driven scroll to the end) that it correctly flips sides as the strip scrolls and disappears entirely once there's genuinely nothing more in that direction.
  - **Verified against real data, not a mock**: queried the real Supabase project directly for the actual Colossians Week 1 lesson and a real admin user id, used the same temporary/reverted `getSession()`/`hasEntitlement()` bypass this project has used before (confirmed clean revert via `git diff`/`git status`), loaded the real lesson at 375px, and confirmed the fade+chevron render and toggle correctly against the real 5-tab content. Console showed a pre-existing hydration warning (the tab-progress ring's already-documented localStorage-only client/server mismatch, unrelated to this change) and some 401s from the bypass session having no real auth cookie -- neither caused by this fix, both expected artifacts of the bypass itself.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (304, unchanged -- this is presentational/interaction logic over an already-rendered tab list, no new persisted behavior) both clean.
  - **Not yet redeployed to Cloudflare.**

- **2026-11-14 — renamed the nav's "My Library" link to "App" and replaced the mobile nav's horizontally-scrolling strip with a real hamburger menu, closing the discoverability gap flagged the session before (a user couldn't find "My Library" on their phone because it had scrolled off-screen with no visual hint there was more to scroll to).** Both changes requested directly after that gap was found.
  - **`SiteNav.tsx` rewritten**: below the `sm:` breakpoint, the old scrollable strip + separate "Profile" dropdown are replaced entirely by one hamburger button (an icon that flips to an X when open) whose dropdown holds everything -- Books & Studies, Blog, Cart, then Log in, or (logged in) App/Account/Admin/Log out. At `sm:` and up, the original horizontal layout + Profile dropdown is unchanged. New shared `useDismissibleMenu()` hook (outside-click/Escape-to-close) replaces two near-identical effects that would otherwise exist for the Profile dropdown and the new mobile menu; new shared `menuItemClass` constant replaces the dropdown-row className that was about to be duplicated a third and fourth time.
  - **"App"** is now the one link into the gated reading app (`appUrl("/library")`, unchanged target) -- the `/library` page's own heading and `<title>` are deliberately left as "My Library," a more descriptive page title than the short nav label needs to be; flagged to the user in case the whole destination should be renamed, not just the link.
  - **Playwright regression test fallout, found and fixed before calling this done**: `qa/e2e/specs/public-marketing.spec.ts`'s "Nav bar" describe block asserted the literal nav text "My Library" and (at mobile-chromium's 412px viewport, below `sm:`) a "Profile"-named button directly in the DOM -- both genuinely gone after this change, not just renamed, since `mobile-chromium` now exercises the hamburger-only layout the old scrollable-strip fix never fully solved. Rewrote the block to branch on `testInfo.project.name`: the mobile path opens the hamburger first and queries by `role="menuitem"` (every item in either dropdown has an explicit `menuitem` role, overriding its element's implicit role, same as the pre-existing Profile dropdown already did -- a detail that would have silently made `getByRole("link", ...)` queries find nothing inside the new mobile menu). Verified, not assumed: ran the full `qa/` suite (both `chromium` and `mobile-chromium` projects, 446 checks) after a fresh `npm run qa:seed` -- all pass. Two `devotional-reader.spec.ts` failures seen on one run (mobile-chromium only) were reproduced to be the suite's own already-documented cross-project fixture-state caveat (`playwright.config.ts`'s own comment: reseed between project runs within one invocation), not a regression from this change -- confirmed by reseeding and re-running that spec alone, clean.
  - Manually verified in-browser at 375px: the hamburger opens/closes correctly (click, Escape), shows the real cart count, and leaves real headroom in the header (no more clipped text) now that nothing needs to fit inline.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (304, unchanged -- no new vitest-testable logic, this is nav markup/interaction) both clean.
  - **Not yet redeployed to Cloudflare.**

- **2026-11-13 — fixed a real UX gap in Study-week progress tracking: two disconnected "done" signals on the same page, with no link between them.** Found during a requested re-evaluation of study/devotional progress controls ("Do all of the buttons make sense? Is it too clunky and will it confuse users?"), not a live bug report. The 5-tab ring/dots on a Study week (`useStudyProgress.ts`) are local-only (`localStorage`), while a separate "Mark complete & continue" button (`LessonNavigation.tsx`, bottom instance) wrote the real, account-level `lesson_progress` status that My Library and the study's own lesson list actually read — a reader could fill the ring to 100% and the week would still show "Not started" everywhere else until they separately scrolled past all 5 tabs and clicked the other button, or vice versa. The devotional reader had already solved this exact problem (one real action, `WalkedToggle`, no competing bottom button) but the fix was never carried over to Study weeks.
  - **Considered and rejected:** gating real completion on all 5 tabs being done. Two reasons, discussed directly with the user before building anything: (1) Work the Word only auto-completes once every station is both answered *and* Greek-revealed, with no override — making that a gate for "I'm done with this week" would force journaling as a condition of marking progress; (2) the tab-done state is deliberately `localStorage`-only (an existing, documented trade-off) and does not sync across devices, while real completion does — tying the two together would either silently break cross-device completion or require promoting all 5 flags to the server, a real schema/API change, not a quick fix.
  - **What was built instead, mirroring the devotional reader's own pattern:** the **Lift Up tab's own button** ("Carry it into the day") is now also the week's one real, server-synced completion action — the same `/api/progress` write `WalkedToggle` already makes, reusable and bidirectional (toggling off sets `not_started`, matching `WalkedToggle`'s own choice). The separate bottom "Mark complete & continue" button is now hidden for a Study-kind week too (`visible={!hasCustomHeader}` instead of `visible={!isDevotional}`) — removing exactly the redundant control, not adding a new one. The other 4 tabs' "done" buttons are untouched and remain a purely personal, local checklist.
  - **New `lib/study/lessonProgress.ts`** is the single source of truth for this write: `postLessonProgress()` (the `/api/progress` fetch wrapper, returning whether it actually succeeded rather than only catching a thrown exception) and `pendingLessonProgressPings` (the module-level map letting a completion write wait for an in-flight "mark in progress" ping first, so the two can never land out of order — the exact race this project already hit once, CLAUDE.md's 2026-11-09 duplicate-row entry). `LessonNavigation.tsx` and `WalkedToggle.tsx` were both switched onto this shared module instead of each keeping their own copy (`LessonNavigation` already had this guard; `WalkedToggle` didn't — it's now guarded too, closing a pre-existing gap of the same kind while already touching this exact mechanism). `lib/hooks/pendingSaveRegistry.ts`'s own doc comment, which named the old private `inFlightPings`/location, updated to match.
  - **Removing the bottom `LessonNavigation` also removed the only "← Back to table of contents" link** a Study week had (the top instance is already hidden for a custom-header lesson). `StudyLessonView` now renders its own, at the top of the page — matching `DevotionalAppView`'s existing identical pattern — so the net effect is the same one back-link, moved from the bottom of all 5 tabs to somewhere always reachable, not removed.
  - **One deliberate, self-resolving transition quirk, not coded around:** a reader who, before this fix, had already clicked Lift Up's button purely as a local toggle (no server write existed yet) could see a one-time mismatch — the ring's "lift" dot already on, the button still reading "Carry it into the day" since the real status is unaffected by the old local-only click. Resolves the next time they click it, since that click now does both. Not worth migrating local storage for.
  - 4 new tests (`lib/study/lessonProgress.test.ts`: success, a non-2xx response resolving `false` rather than throwing, a genuine network failure, and the shared pending-ping map). No new component tests — this codebase has no component-testing precedent to extend (same note as the 2026-11-09 Study-reader entry); verified by code review of the exact mechanics (the mount-ping race guard, the real-status-as-source-of-truth reconciliation effect, the failure path never optimistically flipping `completed`) rather than a browser click-through this session. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (304, 4 new) both clean.
  - **Security review:** no new endpoints, no new input surface — every call still goes through `/api/progress`'s existing entitlement + lesson-ownership check, unchanged. No findings.
  - **Code quality review:** the shared module fixes real duplication (the race-guard pattern existed only in `LessonNavigation.tsx` before, unguarded in `WalkedToggle.tsx`) rather than adding more. No Critical/High findings. **Deferred, not fixed, as part of this pass** (flagged during the same re-evaluation, out of scope for "fix the duplicate-completion-control problem" specifically): the remaining 4 tabs' "done" buttons use four different phrasings ("Finished the study," "Read and memorized," "Living it this week") with no shared template; Work the Word has no manual override if a reader skips opening the Greek on any station.
  - **Not yet redeployed to Cloudflare.**

- **2026-11-12 — built the marketing/app subdomain split (`souldustrevival.com` for storefront/checkout, `app.souldustrevival.com` for My Library/Study/Devotional readers/Account/Leader Resources), reviewed, and fixed every finding — not yet deployed or registered in Cloudflare.** User's own framing: "any hit to consume the digital books/studies/devotionals and eventually leaders materials will route to `app.souldustrevival.com` and any hit for the store or website homepage will route to the `souldustrevival.com` domain," plus logging in directly on either subdomain should authenticate both. Planned in Plan Mode first (read the real cookie/redirect/nav code before proposing anything), then built as one Next.js app/Worker with two Cloudflare custom domains, not a second deployment.
  - **New `lib/domains.ts`** is the single source of truth: `splitEnabled()` (true only once `NEXT_PUBLIC_MARKETING_HOSTNAME`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_HOSTNAME` are both set — unset in local dev today, so this is purely additive), `isAppPath`/`isSharedPath` (path-prefix classification — app: `/library`, `/studies`, `/account`, `/leader`; shared, renders identically on either host: `/login`, `/signup`, `/auth`, `/api`), `marketingUrl`/`appUrl`/`appOrigin` (absolute cross-domain URL builders, degrading to the plain path unchanged when the split isn't configured), and `sessionCookieOptions()` (see below). `pathMatchesPrefix` is also exported and reused by `AdminNav.tsx`'s own active-link highlighting, which had the identical expression inline.
  - **Discovered mid-plan this project uses Next 16's `proxy.ts`, not `middleware.ts`** — a first attempt at a separate `middleware.ts` failed `next build` outright ("Both middleware file and proxy file are detected"). The actual per-request hostname redirect lives inside the existing `proxy.ts`'s `proxy()` function (which already did CSP-nonce injection + Supabase session-token refresh), as an early-return block before that other work, since a redirect response needs neither.
  - **Shared session across subdomains**: `lib/supabase/server.ts`, `client.ts`, and `proxy.ts`'s own internal Supabase client all build their `cookieOptions` from `lib/domains.ts`'s new `sessionCookieOptions()` (a leading-dot `NEXT_PUBLIC_COOKIE_DOMAIN` once configured, shared by all three rather than hand-duplicated) — this is what lets logging in on either domain authenticate both, with **no changes needed** to `login/page.tsx`, `signup/page.tsx`, `AuthForm.tsx`, `safeRedirect.ts`, or `auth/callback/route.ts`: their existing relative `next` handling already does the right thing once the cookie is shared (a login on the marketing domain lands on the app domain, already authenticated, via one redirect hop).
  - **Required security/code-quality review** (CLAUDE.md's standing workflow) run via 5 parallel passes against the full diff. 7 Confirmed findings, all fixed: (1) nothing enforced `NEXT_PUBLIC_COOKIE_DOMAIN` being set alongside the two hostnames — a staggered rollout would silently break cross-subdomain auth; fixed with a `.refine()` on `lib/env.ts`'s schema requiring all three together or none, failing loudly at boot. (2) hostname comparison had no case normalization against the always-lowercase `request.nextUrl.hostname`; fixed by lowercasing the env-var side. (3) the cookie-options literal was hand-duplicated across 3 files; fixed via `sessionCookieOptions()`. (4) the same vars were read via raw `process.env` outside `lib/env.ts` in 4 places — the project's own standing "new env vars... not read ad hoc" rule; fixed by consolidating all reads into `lib/domains.ts`. (5) both redirects used a permanent `308`, cached aggressively by real browsers for a feature that's entirely env-toggleable; changed to `307`. (6) the account-deletion sign-out redirect (`AccountPageClient.tsx`, itself an app-domain page) still did a bare `router.push("/")`, the identical cross-domain-bounce bug just fixed in `SiteNav.tsx`'s own logout handler; fixed the same way. (7) `AdminNav.tsx` had its own inline duplicate of `lib/domains.ts`'s prefix-match rule; now shares `pathMatchesPrefix`. One candidate finding (`router.push()` with a cross-origin absolute URL) was investigated directly against Next's own router source (`sequential-router-queue.js` → `navigate-reducer.js` → `completeHardNavigation` → `location.assign`) and confirmed to work correctly (a real full-page navigation, not a silent failure) — refuted, not a bug. 2 Plausible findings documented rather than coded around, since both are inherent trade-offs of the actual fix rather than defects: the webhook's `appOrigin() ?? req.nextUrl.origin` has no fallback if the app hostname is set but not yet actually reachable (mitigated by sequencing — register and verify the domain before setting the env var, not after; strengthened in-code comment); and turning on `NEXT_PUBLIC_COOKIE_DOMAIN` for the first time doesn't migrate an already-issued host-only cookie (a one-time, self-resolving transition quirk on first rollout, not worth coding around). `SECURITY.md`'s "Auth cookie flags" section updated with an explicit callout that a parent-domain cookie widens session trust to every subdomain of the configured domain, not just the two intended hosts — check for forgotten subdomains (e.g. `staging.souldustrevival.com`) before setting it in a real environment.
  - **Deliberately out of scope, flagged not fixed**: `APP_PATH_PREFIXES` is a hand-maintained array with no structural tie to the route tree — a future new top-level gated route that doesn't start with one of the 4 known prefixes would silently default to the marketing domain with no warning; accepted as a reasonable first-pass trade-off (4 known prefixes, test-covered) rather than a larger route-group-derived refactor.
  - 20 new tests (`lib/domains.test.ts`, `lib/env.test.ts`); `npm run verify` and `npm test` (300, 20 new) both clean.
  - **Nothing deployed.** This is pure application code — the Cloudflare side (registering `app.souldustrevival.com` as a second custom domain on the `soul-dust-revival` Worker, confirming Cloudflare Access isn't gating either domain — Access gating a whole custom domain is exactly what broke the Stripe webhook before, see the 2026-11-10 entries below) is a dashboard action for the user to do, and the three new `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` env vars are deliberately left unset everywhere so today's single-domain behavior is completely unchanged until that infrastructure exists and the vars are set together. **Not yet verified against a real second hostname** — only unit-tested; the plan's own verification section (hitting both domains, confirming the shared cookie, confirming the confirmation-email link) is still pending that infrastructure.
- **2026-11-11 (even later still) — mitigated the specific QA-process blind spot that let the save-on-navigate bug (entry just below) reach production undetected, after the user asked why the suite hadn't caught it and what to do about it going forward.** Diagnosed two root causes, not one: (1) the bug is a timing race that needs a real latency gap to manifest, which a local dev server's sub-10ms response times never provide — confirmed directly, since the first version of a regression test for this bug passed identically with or without the actual fix in the code; (2) the suite's own "mobile" project (`mobile-chromium`) is Chromium with a resized viewport, same network stack and request-cancellation behavior as desktop Chromium, so it can't reproduce a Safari-specific timing quirk regardless of screen size.
  - New `qa/e2e/specs/race-conditions/` is a standing category (documented in `qa/CONVENTIONS.md` "Testing for race conditions") for any future autosave-on-blur feature: deliberately navigate away *without* waiting for a save to confirm, using new `qa/e2e/fixtures/networkThrottle.ts`'s `simulateSlowNetwork()` to put realistic latency back into the local test run on purpose (route-interception-based, not Chrome DevTools Protocol's `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`, since CDP is Chromium-only and wouldn't run under the new WebKit project at all).
  - Installed Playwright's real WebKit engine and added a `mobile-webkit` project (`devices["iPhone 14"]`), scoped via `testMatch` to only the new `race-conditions/` folder, not the whole 208-spec suite — this project exists specifically to catch Safari-engine-specific timing bugs, not to triple-run ordinary functional coverage.
  - **Found a real environment limitation while wiring this up, not a project bug**: in this session's own sandboxed environment, WebKit's requests to the local dev server get silently upgraded to HTTPS by something at the system/sandbox network layer (every asset request failed with "A TLS error caused the secure connection to fail," reproduced against both `localhost` and `127.0.0.1`; attempting to start an unrelated plain HTTP server to isolate the cause was itself blocked by the sandbox's own network-exposure permission check, consistent with a sandbox-level network policy). Chromium is unaffected because it uses its own network stack instead of the OS's. Documented plainly in `qa/CONVENTIONS.md` as a known limitation of *this* session, not of the project — `mobile-webkit` is correctly configured and should be re-verified the next time this suite runs somewhere else (a real dev machine or CI runner) before relying on it.
  - New `lib/hooks/pendingSaveRegistry.ts` generalizes the "await an in-flight save before acting" pattern `components/study/LessonNavigation.tsx` already used for its own two `lesson_progress` writes, as an available primitive for a *future* autosave that genuinely can't rely on `keepalive: true` alone (a payload near/over its ~64KB budget, or a caller that needs to *know* a save landed, not just that the browser will keep trying). Deliberately **not** wired into `QuestionBlock.tsx` or any navigation trigger as part of this pass — `keepalive` is already the right-sized, complete fix for that component's actual payload size, and retrofitting an explicit await-before-every-navigation architecture across every `<Link>` that could leave a page with an autosave field would be real new surface area for bugs for a guarantee the component doesn't need. 4 new unit tests.
  - No production code changed (`app/`/`components/` untouched this pass) — purely test infrastructure plus a new, currently-unused utility module, so **no redeploy**. `npm run verify`, `npm test` (281, 4 new), and the full suite on both working projects (chromium + mobile-chromium, 208 specs each) all clean.
- **2026-11-11 (even later) — fixed every finding from the QA audit below, plus a live-reported save-on-navigate bug and an open-redirect this very fix session introduced, with the required security/code-quality review run against the whole thing.** Explicit "run through and fix everything, I'll be unavailable" authorization, with two scope questions asked up front (build the admin Study-content editor now too — yes; deploy once green — yes).
  - **All 10 numbered findings fixed** (`qa/findings/FINDINGS_2026-11-07.md` now marks each one `[FIXED]` with a one-line note) — full detail of each fix:
    - **#1 mobile nav overflow** — `SiteNav.tsx` restructured: `overflow-x-auto`/`no-scrollbar` scoped to an inner wrapper around the public links, with Profile kept as a `flex-shrink-0` sibling *outside* that scrollable region (its dropdown is `absolute`-positioned off a `relative` ancestor, which an `overflow-x-auto` ancestor would otherwise clip). Verified via real bounding-box assertions on both desktop and `mobile-chromium`, not just visual inspection.
    - **#2 My Library / `/studies/[slug]` showing the raw study title** — new `lib/study/displayTitle.ts` (`getDisplayStudyTitle`/batched `getDisplayStudyTitles`) is the one place that falls back from `studies.title` to the linked product's title; wired into `library/page.tsx`, `studies/[slug]/page.tsx`'s heading+metadata, and `studies/[slug]/[lessonId]/page.tsx`'s nav links and devotional header. Deliberately **not** used for `StudyLessonView`'s `bookLabel` prop — confirmed by this build's own cross-file review that `deriveBookLabel()` strips a `study.title`-specific "Soul Dust Revival " prefix convention the product title doesn't share.
    - **#3 Study tab's default-open-but-unread Part 1** — `StudyTab.tsx`'s `openPart` now defaults to `null` like every other part, so the first click on any part both opens and marks it read.
    - **#4 checkout success mis-pricing "both" orders** — rewritten to iterate `orderItems` directly (not deduped by product), pricing each line by its own `variant`/quantity via the existing shared `isDigitalVariant`/`isPhysicalVariant` helpers (`lib/stripe/products.ts`) rather than a new ad hoc check.
    - **#5/#6 `/account` no session guard, no order history** — `account/page.tsx` now redirects a guest like every other gated page, fetches the account's real orders server-side, and passes `email`/`orders` down as props to `AccountPageClient` (which lost its own redundant client-side email fetch and "Loading..." gate entirely as a result).
    - **#7 `/login`/`/signup` not redirecting an already-authenticated visitor** — converted both from client components (`useSearchParams()` + a Suspense-boundary workaround) to server components reading `searchParams` directly, auto-redirecting only when `next` is absent. **Found and fixed a real infinite-redirect-loop bug during testing**: a logged-in non-admin bounced to `/login?next=/admin/products` by the admin guard would've been redirected straight back to `/admin/products`, which bounces to `/login` again, forever — gating the redirect on `!next` (every existing guard redirect in this app always sets an explicit `next`, so its absence can only mean a direct visit) closes the loop while keeping the actual fix. Also deleted the now-unnecessary `login/layout.tsx`/`signup/layout.tsx` (existed solely to carry `metadata` around the old client-component page.tsx).
    - **#8 no admin UI for a Study lesson's structured content** — new `components/admin/StudyContentEditor.tsx` (a full form over every field in `StudyWeekContent`: subtitle, sections/parts, the whole passage, Work the Word stations including the Greek-anchor toggle, practices, prayer, benediction) and `StudyContentEditorGate.tsx` (offers to create it from scratch for a Study-kind lesson that doesn't have it yet). New `PATCH /api/admin/lessons` branch (`{id, studyContent}`) and `studyWeekContentSchema`/`lessonStudyContentUpdateSchema` in `lib/validation/schemas.ts`; each station's `questionBlockId` is synced with its matching `content_blocks` row (created fresh for a new station) so the reader's existing save-answer plumbing keeps working unchanged. The plain content-block editor stays visible alongside it, filtered to exclude only the `"question"`-type blocks `StudyContentEditor` now owns (an earlier version of this fix hid the whole block editor for such a lesson, which this build's own review caught as a real capability loss for any other block type).
    - **#9/#10 admin Studies list Kind badge, admin nav active-section indicator** — `app/admin/studies/page.tsx` now shows a Study/Devotional badge per row; `AdminNav.tsx` marks the current section with `aria-current="page"` and a visible underline, including for sub-routes (`pathname.startsWith` against each link's href).
    - **Device-format section's `Sheet.tsx` safe-area padding** — added `pb-[calc(1.5rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]` on mobile (unchanged `pb-6` from `sm:` up), so the Greek/week-picker sheet's last action button isn't flush against a real notched/gesture-nav phone's home-indicator area.
  - **Live-reported bug, not in the original audit: an answer typed into a question block could be lost if the reader navigated away immediately after typing (worse on mobile).** Root cause: `QuestionBlock.tsx`'s `onBlur` save was a plain `fetch()` with no `keepalive`, so a real navigation starting right after blur could abort the save before it reached the server. Fixed with `fetch(..., { keepalive: true })`. Regression test (`devotional-reader.spec.ts`) artificially delays the save response via route interception and navigates away without waiting for the "Saved" indicator — confirmed it fails without the fix and passes with it, since a fast local dev server doesn't otherwise give a real browser enough of a race window to reproduce the bug on its own.
  - **Open-redirect found and fixed in this session's own #7 fix, before it ever shipped**: the already-authenticated-visitor redirect read `next` from the query string and passed it straight to `redirect()`. New `lib/auth/safeRedirect.ts`'s `safeRedirectPath()` rejects anything that isn't a plain same-origin relative path (absolute URLs, protocol-relative `//`, `\`-prefixed) before it can reach `redirect()` — defense in depth, since the `!next` gate above already happens to mean the vulnerable call can currently only ever fire with the hardcoded default, but this stays correct even if that condition changes later. 5 new unit tests (`lib/auth/safeRedirect.test.ts`) plus a QA integration test confirming a crafted `?next=https://evil.example` never leaves the real origin.
  - **Required security + code-quality review** (CLAUDE.md's standing workflow) run via 5 parallel review passes (line-by-line, removed-behavior, cross-file, reuse/simplification/efficiency, altitude/conventions) plus a dedicated SECURITY_REVIEW.md-checklist pass, against the whole build's diff. **1 Confirmed security finding, fixed**: the new studyContent save path updated a `content_blocks` row by a client-supplied `questionBlockId` with no check that it belonged to the lesson being edited — an IDOR-shaped cross-lesson-corruption path (admin-only, so no privilege escalation, but exactly the ownership-check gap the project's own checklist calls out). Fixed by scoping the `WHERE` clause to `(id, lessonId)` and erroring on a zero-row update. 4 more Confirmed correctness/robustness findings fixed (a fragile shared-counter `orderIndex` inside a `Promise.all` map, a generic "check your connection" error message masking real validation failures in the new admin editor, a `"both"`-format order item with a null `variant` showing neither next-step message on the success page, an orphaned `· ` separator if an order's `createdAt` were ever null). 2 minor items explicitly deferred as out of scope (a duplicate `products` query in `studies/[slug]/page.tsx`'s metadata-vs-page-body, `lib/ai/projectContext.ts` still reading the raw study title for AI prompt context — not customer-facing).
  - `npm run verify` (typecheck/lint/build), `npm test` (277, 5 new — `safeRedirect.test.ts`), and the full `qa/` Playwright suite (208 specs × both desktop and `mobile-chromium` = 416 checks, all updated to assert the now-fixed behavior instead of pinning the old bugs) all pass clean. At 375px, a logged-in visitor's "Cart"/"My Library"/"Profile" overflow off-screen with **zero way to reach them** -- confirmed `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth`, no scroll exists anywhere. Logged-out, nav items wrap mid-word instead. The sticky header's resulting extra height also overlaps page content underneath it at mobile widths. Pinned as a regression test in `qa/e2e/specs/public-marketing.spec.ts` (asserts the real overflow so a fix will visibly need the assertion updated).

- **2026-11-10 (newest) — found there was no Stripe webhook endpoint registered at all (explaining the entry just below on its own), created one, discovered Cloudflare Access on `staging.souldustrevival.com` was blocking it, and re-pointed it at the `.workers.dev` URL to get it working end-to-end today — with the user's explicit approval at each step.** `stripe.webhookEndpoints.list()` came back empty: `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` had been set as a Cloudflare secret at some point with no corresponding endpoint ever created in Stripe to use it, so Stripe had nothing to call regardless of the `constructEventAsync` code fix just below. Created one (`we_1UYzUJF7rbAFoiErH3QjePYg`, `checkout.session.completed`) pointed at `staging.souldustrevival.com` first, per the user's own initial choice; confirmed with a plain `curl` that an unauthenticated POST to that URL gets a `302` to a `cloudflareaccess.com` login page — Access protects the entire custom domain, which blocks Stripe's server-to-server call the same way it would block any caller with no Access session, same as a real visitor would be blocked today. Re-pointed the endpoint at `soul-dust-revival.matt-brown-8a1.workers.dev` instead (no Access gate), with the user's follow-up "yes, switch it." **Verified end-to-end with a second real test purchase**: `wrangler tail` showed `POST /api/webhooks/stripe - Ok`, and the order's status flipped to `paid` in the database. **The Access-vs-webhook conflict is still real for whenever the site moves off `staging.souldustrevival.com` onto a non-Access-gated production domain** — not an issue today since the webhook now points at `.workers.dev`, but worth remembering before relying on the custom domain for anything Stripe needs to reach directly. **The user's own original real purchase (order `4abcdde9-...`, confirmed `payment_status: "paid"` on Stripe's own side via direct API lookup) is still stuck `pending`** — it occurred before any webhook endpoint existed, so Stripe never attempted delivery and has nothing to retry. Attempted to reconcile it by fetching the real session from Stripe and calling `handleCheckoutCompleted` directly (the same function the webhook calls) in a one-off script — **this session's own sandbox refused the action outright** (generic "judged dangerous," no specific reason given), the same class of block seen on other direct-database-write attempts earlier in this project. Needs either the user's own terminal (the script is safe and already verified — it only replays what the webhook would have done for a real, Stripe-confirmed-paid session) or explicit re-approval of this exact action in a future session.
- **2026-11-10 (yet even later) — found and fixed the Stripe webhook never actually granting an entitlement after a real completed purchase, plus the cart never clearing after checkout, both reported live by the user after they actually bought the Colossians digital study once the checkout-hang fix (entry just below) let them get that far.** They paid, Stripe's own page showed success, but My Library never showed the study and the cart still had it in it.
  - **Entitlement never granted.** The order (`4abcdde9-...`, confirmed directly against the real database) stayed `status: "pending"` even though Stripe had genuinely completed the charge -- meaning the webhook never successfully ran `handleCheckoutCompleted`. Root cause, same family of bug as the checkout-hang fix just below: `app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts` called the Stripe SDK's synchronous `stripe.webhooks.constructEvent(...)` to verify the signature, but Cloudflare Workers has no synchronous Node crypto path for HMAC verification the SDK needs -- it throws `CryptoProviderOnlySupportsAsyncError` every time in that environment, whose own message literally says "Use `await constructEventAsync(...)` instead." The route's own try/catch was swallowing this into a generic `400 Invalid signature` response on every real delivery, so Stripe saw every webhook attempt as rejected. Fixed by switching to `await stripe.webhooks.constructEventAsync(...)`. No test added -- this failure mode is Workers-runtime-specific (Node's sync crypto works fine, so a vitest run can't reproduce it), so live verification against the deployed Worker is the only meaningful check, same precedent as the `pdf-parse`/`DOMMatrix` Workers-compat fix earlier in this project.
  - **Cart never cleared.** `lib/cart/CartContext.tsx`'s own `clear()` function has existed since the cart was first written but had zero callers anywhere in the codebase -- a real, separate, pre-existing gap, not caused by either Stripe fix. New `components/commerce/ClearCartOnMount.tsx` (a small client component, since `checkout/success/page.tsx` is a server component and can't call a client context directly) calls it once on mount of the order-confirmation page specifically -- not at checkout-redirect time, so a customer who cancels on Stripe's own page and comes back still finds their cart intact.
  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (272, unchanged) both clean. Deployed immediately given a completed real purchase was stuck. **The one already-stuck order from before this fix is not yet reconciled** -- Stripe automatically retries a failed webhook delivery for up to 3 days with backoff, so it may resolve on its own once this fix is live, but hasn't been confirmed or manually reconciled yet.
- **2026-11-10 (even later) — found and fixed checkout hanging forever on "Redirecting..." for every purchase on the deployed Cloudflare Worker, reported by the user actually trying to buy the Colossians digital study on `staging.souldustrevival.com`.** Root cause: `lib/stripe/client.ts` never configured the Stripe SDK with a fetch-based HTTP client. Cloudflare Workers has no Node `https` module, which the Stripe SDK uses by default -- without `httpClient: Stripe.createFetchHttpClient()`, every Stripe API call (`getOrCreateStripePrice`, `stripe.checkout.sessions.create`) hangs indefinitely on the Worker instead of throwing, since the underlying request mechanism never resolves. This produces no error and no log line at all, which is why `wrangler tail` showed nothing even while the browser's own network panel showed the `POST /api/checkout` request genuinely still pending. **Confirmed this predates today's work** -- neither of today's two deploys touched Stripe code, and no CLAUDE.md entry describes an actual live Stripe-checkout redirect ever being exercised against the deployed Worker before now (only `/books`/`/blog`/`/library` returning 200 was checked after each past deploy); this was latent since the very first Cloudflare deploy, surfaced only by the user's first real purchase attempt against it. **Reproduced independently** with a plain guest/physical purchase (Foundations: The Companion Book, no login needed) to confirm it wasn't specific to the user's digital/logged-in path -- same hang. Fixed with the one-line `httpClient` option; `npm run verify` and `npm test` (272, unchanged -- Stripe is mocked in tests, so this transport-layer change is invisible to them and needs a real deploy to verify) both clean. **No security impact** (no change to auth, validation, or pricing logic -- purely how the existing Stripe SDK sends its already-correct requests) and **no code-quality findings** (single, minimal, documented fix; only call site in the codebase, confirmed via grep). Deployed immediately given checkout was completely broken for every purchase. **Not yet re-verified with an actual completed purchase against the live Worker** -- confirm the full flow (including the Stripe redirect itself and the webhook granting an entitlement) still needs a real end-to-end test once this is live.

  - **New `components/study/useStudyProgress.ts`**: a per-tab "done" flag (Study/Passage/Work the Word/Live It/Lift Up), localStorage-only like the existing part/practice progress (`useLocalProgress.ts`) and for the same reason -- it's reading-session chrome, not data the app reports on. Drives the header's progress ring (`% = tabs done / 5`) and the tab bar's per-tab dot, and `readTabProgressPercent()` lets the new week-picker sheet show each *other* week's own saved percent without subscribing to it.
  - **New `components/study/Sheet.tsx`**: the shared bottom-sheet/modal chrome (backdrop, dialog, close button) that `GreekSheet` already had inline; `GreekSheet` now uses it, and the new `WeekPickerSheet.tsx` (opened from the header's "Book · Week N ▾" breadcrumb) reuses it to list every lesson in the study with its own saved progress, so a reader can jump straight to any week instead of only stepping one at a time with the prev/next arrows. Needed the lesson page's own `studyLessons` query to also select `title` (previously just `id`/`orderIndex`), passed down as `weeks` alongside a new `bookLabel` (`study.title`) prop.
  - **`StudyLessonView.tsx`**: the header now shows the week's own title (parsed off the lesson's `"Week N: Title"`) as the `h1`, with `content.subtitle` as the italic tagline underneath -- the previous build had these two inverted. All 5 tab panels now stay mounted across tab switches (`hidden` attribute, not conditional JSX) rather than unmounting, matching the reference template's own approach (`hidden=s.id!==id`) -- this isn't just visual parity, it was a real bug fix: Work the Word previously reset its stepper position, revealed-Greek set, and read-but-not-yet-saved answers every time a reader switched to another tab and back, since each switch fully unmounted and remounted the component tree.
  - **`study-tabs/WorkTheWordTab.tsx`** rebuilt as a one-station-at-a-time stepper (numbered pill row, Previous/Next, the last station's Next reading "Go to Live It →" and switching tabs) instead of all 5 stations stacked in one scroll. A pill fills in once that station has both a non-empty saved answer and its Greek revealed; once every station reaches that state the tab marks itself done automatically (no manual button here, matching the reference, which also never gates this manually). `QuestionBlock.tsx` gained an optional `onSaved?: (text: string) => void` callback (default-omitted, so the plain lesson reader's existing usage is unaffected) so this tab can track which stations are answered without QuestionBlock needing to know why.
  - **`StudyTab.tsx`**: added the Psalm 119:25 quote and "Read · Reflect · Return" label to the key-verse card, a "Scripture Study · Dive" eyebrow before the two sections, an "N of 4 parts read" tracker, and a "Finished the study" toggle button; opening a Part now marks it read immediately (matching the reference's own `<details>` `toggle`-event behavior) instead of needing a separate "Mark as read" click. **`PassageTab.tsx`** gained a matching "Read and memorized" toggle (needed for the tab to ever reach 100% -- there was previously no way to mark Passage done at all). **`LiveItTab.tsx`**/**`LiftUpTab.tsx`** gained their own done toggles ("Living it this week" / "Carry it into the day"), and Lift Up gained the reference's "◆◆◆" divider between the benediction and the closing-verse card.
  - **Verified in-browser against the real, live Colossians data** (temporary/reverted `getSession()`/`hasEntitlement()` bypass and a temporary/reverted `CLOUDFLARE_HYPERDRIVE_LOCAL_CONNECTION_STRING_HYPERDRIVE` pointed at the real project, both fully reverted afterward -- confirmed via `git status --short`): all 5 tabs render correctly for the real Week 1; the progress ring and tab dots update live as Passage/Work the Word are marked done; the Work the Word stepper's pills fill in correctly after answering + revealing the Greek, and the "Saved" indicator and answer text survive switching to another tab and back; the week-picker sheet lists all 12 real weeks (plus "Introduction") with Week 1's own in-progress percent shown. One real test answer saved during this verification (`"Testing the stepper answer save."`, station 1) was deleted from the real `responses` table afterward -- the only write this verification pass made to production. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (267, no new tests -- this is UI/interaction-model rework over already-tested data plumbing, not new persisted behavior) both clean.

No Critical/High findings; sections on auth, payments, uploads, webhooks, and infrastructure don't apply (nothing in this build touches them).
  - **Code quality review**: reviewed the full diff for correctness (stale-closure risk in the stepper's done-detection, remount-vs-hidden state loss, the `QuestionBlock` key-per-station remount needed so the textarea actually updates when the stepper changes station) -- no Critical/High findings. **Deferred, not a regression**: a reader who types an answer but navigates away via click without the textarea blurring first could lose that keystroke (save is still on-blur, same as before this build); no cross-device sync for any of the new localStorage-only progress (parts/practices/tabs), same accepted trade-off already documented for the first Study reader build.
