- **2026-11-09 (newest yet) — built the interactive Study reader's 5 tabs (Study / Passage / Work the Word / Live It / Lift Up) for the real Colossians study, per the user's explicit choice of "full rebuild" over a lighter first pass.** Resumes the "Study tabs next" work deferred while the cover-PDF/study-import/save-hang production bugs were fixed. The reference prototype's own parser (`soul-dust-interactive/tools/study/parse_study.py`) classifies PDF structure by font size/weight -- metadata our `pdf-parse`-based pipeline doesn't extract -- so rather than port that parser, this reuses the reference project's own already-verified output directly: `soul-dust-interactive/data/colossians-weeks.json` (12 weeks, each already checked against "2 sections, 4 parts, 5 stations, 5 practices, 3 benediction paragraphs" per its own `CLAUDE.md`) was run through `build_study.py`'s own `enrich()` once (embedding the WEB passage text per week and each Greek station's underline-anchor word, splitting the prayer into its Father/Son/Spirit beats) and the enriched result committed as `soul-dust-interactive/data/colossians-weeks-enriched.json` -- a backfill source, not a new parser to maintain.
  - **New `lessons.studyContent` jsonb column** (migration `drizzle/0007_new_colleen_wing.sql`, additive/nullable; see `lib/study/studyContent.ts` for the `StudyWeekContent`/`StudyStation` shape) holds one week's full structured content. A Study-kind lesson without it (the "Introduction" lesson, or any lesson from before this existed) falls back to the existing plain content-block reading view -- no change to that path.
  - **New `scripts/backfill-colossians-study-content.ts`** matches each enriched week to its already-imported lesson by title (`Week ${n}: ${title}`), creates 5 new `question`-type `content_blocks` per week (one per station) so each station's answer reuses the existing `QuestionBlock`/`responses` save-per-user plumbing unchanged, and writes the week's `studyContent`. Idempotent (a lesson that already has `studyContent` is skipped, so re-running never creates duplicate question blocks) -- verified by seeding a Colossians-shaped study into the local test database and running the real, unmodified script against it: 12/12 matched and backfilled, a second run correctly reported 0 backfilled / 12 already-set with no duplicate content_blocks (still exactly 60, not 120).
  - **New `StudyLessonView`** (`components/study/`) renders the 5-tab shell (a prev/next week header, a `role="tablist"` tab bar) and five tab components (`components/study/study-tabs/`): **Study** (key verse with the existing `HideWordsSlider`/`ReadAloudButton`, the week's two intro sections with pull-quote callouts, then its 4 Parts as an accordion marked read via `useLocalProgress`, a small localStorage-backed hook -- this is UI chrome, not data the app reports on, so it doesn't need the server round trip a saved answer or lesson completion does); **Passage** (the full WEB passage verse-by-verse, chapter/verse numbers as superscripts, with each Greek station's anchor word rendered as a real button that opens a **Greek bottom sheet** (`GreekSheet.tsx`) rather than making the reader hunt for it in Work the Word first); **Work the Word** (the 5 stations, each a verse + the existing `QuestionBlock` wired to a real saved answer + an "Open the Greek" disclosure); **Live It** (the 5 practices as a checklist, a guided **5-minute prayer timer** (`PrayerTimer.tsx`) that auto-advances through the lead-in/Father/Son/Spirit beats/close -- evenly spaced, since the book specifies "keep it to five minutes" but not a per-beat duration -- and the week's Bottom Line); **Lift Up** (the benediction and closing verse, and the Quiet Question to Carry on a dark card, display-only like the devotional's "A Question to Sit With" precedent, not a saved-answer field).
  - **Verified end-to-end against real data**, not just typecheck/build: seeded a throwaway Colossians-shaped study into the local test database (migration applied there directly), ran the real backfill script against it, then loaded the actual lesson page in-browser (temporary/reverted `getSession()`/`requireAdmin()` bypass, `DATABASE_URL`/the local Hyperdrive dev connection string temporarily pointed at the test database, both reverted after) and confirmed live: all 5 tabs render the real week-1 content with no console errors; the "Grace" anchor word in the Passage tab opens the real χάρις/charis Greek study in the bottom sheet; "Go to this question" correctly jumps to and scrolls to the matching station in Work the Word; a station's answer saves via `POST /api/responses` (200) and survives a full page reload; the prayer timer counts down and advances through its first beat. All seeded test rows (study, product, lessons, content_blocks, entitlement) deleted afterward -- the real database was never touched by this verification.

  - `npm run verify` and `npm test` (267, unchanged -- no new vitest file added; this codebase has no existing component-testing precedent to extend, so verification here follows the same real-browser-click-through convention already used for `DevotionalDayView`) both clean. **Deferred by explicit scope, not oversight** (documented here so a future session doesn't re-discover these as bugs): a part/practice's read/checked state doesn't sync across devices (localStorage only, same trade-off the reference prototype itself documents as its "biggest functional gap"); the "answer first, then open the Greek" order in Work the Word is a UI convention, not enforced (gating it would mean changing `QuestionBlock`'s save-callback contract for this one caller); the front-matter sections unique to a Study document that aren't part of a week (none apply to Colossians's 12 real weeks) and generalizing the backfill to a second 12-week study (if one is ever added) are both out of scope for this pass.
- **2026-11-09 (yet even later) — fixed cover-PDF upload and study import crashing for real on the deployed Cloudflare Worker, found via the user actually trying both on staging for the first time and `wrangler tail` against the live failures.** Both features had passed every local test this session (direct script runs, vitest, admin-bypass browser checks against `next dev`) but had never once been exercised against the real deployed Worker -- a real gap in this session's own verification rigor. (1) **Cover-PDF upload** (products and studies): `@cf-wasm/photon` ships separate entry points per runtime -- a `workerd` one importing its `.wasm` file directly (which Wrangler's bundler turns into a real build-time WASM module binding, allowed) and a `node` one that inlines the wasm bytes and calls `new WebAssembly.Module(bytes)` at *runtime* (blocked outright by Workers for security -- live log: `CompileError: WebAssembly.Module(): Wasm code generation disallowed by embedder`). `next build`'s own webpack bundling resolves and fully inlines the package using Node's default conditions before OpenNext's later, Workers-aware re-bundling pass (which does request `"workerd"`) ever sees the unresolved import -- same root shape as the `pdf-parse` fix below it in this file: added `"@cf-wasm/photon"` to `next.config.js`'s `serverExternalPackages` too, deferring its resolution to the stage that actually asks for `"workerd"`. (2) **Study import** (any PDF): `pdfjs-dist` (inside `pdf-parse`) tries to polyfill `DOMMatrix`/`ImageData`/`Path2D` from the optional `@napi-rs/canvas` native addon when they're missing -- a native addon can't load at all in Workers, so the polyfill never runs and a bare `DOMMatrix` reference throws before any text extraction happens (live log: `Cannot load "@napi-rs/canvas"... ReferenceError: DOMMatrix is not defined`). New `lib/import/pdfTextExtractionGlobals.ts` supplies a minimal 2D-only `DOMMatrix` (the only affine transform pdfjs-dist's text-layer positioning needs) plus inert `ImageData`/`Path2D` stubs, satisfying pdfjs-dist's own `if (!globalThis.DOMMatrix)` self-check so its real canvas-backed attempt is skipped entirely -- applied unconditionally (not just in Workers) so dev/test/production all take the identical code path. **Verified byte-for-byte identical extraction** against both real source PDFs before and after (Colossians: still 12 weeks + Introduction, same block counts; Psalms: still all 366 days, same formatting) -- this touches the text-position math the whole parser pipeline depends on, so this wasn't just "does it throw," it was "does it still extract the same text." 11 new tests in a first-ever `pdfTextExtractionGlobals.test.ts`. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (266, 11 new) both clean.
- **2026-11-09 (yet later still) — redesigned the devotional reading view to match a new `soul-dust-interactive/` reference project the user dropped into the repo, and found/fixed a real parser regression along the way.** `soul-dust-interactive/` (committed as reference material, same convention as `design-system/`; `dist/` itself left untracked since it's regenerated from `source/`+`tools/` by `build.sh`) is a from-scratch Python/pdfplumber parser plus two working standalone HTML prototypes showing the intended interactive reader: key-verse cards, a memorization slider, read-aloud, a 5-tab study layout, a devotional Today/Year/Journal structure — see its own `CLAUDE.md`/`HANDOFF.md`. Of the two pieces (Study's 5 tabs vs. the devotional's single-day view), the user chose the devotional first, since real users are reading it right now. New `lib/devotional/parseDevotionalHtml.ts` splits a devotional lesson's already-stored HTML back into its printed parts (title, key verse + citation, body, "Lift Up Your Eyes", "A Question to Sit With") entirely at render time — no parser or schema change, no re-import, works immediately for every already-imported day. New `DevotionalDayView` (`components/devotional/`) renders a day-header with previous/next arrows, a key-verse card with a `HideWordsSlider` (progressively blanks words, evenly spaced, for memorization) and a `ReadAloudButton` (device text-to-speech), then "Lift Up Your Eyes" as an ember-accented callout and "A Question to Sit With" as a dark reflection card — on this project's own tokens/fonts, not the prototype's palette. `LessonNavigation` gained a `visible` prop so its "mark in progress on view" ping still fires for a devotional lesson even though `DevotionalDayView` replaces its visible top-of-page markup. **Found a real bug while testing against the live Psalms devotional:** Day 1's citation ("— PSALM 1:3") showed glued onto the body paragraph instead of its own line, because this data was last reprocessed *before* today's earlier heading-detection relaxation landed, so a dash-prefixed, letter-free line was never wrapped as a heading at import time. Reprocessed all 366 lessons again (confirmed 366/366 matched) and made the new parser tolerant of a plain-paragraph citation too, as defense in depth. 4 new tests in a first-ever `parseDevotionalHtml.test.ts`. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (258, 4 new) both clean; verified in-browser (temporary, reverted session bypass) against the real Day 1 lesson, including confirming the hide-words slider actually blanks words via direct DOM interaction. **Deferred by the user's own choice of sequencing, not oversights:** the Study's 5-tab layout, the devotional's Year/Journal views, and wiring "A Question to Sit With" to a real saved answer (the prototype gives it one; this build keeps it display-only, consistent with the devotional preface's "worth answering out loud or in a journal" framing from earlier this session).
- **2026-11-09 (even later still) — fixed the study list/My Library showing stale status right after marking a lesson complete, found live immediately after redeploying the fix below.** The user tried a fresh lesson (Day 3, not one of the duplicate rows the entry below cleaned up) and it still showed "In Progress" back on the list — but a direct database query showed the row was already correctly `completed`. Not a data bug at all: Next.js was serving a stale cached render of the study list / My Library page after the mutation. `/api/progress` and `/api/progress/reset` now call `revalidatePath()` on the study's own lesson list and on `/library` after writing — the documented fix for a Route Handler's mutation needing to invalidate a cached page elsewhere, rather than relying on client-side `router.refresh()` timing (which the entry below's fix didn't address, since it only refreshed the destination lesson page, never the list page the user navigates back to later). `revalidatePath` needs a real Next.js request context vitest doesn't provide, so it's mocked in both route test files the same way any other genuine external dependency is. 1 new test asserting both paths are actually revalidated. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (254, 1 new) both clean, redeployed to Cloudflare.
- **2026-11-09 (later still) — fixed duplicate `lesson_progress` rows racing the "mark complete" button against the page's own "mark in progress" ping, found live immediately after the entry below was deployed.** Reported directly: Day 1 of the Psalms devotional briefly showed "Completed" after clicking "Mark complete & continue," but the study's own lesson list kept showing it as "In Progress" afterward. Root cause: `lesson_progress` had no unique constraint on `(user_id, lesson_id)`, and `/api/progress`'s `POST` did a separate select-then-insert-or-update. The lesson page's "mark in progress" ping (fired once on load, from the top `LessonNavigation` instance) and the "mark complete" write (from the bottom instance's button) are two independent requests with no ordering guarantee — when they landed close together, both could see no existing row and both insert, leaving two rows for the same lesson with no deterministic read order. Confirmed directly against the live database before touching anything: Day 1 had 5 duplicate rows, Day 2 had 3. Fixed with a `UNIQUE(user_id, lesson_id)` constraint plus a single atomic `onConflictDoUpdate` upsert replacing the check-then-act pattern; `LessonNavigation.tsx`'s `postProgress()` now checks `res.ok` instead of only catching a thrown exception (a non-2xx response previously looked identical to success, so the button would optimistically navigate to the next lesson even when the write had actually failed, now shows a visible error instead); the "mark complete" click also explicitly waits for any in-flight "mark in progress" ping first, so the two can never be sent out of order even without relying on the DB constraint alone. **Cleaned up the real duplicate rows already in production first** (required — a unique constraint can't be added over existing violations), with the user's explicit go-ahead: kept the most-advanced status per lesson (`completed` over `in_progress`) for each duplicate set, confirmed before (5 and 3 duplicate rows) and after (0 duplicates, both lessons correctly `completed`) via direct query. 5 new tests in a first-ever `app/api/progress/route.test.ts`, including a concurrency regression test that fires both writes with `Promise.all` and asserts exactly one row results. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (253, 5 new) both clean. Migration applied to the real database (re-approved after the sandbox's "Production Deploy" classifier blocked the first attempt, same as the entry below) and redeployed to Cloudflare the same session.
- **2026-11-09 (even later) — added Devotional-vs-Study as a real distinction (a new `studies.kind` field, a Study-specific PDF parser that extracts saved-answer and word-study blocks), lesson completion with prev/next/table-of-contents navigation and auto-advance, and replaced "Your Studies" with a redesigned "My Library" destination (Continue / Not started / Completed, with Start over). Migration applied to the real database the same day, after being re-approved — see below.** Driven by two real study PDFs the user attached directly (the Psalms devotional, and a 12-week Colossians study) and a request to tell the two apart, give Study documents proper input sections, mark lessons complete with a real plan-progress model, and make "My Library" its own destination people can log straight into.
  - **`studies.kind`** (`"devotional" | "study"`, default `"devotional"`) is now an explicit admin choice on both the Study edit form (`StudyForm.tsx`) and the import form (`ImportStudyForm.tsx`) — it can't be reliably inferred from document content, so it's never guessed.
  - **Study PDF parser** (`lib/import/splitIntoLessons.ts`'s new `splitStudyPlainTextByHeadings`): reverse-engineered directly against the real Colossians PDF's actual extracted text (not just its rendered appearance) to find each week by its own title-page marker (`WEEK N`, tolerant of the roman-numeral-page-ornament merge PDF extraction produces, e.g. `IIWEEK 2`) with the Table of Contents' own two-line entries explicitly excluded first (they match the same pattern and would otherwise double every week and shift every title). Within a week, a `QUESTION` label pulls the following lines into their own `question` content block (a real input, saved per user via the existing `responses` table and `QuestionBlock` component — this schema and save path already existed, just never had a parser that produced `question` blocks from a PDF); a `GOING DEEPER · THE GREEK` label does the same for a `callout` block (the studied word as its title, the definition/paragraph/"LIVE IT" as its body). A reflection-only prompt with no literal `QUESTION` label (the devotional's "A Question To Sit With", the Study's own closing "Quiet Question To Carry") is deliberately left as plain reading text, not turned into a saved-answer field — matching the real documents' own distinction between "answer in writing" and "sit with this." A multi-reference citation list (`COLOSSIANS 1:2; EPHESIANS 2:8; ...`) is recognized by its semicolon and kept inside a callout's body rather than being mistaken for the next section's heading.
  - **Parser hardening found by testing against the real documents, not synthetic ones:** (1) `pdf-parse`'s default `pageJoiner` footer frequency-check was over-firing on legitimate, highly-repetitive *structural* content — a devotional's "Lift Up Your Eyes" callout, present on nearly all 365 pages by design, was being deleted as if it were a running header/footer (a real regression in the PDF-formatting fix two entries below, now fixed: the frequency check only looks at a page's first/last 2 lines, true header/footer position, not the whole page). (2) Added a generic letter-spacing collapser (`collapseLetterSpacing` in `parsePdf.ts`) — small-caps-styled text sometimes extracts as `"G e n e s i s"` / `"S O U L D U S T"` (one space-separated character per letter); consecutive single-char tokens are rejoined into real words (letters and digits merged separately, so `"1 9"` becomes `"19"`, not glued to a neighboring word). **Known, accepted limitation:** a merged multi-word decorative caption (e.g. a pull-quote's "— Soul Dust Revival · Colossians" attribution) can't be split back into its original words once collapsed, since the spacing that marked word boundaries is indistinguishable from letter-spacing once removed — cosmetic only, flagged rather than chased further. (3) A bare page-footer number with nothing else on its line (`"19"`, `"20"`, ...) is now stripped outright — it has no recurring text to pattern-match against, so the frequency-based header/footer stripper couldn't catch it on its own.
  - **Lesson reader:** `QuestionBlock.tsx` now loads and shows the user's previously saved answer (fetched server-side in the lesson page from `responses` by content-block id) instead of always starting blank — the save path worked, but the answer appeared lost on every revisit. New `LessonNavigation` (top and bottom of the lesson page): a "back to table of contents" link and previous/next lesson links computed from the study's own published, ordered lesson list; pings progress to `in_progress` once on first view; a "Mark complete & continue" button that posts `completed` and immediately navigates to the next lesson (or back to the study's contents if it was the last one) — completing a lesson was previously impossible from the reader at all, so every study/devotional's progress bar stayed at 0% regardless of what a paying customer actually read.
  - **My Library** (`app/(study-app)/library/page.tsx`, new): replaces `/studies` as the logged-in destination (nav label "My Library", `/login`/`/signup`/magic-link default redirect, and the order-confirmation email's "access your content" link all point here now) — grouped into Continue / Not started / Completed sections, each item resuming at the first lesson that isn't already completed rather than always lesson one, with a Devotional/Study badge per item. Completed items get a "Start over" action (new `POST /api/progress/reset`) that clears this user's lesson-progress rows for that study back to not-started while deliberately leaving `responses` (saved answers/reflections) untouched — restarting a plan shouldn't erase what was actually written. The old `/studies` index is now a plain redirect to `/library`, so existing bookmarks and `?next=/studies` links still land somewhere real.
  - **Not done / deferred:** the front-matter sections unique to a Study document (the week's reflective essay sub-headings, pull-quotes, "Life Application" numbered items, "Daily Prayer"/"Bottom Line"/"Benediction") are rendered as plain heading-shaped bold lines within ordinary reading text, not as their own distinct content-block types or visual treatments — a reasonable scope line given the two real asks (input sections, Title-vs-header distinction) were about the *input* mechanism and basic heading hierarchy, not a bespoke block type per section style. `.docx` imports are unaffected by `kind` — they still always produce a single text block per lesson regardless of what's selected, since nothing in this build's two sample documents was a `.docx`.
  - 18 new tests (`splitIntoLessons.test.ts`'s new `splitStudyPlainTextByHeadings` describe block, 7 cases; `parsePdf.test.ts`, 3 new regression cases for the header/footer position fix and letter-spacing collapse; a first-ever `app/api/admin/studies/import/route.test.ts`, 4 cases exercising both kinds end-to-end against the real insert path; a first-ever `app/api/progress/reset/route.test.ts`, 4 cases including that a saved response survives a reset). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (248, 18 new) both clean against the local test database.
  - **Verified directly against both of the user's real PDFs** (not just synthetic fixtures) via a throwaway script invoking the real parser: the Colossians study correctly splits into 12 weeks + an Introduction, with every `QUESTION`/`GOING DEEPER` pair in all 12 weeks extracted as real question/callout blocks (spot-checked against the actual extracted text, not assumed from the PDF's rendered appearance); the Psalms devotional still produces 366 correctly-formatted lessons with "Lift Up Your Eyes"/"A Question To Sit With" intact as sub-headings.
  - **Migration `drizzle/0005_many_jack_power.sql` (`studies.kind`, additive/default-`'devotional'`) was applied to the real connected Supabase project the same day, after initially being refused.** This session's sandbox refuses `npm run db:migrate` outright as a "Production Deploy" action, the same recurring block documented in earlier entries above; applied directly to the local test Postgres first (`ALTER TABLE studies ADD COLUMN...` via `psql`) while waiting, then the user explicitly approved re-running `npm run db:migrate` against the real project, which succeeded. Confirmed live afterward: `/admin/studies/[id]` loads with the Kind selector correctly defaulted to "Devotional" for the pre-existing study, and `/studies/[slug]` 307-redirects cleanly instead of 500ing. **Also deployed to Cloudflare the same session** (`npm run cf:deploy`'s documented safe placeholder-build procedure, `--keep-vars`) — this had been missed initially (code was pushed to GitHub but not deployed, so staging kept showing the old build until the user noticed and asked directly), confirmed live via `/library` and `/books` both responding correctly post-deploy.
- **2026-11-09 (later) — fixed PDF import formatting: a stray page-marker line, running headers/footers extracted as content, and every section collapsing into one giant unbroken paragraph; re-processed the already-imported, already-published 366-lesson study in place.** Reported directly against a live, published lesson: one run-on paragraph with a `-- 9 of 373 --` line and the book's own running footer mixed into the text. Two bugs in text extraction, both upstream of lesson-splitting: (1) `pdf-parse`'s `getText()` appends a `"-- page_number of total_number --"` marker after every page by default -- its own page-boundary notation, not real content -- now suppressed with `pageJoiner: ""`. (2) A print-ready PDF repeats a running header/footer as real extracted text on every page; `lib/import/parsePdf.ts` now strips any line that recurs across most pages (frequency-based, nothing hardcoded to this book) after stripping a trailing page number. Separately, `pdf-parse`'s line-based extraction has no blank line between real paragraphs, so `paragraphsToHtml` (`lib/import/splitIntoLessons.ts`) was merging every non-blank line in a section into one paragraph; it now starts a new paragraph after a line ending in sentence-terminal punctuation, and keeps a short, heading-shaped line (e.g. "A Question To Sit With") as its own bolded paragraph rather than folding it into the surrounding prose -- still a heuristic over unstructured text, review after import is still expected. Added `scripts/reprocess-study-lessons.ts`, a one-off maintenance script that re-runs the fixed parser against a study's already-stored `sourceFileUrl` and updates its existing lessons' bodies in place (matched by title) -- run against the real affected study with the user's explicit go-ahead: 366/366 matched and updated, 0 mismatches, no re-upload or re-purchase needed. 9 new tests (2 new cases in `splitIntoLessons.test.ts`; `parsePdf.test.ts` is a first-ever test file for that module, 4 cases). `npm run verify` and `npm test` (229, 9 new) both clean.

Where are the lessons?" against `/studies/[slug]`, which showed "Lessons for this study are coming soon." Root cause: a document import creates the study's lessons as unpublished drafts for human review (`app/api/admin/studies/import/route.ts`, intentional, pre-existing) and the public study page correctly filters to `published = true` — but there was no way to publish more than one lesson at a time, so a 365-lesson devotional import (the one that benefited from the TOC-splitting fix two entries below) was realistically never going to get reviewed and published lesson-by-lesson. Added a `PATCH /api/admin/lessons` branch (`{ studyId, published }`, new `lessonBulkPublishSchema`) that updates every lesson in a study in one query, and a `PublishAllLessonsButton` on the study edit page (confirms before acting, shows the real unpublished count, hidden once there's nothing left to publish). Verified against the real affected study: the button correctly renders "Publish all 366 draft lessons" (365 imported days + the pre-existing "Introduction" lesson) — not clicked, since that's the admin's call to make, not something to do unsupervised against a paying customer's already-live study. 6 new tests in `app/api/admin/lessons/route.test.ts` (first test file for this route): the new branch, admin-only, study isolation (doesn't touch another study's lessons), and that single-lesson POST and the existing bulk-reorder PATCH branch are unaffected. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (223, 6 new) both clean.

  - **Save hang:** `StudyForm`'s `handleSubmit` had the exact same bug just fixed in the import flow — `res.json()` called with no `res.ok` check and no try/catch, so a server crash or bad response left the button on "Saving..." forever with no error. Fixed the same way (and found + fixed the identical pattern in `BlogPostForm` and closed a smaller gap in `ProductForm`, which already checked `res.ok` but not a raw network failure). Grep found this same unguarded `res.json()`-before-`res.ok` shape in 9 more admin files (`AddBlockButton`, `AddUserForm`, `BlockEditor`, `ConnectionsPanel`, `ImportStudyForm`'s own upload path, `LeaderResourceForm`, `NewLeaderResourceButton`, `NewLessonButton`, `UserRoleTable`) — flagged, not fixed, as a follow-up pass.
  - **Cover image:** `StudyForm`'s cover image was a plain direct image upload; switched it to the same facility as Products' Cover PDF uploader (new `app/api/admin/studies/cover-pdf/route.ts`, reusing `splitBookCoverPdf`) — admins already have the full-wrap back+spine+front PDF for a study's linked physical product, if it has one, rather than needing a separate cover graphic. A study has no back-cover concept, so only the front crop is uploaded; the back crop and source PDF are discarded. 5 new tests mirroring the product route's own suite.
  - **PDF lesson splitting:** the heuristic only recognized "Week/Lesson/Session/Chapter/Part N" as headings — a day-by-day devotional ("Day N") matched nothing, collapsing the whole document into one "Introduction" lesson, which is exactly what happened on the user's own real import. Added a first tier that detects a genuine Table of Contents and uses its entries (the author's own section list) as the authoritative lesson boundaries, locating each one again in the body by its leading label; falls back to the (now "Day"-aware) keyword heuristic otherwise. 10 new tests (this file had none before). **Not verified against the user's actual document** — this is a heuristic over unstructured extracted text; re-run the import and review the resulting lessons.

- **2026-11-08 (later) — fixed the real reason the study-document PDF import hung on "Importing..." forever: a server-side crash the client was silently swallowing.** Reported as "I don't know if it's actually working" alongside a progress-bar request — it wasn't working. Root cause: `pdf-parse` (wrapping `pdfjs-dist`) does its own relative-path/dynamic-import resolution to find its worker script, which Turbopack's dev bundling breaks (`Setting up fake worker failed: Cannot find module '.../pdf.worker.mjs'`) — every PDF import crashed server-side with an empty response, and the client's unguarded `res.json()` then threw an unhandled `SyntaxError` that bypassed all error handling, leaving the button disabled forever with `setImporting(false)` never reached. Fixed two things: (1) added `pdf-parse` to `next.config.js`'s `serverExternalPackages`, deferring its resolution to Node's own `require`/`import` at runtime instead of Turbopack's bundler — tried the package's own `/worker` data-URL escape hatch first, but that pulls in `@napi-rs/canvas` (a native addon Turbopack can't place in an ESM chunk at all), breaking the production build; `serverExternalPackages` avoids the whole bundling problem instead. (2) `ImportStudyForm.tsx`'s fetch is now wrapped in try/catch, so any future failure of this shape (network error, server crash, non-JSON response) always surfaces a visible error and resets the button instead of hanging silently. Verified `pdf-parse` itself works correctly in plain Node outside Next's bundler. `npm run verify` (the production build now passes — previously failed outright with a Turbopack ESM-chunk error once the `/worker` approach was tried) and `npm test` (202, unchanged) both clean. **Needs a dev server restart to pick up the `next.config.js` change** — not yet re-tested against a real document end-to-end after that restart.

Switched both to `type="text" inputMode="decimal"` with their own local display-text state, sanitized to digits + one decimal point while typing and normalized to two decimals on blur. Separately, found and fixed the actual cause of "active digital products not showing in Studies' linked products": the query powering that dropdown (`app/admin/studies/new/page.tsx` and `studies/[id]/page.tsx`) only matched `products.format = 'digital'`, silently excluding every `format: "both"` product — even though a "both" product's digital side grants an entitlement exactly like a plain digital one (`lib/stripe/webhook-handlers.ts`'s `isDigitalVariant`). Both pages now also match `format: "both"`. Verified in-browser: typing "12.5" no longer reformats mid-edit and correctly shows "12.50" on blur; the Psalms Devotional ("both") now appears in the Linked product dropdown. `npm run verify` and `npm test` (202, unchanged) both clean.
